# Net Profit

A cosy isometric fishing tycoon for the browser. Tow a net through schools of fish, haul the catch home, build up the boat, build up the island, sail further, find bigger things.

This document hands the project from a chat-built prototype to a real repo. It describes what exists, how it works, what is wrong with it, and what comes next. The prototype is `legacy/net-profit.html` (one file, ~1,300 lines, no dependencies, no build). Treat that file as the behavioural reference until the port reaches parity. `index.html` at the repo root began as a copy of it and is where the port happens; the README covers how to run and test.

## Who this is for

The owner (engineer, final say) and her kid (co-designer, chief playtester, source of the best ideas in this doc). It started as "like Harvest Hero, but not wheat" and turned into something they both want to be real.

Working style the owner expects from a collaborator: real opinions, pushback when something is a bad idea, corrections over comfort. Say "I think this is wrong because…" and then do the work. No performing.

## Design pillars

1. **The sweep is the game.** The net is towed like a trailer, so turning swings it wide. Carving an arc through a school has to feel good before anything else matters. Never trade this away for features.
2. **Short loop, long horizon.** A trip is under a minute: catch, fill, dock, sell, buy. The horizon is the palace, the flagship, the leviathan, the next island.
3. **Base grows the boat, boat grows the world.** (Kid.) Building the island unlocks upgrade levels. Upgrades grow the boat. A bigger boat has a bigger range. Further out there are richer fish and stranger things.
4. **The game grows up with the player.** (Owner.) Each new island introduces a new verb and a visible step up in graphics and mechanical depth. Island 1 is flat shapes and trawling. Later islands earn shaders, weather, crew, line fishing. The game gets a bit meta about its own evolution.
5. **Cosy with teeth.** Threats exist (pirates, sharks, later ninjas) but always have a counter the player can buy, befriend, or outrun. Losing fish stings; nothing is ever lost permanently.
6. **Rare things sparkle.** (Kid.) Dawn and dusk ultra-rares, night-only glowing fish, a leviathan you see long before you can do anything about it. Discovery is a reward in itself; the catch log is a collection.
7. **Phone first.** One thumb, floating joystick, portrait. Everything is tested at 390×780. Desktop (WASD/arrows) is a bonus.

## What exists today

All of this is implemented and smoke-tested in the prototype.

**Core loop**
- Floating joystick (touch or mouse) that points where to go. The keyboard has two feels, chosen by a HUD button that appears where a mouse lives or once a key is pressed, and saved: drive (A and D turn the hull, W is throttle, S brakes; the default) or point (WASD and the arrows aim the boat like the stick, eased over 150 ms). Arrows match WASD in both. Boat has heading, turn rate, acceleration, drag.
- Net follows the stern at a fixed tow length (trailer physics). Catches any fish within `netWidth/2 + 5` of the net centre while the net is moving (> 22 u/s), the hold has room, and the net is not torn.
- Hold fills; HUD bar turns red and nudges when full; gold edge arrow points to the dock.
- Inside any room (tower floors and roofs, the hall and the demon dimension, the drowned temple, Gigantis) the boat's bubbles are put away: the fish order, the hold, the hull, the fishmonger's pick and the driftwood (body.inside). Coins, hearts and the time of day stay, and the message drops down less far over the fight. Everything comes back on the way out. At sea, in a boss fight too, they all stay: there the hull and the hold are what is at stake (2026-10-10, the owner's ask).
- Dock ring: entering it opens the shop and auto-sells one fish every 45 ms, cheapest first. Docking also mends a torn net. The shop covers where the thumb lives, so a drag on its wood steers the boat straight through it and the panel fades to a quarter while you do; buttons and the catch log still take taps (2026-09-17, after a playtester had to reach over the panel to leave).
- Upgrades: net width, hold size, engine speed. Six levels each.
- Ashore (2026-10-06), the kid's third stop: while docked, a Step ashore tab stands up off the shop's plank sign. Tap it and the boat comes round to its berth, bow in a hand's width off the end of the pier, over at most 1.6 s; then a little figure in a yellow sou'wester and a shirt in the hull's paint hops onto the planks and the shop folds away. The same stick walks it at 85 a second, the same pace in every direction, and full pace takes only half a push (added 2026-10-08: the owner found the monkeys too fast, because a thumb resting on a phone pushes about half way and walked at half pace, slower than a monkey; the tests had always pushed the stick all the way); the keys always point while ashore, since driving a person by turning them reads as a tank. Dry land is the island (to 12 short of the waterline, because on the far side the sand is squashed to half its depth on screen and a figure nearer the edge than its shadow looks as if it is wading), the pier but the strip behind the crates, the bridge deck between the rails and the beach. The hut, the palace tree and its ladder, the palms, the watchtower, the stall, the smokehouse, the crates, the umbrella and the toll sign stand in the way, and the figure slides along what it walks into. It steps up onto the pier and the bridge rather than popping, leaves footprints in the sand that fade over 7 s, and carries a lantern at night. The camera comes in 60% closer and follows it, and an edge marker points back to the boat. Back at the end of the pier, after a walk (not straight after landing, where the button would sit under the thumb that lands to start walking), a Back aboard button hops it onto the bow and the shop comes back. Nothing to do ashore yet, on purpose: walking has to feel right on a phone first. Being ashore is not saved; a trip saved ashore comes back aboard at the berth.

**World**
- 4800×4800 world, island at the centre, rendered as a 2:1 isometric diamond with concentric depth rings and a buoy line at the edge.
- The deep (2026-09-28): past the buoys the water runs on for 1,200 on every side, darkening in bands like the depth rings inside it, with a bigger, slower swell on screen and a deeper, lower one in the ambience. Only the flagship crosses the buoys; any other boat that reaches them is told so. Past the last band the deep ends at the far buoys (the far deep, below). A school of mahi-mahi sits past each side of the buoys under a line of floating weed, built from its own seed after the home water so the prototype's schools are unchanged. Crossing outward toasts at most once a minute.
- The far deep (2026-10-08), for the kid's islands 5 to 7: where the deep ended, a second line of buoys stands every 300 round the deep's end, dark red with a black band, and past them the far deep runs on 1,800 more on every side, its water darker again in two bands. Until island 4's Tar Anchorer is beaten an anchor chain sags between the far buoys and the flagship is held at them ("An anchor chain runs between the far buoys, and nothing gets past it", adding that whatever holds the anchor is on the tar island once the tar has spread); beating it sinks the chain, says so, and opens the far deep for good (it reads isle4Stage, so nothing new is saved). Crossing the far buoys outward toasts at most once a minute, and a trip restored out there does not count as crossing. At the far deep's end it said "Nothing out here but water. For now." until the far far buoys went up there (2026-10-16, below). A school of marlin (new, 60 a fish, long and blue) swims at each of its four corners, 5,600 to 5,900 from home, clear of islands 2 to 4 and out of the Cthuluviathan's reach, built from their own seed. A trip saved in the far deep is restored there. Islands 5 to 7 will sit at the far deep's other edges.
- Island 5, the island of bones (2026-10-08), the kid's, "like Jaws on the NES": a reef out in the far deep straight off the deep's top-left side (2,100 past the buoys), where the Skeleton Shark King lives. A bleached sand bar ringed by thirteen pale rocks, white coral round its edge, a giant shark's jaw standing on the bar facing home with its teeth all round, a throne of bones with a shark's skull under it, and a fishing boat wrecked on the rocks on the far side. Its shallows are pale, with ribs and skulls on the bottom, and three bone sharks' fins circle it 300 out. There is nowhere to land; hulls are kept 185 off. A bone-white marker points to it from anywhere past the buoys once the far deep is open, pulsing until it is found; the first time the boat comes within 900 it is announced with the Cthuluviathan's waking sound and logged (isle5Seen), and the far deep's greeting says to follow the white marker until then. The King himself is the next entry.
- The Skeleton Shark King (2026-10-08), the kid's island 5 boss, "like Jaws on the NES": a great shark of bare bones, half again the boat's length, with a gold crown on his skull and a gold trident, fought from the boat with the harpoon. He circles under his reef until a boat comes within 700 of it, then his fin comes up and the two-note warning plays (two low notes a half step apart, faster when he is angry) while he stalks the boat, faster than any boat swims (450). He alternates two attacks. The charge: he stops, the water churns round him, and a dashed red line shows on the water from him to where the boat will be in 0.9 s plus his run, reckoned as it is turning now, so circling does not save it; then he bursts along the line at 600 and bites a boat within 34 of his snout plus the hull (35 of its 100). The counter is to change course off the line; he only charges from within 450. Charge or miss, he breaches at the end of it in a 1.4 s leap: that is when the harpoon reaches him. The trident: he swims out to 220 to 420 from the boat, rises, and raises it for 1.1 s (the harpoon reaches him then too); a gold ring shows where the boat will be for the last half second, and the trident lands 0.7 s later (20). Six harpoon hits, shown as pips (ten until 2026-10-09, when the kid found him too hard); past half he is angry, with a red eye, 0.7 s of churn and less stalking between. Sail 1,400 from the reef, or be eaten (spat out at home as for any boat eaten), and he sinks back, whole. Harpooned down, he rolls over at the surface and dies, dramatically (the kid's): letterbox bars, the camera on him, the markers away, and in a speech bubble "Argh... You have beaten me, little fisher. Me! The King of all the sharks!", "Hear me. I know of a portal... a portal to another world...", "It lies on the island of... of...", "...", then his eye goes out to an X and the narration says he is dead (12 s; a tap skips it). 3,000 coins, his crown left on his throne, a log chip and a guide page; saved as isle5Stage 1, and he stays dead. A harpoon fired while he is up counts when it lands, even if he has gone back under by then: it flies for up to most of a second and he is up for only one or so. Until 2026-10-10 it counted only if he was still up when it landed, which the tests missed by giving the harpoon a quarter of a second's flight; the owner could not beat him, and in the tests a thumb that tapped 0.7 s after he came up won 1 fight in 30, and a quick one took two minutes. In the tests a person who sails round the reef, makes for him whenever he is up, taps the harpoon anywhere from a third of a second to most of a second after he comes up (the harpoon flying its real time), turns off the line a third of a second after the churn and steers out of rings wins every time in about 40 s at almost no cost; one who takes 0.6 s loses about a quarter of the boat (a third before the fix, the fights being longer), and one who takes 0.8 s still wins 29 times in 30 (at ten, 2 in 3); one who never minds the warnings loses most of it and is sometimes eaten.
- Island 6, the big island (2026-10-08), the kid's: out in the far deep off its top-right side, opposite home across island 3's corner. He asked for fifty boats wide (about 3,700, three quarters of the sea inside the buoys); the owner's call on that is still open, and until she makes it the island is radius 650 (about eighteen boats across, three times island 3, several screens of walking), one number in world/isle6.ts. Its shore wanders with the angle round it, so it has bays and points, with pale shallows following them. A long pier runs out from its beach toward home with a dock ring at the end (it sells the hold and opens the shop, the fish flying to a trading post with a blue awning at the pier's root, and the dock camera frames it); step ashore and the figure walks the pier and the whole island. A rocky hill stands in the middle, too steep to climb; an old temple of green stone, three stepped tiers with a tentacle carved over a dark doorway, stands half in the sea on the east side, where there is open water, and the leviathan sleeps off it (the next entry). Thirty-four palms and sixteen boulders are placed once from a seed, and six treasure chests are hidden round it, at least 250 apart: walk up to one and it opens for 300 coins, once (chests6 in the save, a bit each; the log counts them). A green marker points to its dock from past the buoys once the Skeleton Shark King is dead, pulsing until it is found; the first time the boat comes within 900 of its shore it is announced and logged (isle6Seen). Hulls are kept off its shore and its pier.
- The Deep One (2026-10-08), the kid's island 6 leviathan, in two rounds. At the surface, fought from the boat with the harpoon: huge and slow, asleep in the open sea 420 off the temple (east of island 6), it rises when a boat comes within 650 and wades after it at 38, never more than 500 from where it slept; nothing sails through its back. One tentacle grabs: within 230 of its body the water boils for 0.9 s where the boat will be (reckoned as it is turning), then the tentacle bursts up there, and a boat in it is held fast and squeezed (9 a second) until a harpoon in the tentacle makes it let go (the harpoon aims at the tentacle first), or 2.6 s pass; then 5 s before it grabs again. Two tentacles either side shoot: every 2.3 s, in turn, within 520, a driftwood log lobbed at where the boat will be, landing in a ring 0.95 s later (15). Its one great gold eye opens for 2 s every 5 s, and only then does the harpoon hurt it: ten hits, shown as pips. Past half it is angry (the eye every 3.6 s, a log every 1.5 s). Sail 1,300 from its lair, or be sunk, and it sleeps again, whole. Beaten, instead of dying it takes hold of the boat and drags it under (2.4 s): the boat is left tied up at island 6's pier and the figure, in its diving helmet, is in the Deep One's drowned temple below (a green-stoned hall with tentacles carved in its walls), where it waits as Cthulhu (the kid's): an octopus head with a beard of tentacles, folded wings, glowing eyes, ten spear hits. It does not move; every 2.4 s (1.6 when angry) the floor ripples for 0.75 s where the figure is going and a tentacle bursts up there and chases the figure at 112, faster than it walks, one spear each, three at most, bonking for a heart. The warlock and the tarling fight there too. Beaten, it crumbles into the deep and leaves a rare fish worth 1,000 coins (isle6Stage 2). Bonked out below, the figure wakes aboard at the pier; the temple's door on island 6 (Into the temple) is then the way back down (isle6Stage 1), and each stage stays done. In the tests a person at the wheel who circles outside its grab and turns hard over a third of a second after a log's ring shows wins every time in about half a minute at a few health; one who never minds the rings is sunk every time. Below, one with the harpoon throwing as a spear and the warlock wins every time for about a heart; alone with the barbed spear, most times.
- The healer cat and the Cthulhu warrior (2026-10-08), the kid's: the two companions Cthulhu leaves behind when it crumbles, with five hit points each. They come along as the warlock does: aboard they ride the boat (the cat curled on the cabin roof, the warrior at the stern); ashore they follow the figure everywhere, every island and every room, popping over when left 200 behind. In a fight they go for whatever comes within 130 of the figure and hit it up close: the warrior (a little figure with an octopus head and a beard of tentacles, a sword and a round shield) for 2 every 1.4 s, the cat (white, with a pink nose and a red cross on its collar) with a swipe for 1 every 1.1 s. What would hurt the figure hurts them if they are in the way, as for the warlock; at none one is worn out and goes back to the boat to rest until the next landing. The cat heals: a moment (1.2 s) after the figure loses a heart it gives one back, twice a fight, shown as pink crosses after the hearts; aboard at sea, tap the cat on the cabin roof with fish in the hold and it eats one (the commonest) for another heal, up to five, kept for the fights to come (ashore the hold is always sold, since every landing but the tar's is a dock, so feeding is on the boat); tap it otherwise to pet it, hearts rising over it. They are free once isle6Stage is 2, so nothing new is saved; the extra heals are not kept in the save.
- Star, the meteor serpent (2026-10-09), the kid's second island 6 leviathan, from his drawing: a head like a meteor, lumpy rock with a glowing crater and a mouth full of teeth, on a long snake's body, with two long wings and four legs of three claws each. He asked for it to haunt an island, "maybe island 6", and named it Star (the game says "Star, the meteor serpent" on its guide page). It flies slow loops 780 out from island 6's middle, 230 up, its shadow sliding over the water (entities/meteor.ts, drawn in the air layer, the shadow and its ring on the surface). A flagship within 1,500 of the island's middle sets it hunting, with a groan and a toast: it circles over the boat 150 out and 110 up at 430 a second (faster than a flagship, close enough to stay on a phone's screen), and after 2.4 s (1.6 once it is down to half) it aims: its head lights up red-hot and a ring shows on the water where the boat will be when it lands (reckoned as the boat is turning), then it dives head-first into the ring, 1 s of warning (0.75 once angry) and 0.45 s falling. A boat in the ring takes 35, the hardest single hit at sea (the kid's "6 damage, its hardest hitter"); three and it is sunk. After a dive it thrashes in the water for 2.6 s and takes off again, and only then, while it is down or just lifting, does the harpoon reach it: four hits, as he wrote, drive it off for 120 s, and it leaves a meteor shard (1,000 coins the first time, 200 after; meteor in driven). It holds off while the Reaper hunts, and flies off to space while the Deep One is up (below), so no two come at once; it gives up a boat that sails 2,100 off or ties up, and is whole again after. A sighting within 650 (ashore too) is a toast, a chip and a guide page (meteorSeen in the save); the first time it lands near a boat with the harpoon a toast says to harpoon it now. In the tests a person who swerves hard off the ring and harpoons it in the water wins every time, in 12 to 20 s, without a scratch if they turn within 0.7 s; at 0.9 s it costs about a third of the hull, and one who never minds the rings loses 70 and still drives it off, because it lands on them.
- The Reaper (2026-10-11), the kid's leviathan from his fifth list and his drawing: a great blue fish with lighter blue zigzags, a pointed snout with a jagged mouth, a big yellow eye under an angry brow, a tall rounded fin on his back striped yellow and black, a forked tail, and four legs with claws underneath, for he is semi-aquatic. His line: "The Reaper... but he doesn't answer to that." There are two of him, one in the water and one on land (the owner's calls: the one in the water is fought from the boat with the harpoon for the ice fishing gear, the one on land on island 6 on foot for an egg that hatches into a pet; the one on land is the entry after Under the ice). He roams a loop of fourteen points through the deep and the far deep at 150 (entities/reaper.ts; drawn by render/reaper.ts, which will do for the land one and the young one too), past the buoys all the way, at least 200 off every island, about two thirds of it in the far deep, all the way round in under four minutes; in the water only his fin shows, over his dark shape. A boat past the buoys within 650 of him is hunted, with a groan and a toast: he circles it 300 out at 420 (faster than a flagship) for 2.4 s (1.5 once he is down to half), then closes in, and once his head is within 190 a red fan shows on the water across where the boat will be when his tail gets there: from a point beside the boat's way on his side of it, 125 from where the boat will be, out to 230 (his tail's reach), 1.1 radians each side. He surges there under the water (up to 900) and pivots on his head, and after 1.4 s of warning (1.1 angry) his tail sweeps through the fan in 0.45 s, like a scythe, and a boat in the part it has swept takes 25. He swims faster than any boat, so the counter is the King's: turn hard out of the fan, not run on through it. Then he lunges at the boat under the water and rears up out of it 260 off, on his legs, roaring, drawn side on toward the boat's side of the screen, for 1.6 s: the harpoon's chance (a harpoon fired while he is up counts when it lands). Five hits drive him off for 120 s and leave a yellow fin-spine (1,000 coins the first time, 200 after; reaper in driven); the first time, the same toast says he spat out an ice fishing kit, and the log shows it as a chip (driven.reaper above none is the kit, which fishes the ice floe, the next entry). Past half he is angry: shorter warnings, and two sweeps, there and back (the second with 0.9 s of warning), before he rears. He gives up, whole, on a boat that sails 1,600 from where he found it, goes back inside the buoys, or ties up, and then will not hunt again for 25 s. He holds off, and breaks off, while the Skeleton Shark King is fighting, Star or the gulper is hunting, the Deep One is up, the Cthuluviathan is awake, the Heron's hired boats are chasing, the boat is being swallowed, during a dive or a cutscene, or when nobody is aboard; Star holds off while he hunts, so two never come at once. A sighting within 700 is a toast, a chip and a guide page (reaperSeen in the save). In the tests a person who turns hard away from the fan a third of a second after it shows, and harpoons him reared up, wins every time in about 42 s without a scratch; at 0.8 s it costs about 6 of the boat's 100, and one who never minds the fans is sunk every time.
- The ice floe and ice fishing (2026-10-11), the kid's, like the game he plays, on the owner's call of an ice floe in the far deep: a sheet of old sea ice in the far deep's north-west corner (world/icefloe.ts, 240 across with a wandering shore; drawn by render/icefloe.ts with chunks bobbing round it, snowy drifts and cracks, the hole with a red flag by it, and two seals hauled out, one lifting its head now and then). Hulls are kept off it. The first time the boat comes within 900 it is a toast (iceSeen), saying, without the kit, that the Reaper carries one; with the kit and the floe not yet found, a pale marker points to it from past the buoys. Alongside it (within 150 of its shore), all but stopped (under 90), with the kit, the Ice fish button shows (and a boat under way alongside is told to let go of the stick, now and then; one without the kit, that it needs one). The sea waits while the fisher is on the ice, as it does on a dive. Side on (render/icefish.ts): a pale sky with snow drifting, the ice across the top with the hole in its middle, the fisher on a stool with the rod beside it and a red bucket, the line down through the hole, the cold water darkening 1,100 down to the floor. Drop the line and the hook goes down, down, down on its own at 320, taking nothing but steering, to the floor; then it comes up at 170, steered at 240, and every fish it touches is on, up to five (fishing/icefish.ts). Pale jellies drift in the middle water: brush one and the last fish on the line wriggles off and swims away, still under the hole. Near the top the line swings the hook back under the hole. The fish are a day's worth under the hole (icePool, the same all day, a new lot each dawn): five arctic char 90 to 380 down (18), four polar cod 400 to 720 (28), three icefish 740 to 1,010, nearly see-through, with clear blood as the real ones have (50), and three snow crabs walking the floor (60, a crab: steer the hook down onto one and it grabs on as the line turns); species 19 to 22, in the hold, the log, the guide and the aquarium. What is caught stays caught that day; when there are none left the game says to come back tomorrow. The view follows the hook, easing, and with the line up sits the ice low on the screen, under the game's messages; a label at the bottom right says how many are on the line and how many are left today. A drop takes about ten seconds. The hold takes what fits; the rest go back down the hole. iceRuns in the save counts the drops, shown as a chip. In the tests a hand that steers for the nearest fish ahead of the hook lands three or more a drop, more than one that does not steer, and fishes a day's hole out in three to eight drops.
- The igloo (2026-10-11), the owner's addition to the kid's ice fishing ("we absolutely need to build an igloo"), on her call of a base on the floe: the floe's base (FLOE_BASE in world/bases.ts, drawn by render/base.ts), built not from a dock but from the boat, alongside the floe and all but stopped, as for ice fishing. Once the floe has been found, a Build an igloo button shows there over the Ice fish one; it takes 30 driftwood and 1,500 coins, as a hut does, and puts up a dome of snow blocks a little in from the hole, clear of it and of the seals, with a tunnel to a dark door and the player's flag on top, lit at night. It makes the floe a home port: a boat that goes down nearer the floe than home or the other bases comes back up about 200 off its south-east shore, toward home, facing it ("Spat out by your igloo on the ice floe"). Built, the button says Sleep till morning: a night in the igloo, the screen closing and opening on the next dawn, the next day, and a new day's fish under the hole. Not while it is morning already (dawn), so the days cannot be skipped one after another. bases.floe in the save, 0 or 1.
- Neil the seal (2026-10-11), the owner's addition to the kid's ice fishing ("a seal pet named Neil"), on her call of a floe seal fed like the otter (entities/seal.ts). Wild, he lies at the floe's edge south of east, a little round from the hole, dappled grey, lifting his head now and then and turning it to a boat that comes within 600; the first time the boat comes within 420 once the floe has been found, a toast says he looks like a Neil. From the boat alongside him (within 170 of him), a tap tosses him an ice fish from the hold, the commonest of arctic char, polar cod and icefish (no other fish will do, and the toast says so); on the third he is yours, and his name is Neil. Then he slips in off the ice and goes where the boat goes: in the water off its far side from the viewer, level with the cabin (the otter has the near side, off the bow), ducking under between breaths as it goes. When the boat has been stopped 2.5 s he flops up onto the deck at the stern to rest, as seals do on boats, and slips back in once it has been under way 1.5 s; something in the water near the boat (what makes the otter climb aboard) has him up on deck at once and keeps him there. Tap him to pet him. Ice fishing, he sits on the ice to the right of the bucket, and when the line reaches the bottom, two times in five, he slips into the hole after the nearest char, cod or icefish not on the line (never a crab or a jelly), swimming at 230; he takes it in his mouth and brings it up onto the ice, and it goes in the hold ("Neil caught a polar cod for you!"), or back down the hole if the hold is full. One the hook takes first, he gives up on and comes back empty-mouthed; one in his mouth when the fisher leaves the ice is still yours. The water in the hole is now painted fresh each frame, which also cleared the hook's old trail through it. neilFed in the save, 0 to 3; a chip in the log once the floe has been found.
- Under the ice (2026-10-11), the owner's addition to the kid's ice fishing ("an all white scuba set to keep warm"), on her call of a third place to dive under the floe, freezing without the white suit (world/icedive.ts, drawn by render/icedive.ts on the dive's renderer). Alongside the floe, all but stopped, once it has been found and with scuba gear, Dive under the ice shows (over Ice fish, under the igloo's button). Side on, 760 across: the ice is a roof overhead, white on top and blue under, with a hole in it 52 across, the way in and the only way up; light pours down through the hole. Its underside hangs down in two ridges, the left one 240 deep with a cave cut 180 back into it, open on the side nearer the hole, and the right one 200 deep with a tunnel right through; icicles hang in both. Five things are frozen in the ice, each behind its own skin of ice, and swimming up to one chips it out, once, for coins: an explorer's spyglass by the hole (200), a chest in the cave's back wall (800), a blue gem in the left ridge's tip (400), a gold crown in the tunnel's roof (500) and a ship's bell in the right ridge's tip (250), 2,150 in all (iceLoot in the save, a chip in the log). Polar cod, char and icefish swim under it, which a spear takes into the hold; two seals swim loops; Neil, if he is yours, swims along behind the diver; kelp and pale anemones grow on the dark stony floor 400 down. A yellow rope runs from the hole to the diver: swimming up anywhere but the hole is swimming into the ice, so Swim back up, running out of air and getting too cold all have the rope pull the diver straight back to the hole and up through it, whatever ice is in the way. Without the white scuba suit the water is freezing: a Cold row of snowflakes under the air fills in 15 s under the water (a warning at 60%), warming again at the hole, and too cold the rope pulls the diver up. The white scuba suit (6,000 at the shipwright, on the shelf once the floe has been found and there is scuba gear) keeps the diver warm, and the diver is drawn in it, all white. The trench's lurker and the wreck's bone sharks are not there.
- The Reaper on land and his egg (2026-10-12), the second of the kid's Reaper, on the owner's call: on island 6, fought on foot, for an egg that hatches into a pet (the young one is the next piece; this one makes the egg exist). He is the one in the water's shape on four clawed legs, 130 long to the sea one's 240, drawn by the same render/reaper.ts (which learned a lying pose with the legs folded and a shut eye), side on as the kid drew him except while he swings round for a sweep (entities/reaperland.ts). He lies asleep on the sand of a cove on island 6's north-west shore (NEST6 in world/isle6.ts: up and to the left on the screen, the far side of the hill from the pier, more than 250 from every chest so the chest hunt never wakes him), at the water's edge with his tail toward the sea, Zs rising, and in front of him a nest of seaweed with his egg in it, pale blue with his zigzag round it and yellow speckles, sparkling a little. Within 330 of the nest he is sighted, a toast saying there are two of him (reaperLandSeen in the save); within 190 he wakes, gets up roaring and comes for the figure at 140 (it walks at 85), and the hearts show and the camera eases a third of the way toward him, since he is big and the messages sit over the figure's head. He prowls round it at 125 for 2 s (1.3 angry), then, in turn, starting with the charge: the charge, from 90 or further, a crouch with a red line on the sand from his snout through where the figure will be (reckoned as it is walking) and 50 past, for 0.8 s (0.7 angry), then a dash along it at 420 that bites whatever is within 16 of his snout's way; and the tail sweep, the one in the water's: he closes until his head is within 100, a red fan shows from a point 55 from where the figure will be, on his side of it, out to 115, a radian either side, and he plants his head there and pivots, and after 1.25 s (1.05 angry) his tail sweeps through it in 0.4 s. A heart each (Chomp!, Thwump!). Angry (half beaten) he sweeps twice, there and back, the second with 1 s of warning. After either he scuttles to within 85 of the figure and rears up on his hind legs, roaring, for 1.8 s, and only then does a spear get past his scales: the throw button shows only then, and a spear let go while he is up counts when it lands. 30 hits. The helpers go for him only while he is up, and the tarling's tar holds him up there a second longer: tar that cancelled his sweeps and charges, as it does other bosses' wind-ups, would have had the tarling, which everyone this far has, stop him ever rearing at all. His tail and his jaws hurt a helper in the way as they do the figure. Walk 420 from his nest and he goes back to it and lies down, whole; be bonked out or leave the island and he is back there at once. Beaten, he slumps, then walks down to the water and his fin goes off under the sea: 1,000 coins, hearts full, and the egg waits in the nest (reaperEgg 1). Walk up to it and it is yours (reaperEgg 2): it rides aboard in a coil of rope at the bow, on the side away from the necromancer, and wobbles every few seconds. He stays gone, and the nest stays, empty. The log has a chip (Reaper on land seen, then beaten, then Reaper egg), the guide a page of his own after the Reaper's, and the tester panel's extras the egg. In the tests a person who keeps a throw off with the barbed spear and the warlock and steps out of each fan the quickest way, or off each line, a third of a second late (give or take a quarter) wins 30 fights in 30 in about 28 s for about a fifth of a heart; one who just walks straight away from his head does as well; half a second late, 30 in 30 for under a heart; 0.7 s, 25; one who never minds the warnings, none. The harpoon thrown as a spear is no quicker, since his time up is what limits the throws; the long spear wins 27 in 30 in about a minute.
- The baby Reaper (2026-10-12), the young one out of the Reaper on land's egg, and the end of the kid's Reaper (the owner's call: the young one swims beside the boat and walks ashore). The egg came aboard on a day (eggDay in the save); it hatches at the first dawn after (entities/egg.ts), once the figure is aboard, out at sea and not tied up, with nothing covering the boat (the shop, the map, a dive, the ice, being swallowed, the tank) and nothing after it (no shark or leviathan near, no Reaper, Star, Shark King, Deep One, Cthuluviathan, gulper, pirate or hired boat hunting it). A game day is five minutes, and sleeping at a base skips to the morning. 2 s after all that holds, the hatching plays as a cutscene, the owner's (2026-10-12, her words: "Little egg shaking on deck / Crack crack crrrrack / Congratulations! It's a baby reaper!"), 9.6 s on the tar monster's machinery (cine, now with a kind: tar or egg). The bars come in and the camera comes in close on the bow, 2.4 times as near; the boat is not stopped dead as for the tar but coasts, its steering let go. The egg shakes in its coil of rope, harder and faster and swelling as it goes (The Reaper's egg is shaking...), and at 1.6, 2.8 and 4 s it cracks, a jolt and a step further round each time, with Crack!, Crack! and Crrrrack! popping up over it in big comic letters. At 5 s it pops: shell bits and confetti, a tiny roar and a fanfare, and he sits in the bottom half of his shell looking about, while Congratulations! It's a baby Reaper! comes up big in gold (He has his dad's zigzags, and his dad's fin.). At 8 s he hops from the bow into the sea (And over the side he goes!; reaperEgg 3), and the camera goes back out. A tap skips it, but not in its first 1.2 s, so a tap that was steering the boat as it began does not lose it; skipped, he is simply in the sea. Afterwards a toast says what he does. He is a companion like the naga (entities/pals.ts, kind baby), drawn by render/reaper.ts small (54 long ashore, 48 at sea; the land Reaper is 130), side on as his father is drawn, with a big round yellow eye with a shine in it and no angry brow. At sea he never rides the boat: he paddles at the surface (cut off at the waterline, so his back, his fin and his eye show and his legs do not) off the bow on the side away from the viewer, the otter's place mirrored (30 ahead, 18 out, in the hull's units), keeping up at any speed, and every 4.5 s he leaps clear of the water, 18 high for 0.8 s, nose up rising and down falling, with rings where he leaves and lands. Tap him and he leaps for you, and gets a pat. Ashore he trots at the figure's heel on every island and in every room and nips whatever it fights, 1 a bite every second from 24, five hit points; worn out, he swims back to the boat to rest. Hurt by what hurts the other helpers. The log's chip says Baby Reaper, the Reaper on land's guide page tells of him, and the tester panel's extras can give the egg ready to hatch at once. A save from before he could hatch has no eggDay and reads it as 0, so an egg already aboard hatches the first time the figure is aboard. The kid may name him.
- The flotilla (2026-10-13), the kid's. With the whole army the deck was a pile, and four of them had nothing to do at sea: the cat feeds on the cabin roof, the otter's climbing aboard is a warning, Neil flops on deck and goes ice fishing, the warlock sails his own boat, and the naga and the baby Reaper swim; but the Cthulhu warrior, the Forgotten One's bones, the necromancer and the tarling only rode. "Those four get their own boats," the kid said, and named them: an airboat, a little catamaran, a windsurfer and a banana boat (and a swan-shaped paddle boat for two, Caterman's, built later, below). Each is drawn by render/flotilla.ts: the airboat flat and silver, its rider on a high seat before a great caged fan, orange-rimmed, that spins faster under way; the catamaran two slim white hulls with a net between and a red, yellow and teal striped sail, its boom swung out to the side so the sail shows from most of the ways round; the windsurfer a white board and a pink sail with yellow and blue, leaning and swaying, its rider standing at the boom; the banana a fat yellow tube curling up at the ends, brown at the tips, as long as the warlock's boat and no fatter (the owner's), its rider astride. Each bobs and leaves a white V of wake under way. In entities/flotilla.ts each crewed craft keeps a station round the boat, in the hull's units 75 ahead and 52 out either side for the first two and 12 ahead and 88 out for the next two, never behind it where the net is and clear of the otter and the baby off the bows and the naga and Neil alongside; it keeps up at any speed (to 430) and is over at once when 1,200 off (spat out at home). Tied up, with the figure ashore, or with its station up on the land by a shore, it drifts and holds where it is. Ashore the rider steps off and walks with the army and the craft waits; back aboard, each is on its own again, worn out or not. To start, the warrior has the airboat, the necromancer the catamaran, the bones the windsurfer and the tarling the banana, one rider to a craft (data/crew.ts). Who rides which is the kid's: once any of the four is yours, a Boats button shows in the shop, and its card lists the four craft, each with a chip for every rider who is yours and one for Nobody, the one aboard in green. Put a rider on a craft and whoever was on it takes the rider's old one, so the two swap and nobody is left out (assign); Nobody sends the rider back to the deck. The boats are free (the owner's). The crew is saved (crew in the save). And the airboat tows the banana boat (the kid's, the owner's yes): with both out, the banana takes no station but rides on a rope behind the airboat, its nose 22 behind the airboat's stern (15 behind its middle) and its own middle 23 further, in the craft's units, pulled along the rope when it is taut and coasting when slack, always pointing along it, so it swings in behind like a trailer on the turns. With the airboat ashore or unmanned, the banana takes a station and runs on a little outboard on its tail. The deck keeps the cat, and the otter and Neil when they climb aboard.
- Island 6's birds (2026-10-13), the kid's ("he wants island six to have lots of birds"; entities/birds6.ts, drawn by render/birds6.ts). Two parrots perch in the crown of every other palm, scarlet macaws, blue-and-gold ones, green parrots and white cockatoos with a yellow crest, bobbing a little; walk within 70 of a tree and its two burst out squawking and wheel round it for at least 5 s, then settle back once the figure has moved off, the trees round about undisturbed. Little brown sandpipers with white bellies work the wet sand along the waterline in six groups of five, clear of the pier, pecking; within 48 of the figure they run off along the shore, right underfoot (22) they flutter up a moment, and once it has gone they drift back to where they were. Every 22 s or so a skein of seven crosses high over the island in a V. Drawn big enough to read at the zoom a big island is seen at: the parrots with their palms, the sandpipers among the island's solids, the rest in the air. Nothing is saved.
- The tuna cat (2026-10-13), the kid's ("cat can heal companions as long as you fed it tuna"). Tapped aboard with fish in the hold, the healer cat now eats a tuna if there is one (it can smell it), and will eat one for this even when it is full of other fish. Fed a tuna, its heals go to a hurt companion as well as to the figure: a moment (1.2 s) after one of the companions on foot takes a hit that does not wear it out, the cat gives it back, the warlock a heart and the others a hit, with the same pink sparkle and +1 as the figure's heal, from the same heals. The tuna is spent with the last of its heals (it still gets at least two each fight); fed again, it heals them again. A toast says so the first time. Not saved, like its heals.
- Island 10, the place (2026-10-13), the kid's ("a forest a prairie a swamp and a pond"), where the owner put it: the far deep's empty north-east corner (+x, -y; world/isle10.ts, drawn by render/isle10.ts), across from the ice floe in the north-west, centred 58% of the way across the far deep, radius 520 with a wandering shore, clear of the north-east marlin school off its dock. Its pier runs out toward home (+y) like island 6's, with crates and a barrel at its root where the catch flies when the hold sells in its dock ring. Three parts by the way round it, their borders wandering as they go out, and a meadow of prairie in the middle: the prairie toward home and round the pier (the screen's near side and left), golden grass speckled with wildflowers and grass tufts, a few lone oaks and rocks, and the pond, round and still, with lily pads, a pink flower or two and a reedy rim; the forest across the far side (the top of the screen, so its trees hide nothing), forty-odd oaks and pines close together on dark green ground with fallen leaves; and the swamp on the right, olive mud with seven pools of black water ringed with mud and lily pads, reeds everywhere, and cypresses hung with swaying grey moss, kept clear of the pools' edges. The pond and the pools are water: the figure walks round them; trees, rocks and the crates stand in the way. Sighting it (within 900) is announced and logged (isle10Seen, a gold chip in the log); a pale green marker points to its dock from past the buoys once islands 8 and 9 have been found; it is on the sea map once seen. Hearts show ashore, for what is coming. The creatures, the old man's store, the tower and the crows come after, a piece at a time (the roadmap has the kid's list and the owner's answers).
- Island 10's prairie animals (2026-10-13), the kid's ("Prairie has cool frolicking animals"; which animals were left to me): four kinds out in the golden grass, none of them any trouble (entities/prairie10.ts, drawn by render/prairie10.ts, each a size that reads at a big island's zoom). Bunnies, eight, in pairs round four homes, brown, grey or white with pink inside their ears and a white puff of a tail: they hop about within 80 of home (a 0.3 s hop of about 15, nibbling between), and every 7 to 16 s a pair frolics, easing out from where they sat onto a ring 20 across and chasing each other round it for 4 s. The figure within 70 sends them bounding off (0.2 s bounds of 26) until it is past; a tap makes one hop on the spot (Boing!). Prairie dogs, three towns of three burrows, each a mound of earth with a hole: sat up looking about, one in each town standing tall on watch, paws up, in turns of 6 s. The figure within 90 of a burrow and the whole town squeaks (once) and ducks down its holes; they come back up, slowly, peeking, 2.5 s after it is 120 away. A tap on one sends it down a moment (Squeak!). Butterflies, nine, orange, yellow and blue, fluttering in lazy loops over their own patch of flowers. And a little herd of bison, four and a ginger calf, dark and shaggy with a woolly hump, little horns and a beard: the herd drifts 90 at a time at 9, then stops 5 to 10 s to graze, heads down; the calf skips round its mother in a little ring the whole time. Walk within 64 of one and it ambles out of the way; a tap and it snorts (a new low sound). They all keep to the prairie (off the pond, the beach, the crates and the way up from the pier), nothing moves while the figure is more than 1,400 from the middle, and every landing on island 10 puts them back where they live.
- Fishing at island 10's pond, with birdsong (2026-10-13), the kid's ("Can fish at the pond and it's peaceful with bird song"), peaceful on purpose (fishing/pond.ts). Within 30 of the pond's edge, ashore on island 10 and not in a fight, a Cast into the pond button shows (in the lionfish rod's place; Back aboard only shows by the boat). Cast, and a red and white float lands out on the water 30 in from the edge, toward the middle, and bobs on a line from a rod the figure holds out (render/pond10.ts). After 2.2 to 5 s it twitches, with rings and a plip (a nibble, 0.9 s), then goes under in spreading rings with the strike sound: Reel in!, for 1.8 s, the button gold and pulsing. Reeled in, the fish flies up to the figure with its name over it, into the hold, and the first of each kind is a toast; tied up at the pier, the hold sells it at once at the crates. Nothing goes wrong: too soon changes nothing (a toast the first time says to wait for the float to go under), and a bite let go just swims off (It swam off) while the float waits for the next. Walking off brings the line in. Four new species, 23 to 26 (in the table), bite four, three, two and one times in ten: the bluegill, the yellow perch, the largemouth bass and the channel catfish, with guide pages (where: the pond; when: day and night) and places in the aquarium (the catfish near the floor). A full hold lets the fish go. Three songbirds perch on reeds round the pond's edge (entities/pond10.ts): a cardinal, a bluebird and a goldfinch. By day, with the figure within 300 of the pond (fading out by 460), one sings every 2.5 to 6 s: it lifts its head, opens its beak and bobs, a note rises over it, and the ambience plays its song, panned to where it sits: the cardinal's clear whistles sliding down, "cheer cheer cheer"; the bluebird's soft warble; the goldfinch's quick rising "po-ta-to-chip" (audio/ambience.ts songbird, each a little different every time). They are quiet at night.
- The old man's store on island 10 (2026-10-13), the kid's ("a little old man with a beard and cowboy hat in a rocking chair who tells dad jokes"; "snacks that give speed buff (apple) attack buff (carrots) on a time limit and a banana that gives you heals"). The owner was asked whether the snacks are given or sold; with no answer yet they are sold, cheap, carried in a bag and eaten when wanted, as proposed to her (data/snacks.ts; prices and timings are hers to change). His store is a little plank shack with a red tin roof, STORE painted over its door, a window of jars and a porch of boards, on the prairie up and to the left of the pier as the figure comes ashore (world/isle10.ts STORE10; the shack and his chair stand in the way, and the island's oaks, rocks and animals keep clear of it). He sits on the porch in his rocking chair (entities/oldman.ts, drawn by render/oldman.ts), rocking, little, in a red plaid shirt and jeans, with a long white beard and a big brown cowboy hat, and waves when the figure comes within 160. Tap him from within 70 and he tells the next of his eighteen dad jokes, in turn (some about his island: an alligator in a vest, gorillas' nostrils, a pelican's bill, a crow's long-distance caw), rocking harder and chuckling (Heh heh heh!), and his store card opens with the joke at the top: an apple (25: you walk half again as fast for 30 s), a carrot (40: the spear hits twice as hard for 30 s) and a banana (30: two hearts back). Further off, a toast says to walk up to him. Bought snacks go in the bag, nine of each at most; each kind there is shows as a chip by the hearts with its count, and an apple or carrot still working shows its seconds left on its chip, lit. Tap a chip to eat one, ashore only (aboard a toast says to keep it for the next landing); a banana with full hearts is kept for a fight. The card closes if the figure walks off. The bag is saved (snacks); the tester panel's extras can fill it with three of each. The radar upgrade, the badges, the bug spray and the submarine join his shelf later.
- Island 10's gators (2026-10-13), the kid's ("alligators (hostile, 10 hp)"); the bite and the rest were left to me, so they follow the lancers: one heart a bite, and wound up first so it can be dodged (entities/gators.ts, drawn by render/gators.ts). One lies in each of the swamp's four bigger pools (radius 35 and up), low in the black water: a dark shape under it, the ridge of its snout and two yellow eyes above, a ring of ripples, drifting slowly round. The figure within 60 of a pool's edge, or a spear, and its gator surges out with a splash (a new sound) onto the bank toward it, and comes on across the mud at 42 (the figure walks at 85), legs paddling and tail swinging. Within 32 it stops, hisses (a new sound) and opens its jaws wide for 0.8 s, pink inside and lined with teeth: the warning. Then they snap shut, catching anyone within 40 in front of it (a 0.7 rad arc) for a heart (Chomp!), and it is open for 1.1 s before it comes on again. Ten hits beat one: it rolls belly up and fades, and leaves 30 coins. The figure 230 from its pool, or gone aboard, and it gives up, walks back and slides in (still hurt). The spear goes for a gator out of its pool, and one in it only when there is nothing else in range, which rouses it; companions fight the ones that are out, tar holds one still and spoils a bite, and a snap hurts a companion in front of it too. A gator out counts as a fight (so the pond waits). The first rising and the first beaten each get a toast. Every landing on island 10 puts all four back in their pools, whole. Nothing is saved.
- Island 10's swamp creatures (2026-10-14), the kid's ("swamp things: everything but mosquitoes": frogs, snakes, a swamp monster, swamp panthers, tiny bears and tiny deer). Only the gators were said to be hostile, so these are all gentle, like the prairie's animals, which was my call (entities/swamp10.ts, drawn by render/swamp10.ts; the spider comes next with the bug spray). Frogs, nine, round the three small pools (the ones with no gator): one on a lily pad of each (world/isle10.ts padsOf, shared with the drawing) and two on its bank, green, brown, or bright green with red eyes; each puffs its throat out to croak every 6 to 14 s (a soft croak within 170 of the figure). The figure within 45 and it hops into the water (a plip), a ring of ripples; 3 to 5 s later its eyes peek out and it climbs back, once the figure is 70 off. Snakes, three, green banded yellow, brown banded dark, black banded orange, slithering at 13 in a slow wander within 80 of home, S-curving down their length; the figure within 50 and one rears up, its front standing in a curve, tongue flickering, and hisses (the gator's hiss), then slithers off the other way at 38 for 2 s and stays calm 3 s. Swamp panthers, two, long, low and black with green eyes and a tail that swishes and curls: they prowl at 16 within 110 of home, and sit now and then (3 to 6 s) to wash a paw, tail curled round their feet; the figure within 85 and one crouches, ears flat, and slinks off at 60. Tiny bears, three cubs round a den, brown with tan muzzles: they toddle at 11 within 45 of it, sit, and sometimes roll over; the figure within 80 and they sit up facing it and wave. Tiny deer, four, little spotted fawns: they browse with their heads down, then wander at 10 within 60 of home; the figure within 75 and one bounds off (30 a bound, white tail flagged up) until it is 110 away. The swamp monster lies deep in the swamp as a mound of moss with weed hanging off it, its eyes shut (a glint now and then); the figure within 90 and it rises in a second, dripping, into a mossy hulk twice the figure's height with glowing yellow eyes, roars with its arms up and its mouth wide (BLAAARGH!, a new deep sound, a little screen shake, and the first time a toast that it is a friendly one), then waves; 2 s after the figure is 170 off it sinks back into a mound. A tap: a frog ribbits, a snake rears and hisses, a panther sits and yawns (Rrrowr!), a cub rolls over (Grr!), a deer bounds off (Meep!), the monster wakes, or awake roars again; each a new sound but the hiss. They all keep to the swamp, nothing moves while the figure is more than 1,400 from the island's middle, every landing puts them back where they live, and nothing is saved. They can wander near a gator's pool, and a figure following them there wakes the gator.
- Island 10's giant spider and the bug spray (2026-10-14), the kid's ("a spider that can be killed only with bug spray from the prairie's vendor; without it he traps you in his web"); the rest is mine (entities/spider10.ts, drawn by render/spider10.ts). His web is an orb strung upright between the two swamp cypresses 45 to 65 apart furthest from the island's middle (world/isle10.ts WEB10), spokes, a spiral and threads out to the trunks, its hub 24 up; he hangs there, a cartoon spider seen from the front, round and black with a red hourglass, six red eyes and two little fangs, eight jointed legs. The figure within 100 of the web and he skitters down his thread (0.6 s, a new sound) and scuttles after it at 50 (it walks at 85). Within 14 he catches it (a heart, Wrapped!), wraps it in silk and drags it back at 40 to his web, where it hangs, a white cocoon, for 2.5 s while he climbs back to his hub; then it drops free on the near side, and he waits 4.5 s before he will drop again. Walking into the web (within 5 of the line between its trunks) sticks the figure 1.5 s (Stuck!) and wakes him. He gives up 210 from his web. A spear just bounces off him (Tink!, a toast the first time: only bug spray beats him). Bug spray is on the old man's shelf: 50 coins a can of 6 sprays, 30 at most, saved (bugSpray, old saves load with none). With spray in the bag and him within 90, a green spray-can button with the sprays left on it shows over the Throw button; a tap (0.6 s apart) sends a puff of green mist at him (a new sound) and he flashes white and shrieks (a new sound) and flinches back, or, up in his web, comes down. Three sprays and he rolls on his back, legs curled, and is gone: 100 coins, and his web hangs torn. While he has the figure, the stick does nothing and the spray can hides. He counts as a fight while he is down; companions leave him be (nothing of theirs hurts him). Toasts the first time he drops, catches you and is beaten. Every landing he is back at his hub in a whole web. The tester panel's snacks button now adds twelve sprays as well.
- Island 10's gorillas (2026-10-14), the kid's ("the gorillas, which hit hard": his 7 hp and 3 damage, and the owner's word that each slam is wound up so it can be dodged); the rest is mine (entities/gorillas.ts, drawn by render/gorillas.ts). A troop of three sits about deep in the forest (230 to 430 from the island's middle, clear of the trees, within 120 of each other), one of them a silverback with a grey saddle, munching, a hand up to its mouth now and then. The figure within 110 of one, or a spear, and it rises up beating its chest (hollow thumps and a hoot, a new sound), and the troop within 170 of it with it; 0.9 s later they come on through the trees on their knuckles at 46 (the figure walks at 85), sliding round the trunks. Within 30 one rears up with both fists high over its head and roars (a new sound), and a red ring 30 across shows on the ground just in front of it where they will land, redder as they rise: the warning. 1 s later it slams them down there (a whump, the screen shakes, a burst of dust): three hearts to the figure if it is in the ring (Slam!), and a companion in it is hurt too; it is open 1.3 s after. Seven hits beat one: it tumbles over and runs off into the trees, dropping 40 coins. The figure 240 from its patch, or gone aboard, and it walks back and sits down (still hurt). The spear goes for one that is up (the nearer of a gorilla or a gator), and one sitting only when nothing else is in range, which rouses its troop; companions fight them; tar holds one still and brings its fists down on nothing. A gorilla up counts as a fight. Toasts the first time they rise and the first time one is beaten. Every landing they are all back where they sit, whole; nothing is saved.
- Island 10's tower and its giant pelican (2026-10-14), the kid's ("the tower's boss: a giant pelican (15 hp, 3 damage); beaten, it leaves a little bread"); the rest is mine (world/isle10.ts TOWER10 and DOOR10, world/tower.ts ROOF10, entities/pelican.ts, drawn by render/pelican.ts). The tower stands in the meadow at the island's heart, a little toward the forest to keep clear of the rock and the bunnies there (the prairie's animals keep 48 off it): tall and round, 190 high, of sandy stone in courses, with slit windows and a door toward the viewer, and the pelican's nest of sticks on top with the pelican sat in it, its bill over the edge; once it is beaten, the player's flag. Climb the tower takes the figure straight up a long winding stair to the roof, its only room (no floors to fight through: the kid asked only for the pelican at the top; floors can come if he wants a climb): a sunny roof, a bright sky with clouds going by, sandy stone battlements, and the nest at the back. The pelican wakes as the figure comes up (a squawk, a toast the first time), stands flapping on its nest 2.4 s (so the toast can be read), then round and round: it waddles at the figure 1.2 s; rears its head back, pouch swelling, and spits a fan of three fish (0.7 s; each 80 a second, a heart where one hits: Splat!; a companion in the way is hurt and the fish gone); flaps up 70 high (0.7 s) and circles over the figure 1.7 s while a ring on the floor 26 across follows it, dashed and orange, until the last 0.6 s, when it stops and turns solid red (a hiss: the warning); then dives into it bill first (0.35 s): three hearts to the figure if it is in the ring (Gulp!), and a companion in it is hurt too; then its bill sticks in the floor and it kicks for 1.5 s (a toast the first time: spear it now!). Up in the air the spear cannot reach it and it is not a target; on the ground (waddling, spitting, stuck) it is, for the spear and the companions, and tar holds it still and spoils a spit. Fifteen hits beat it: it flops over and is gone, the hearts are filled, 1,500 coins the first time and 500 after, the flag flies on the roof and on the tower outside, and it leaves a little loaf of bread (flying to the figure) if there is none in the bag: for a birdfeeder, to bring the crows (next). The win and the bread are saved (pelicanBeaten, bread; old saves load with neither). Every climb it is back on its nest.
- Island 10's birdfeeder and its crows (2026-10-14), the kid's ("the pelican leaves a little bread, which in a birdfeeder brings crows; each asks a science problem, at a third grader's level, and solved, you can pet it, and the crows become pets, needed for island 11"); the rest is mine (world/isle10.ts FEEDER10, entities/crows.ts, data/science.ts, drawn by render/crows.ts). The feeder stands on its pole on the grass past the old man's porch (to its right on the screen; its pole is in the way, the prairie's animals keep 40 off it): a wooden tray with a little red roof over it on four posts, like his shack's. Empty, a tap on it says crows love bread and hints at the tower (or, once the pelican is beaten, that it leaves a loaf each time). Tapped from within 70 with the pelican's loaf in the bag, the bread is crumbled into it (seeds on the tray and spilled on the grass) and three crows fly in from out over the forest, one after another 0.7 s apart, 110 a second, and settle: one on the front of the tray, the others on the grass round the pole, pecking and hopping about between six spots (never two on one), cawing now and then. Tap a crow from within 90 and it turns to the figure, head cocked, and the crow card opens: a question and three answers (shuffled), big enough for a thumb. Right: the answer turns green, the crow says "That's right!" and a fact, and Pet the crow: hearts over it 1.4 s, then it flies off to the boat and rides on its rail, one of up to three along the far side, bobbing and now and then stretching its wings. Wrong: it turns red, the right one green, the crow says the right answer and the fact, and Another question asks the next; nothing is lost and nothing costs. Closing the card after a right answer keeps the crow solved for the visit: a tap pets it. The questions (27, third-grade science: magnets and forces, life cycles, animals and plants and where they live, weather, water, fossils, the sun) come from a shuffled deck, so none comes round again until all have; each has its right answer, two wrong ones and a short fact, and more can be added to data/science.ts with nothing else to change. Saved: the feeder full and the crows won (feederFull, crows 0 to 3; old saves load with neither). Each landing the crows not yet won are at the feeder; once all three are won it stays full and quiet. The tester's extras have the pelican beaten with its bread. Island 11 is where they earn their keep: let one go in the far far sea and it flies toward land (to come).
- Island 10's treasure finder and the sunken treasure (2026-10-14), the kid's ("a radar upgrade that shows treasure", on the old man's shelf); the rest is mine (world/sunken.ts, entities/sunken.ts, the scope in render/radar.ts). The old man sells the Treasure finder for 2,000 coins; it fits the shipwright's radar (without one it is greyed out and says so). Treasure lies on the sea floor all over the sea: each day 8 sink, 3 in home water, 3 in the deep and 2 in the far deep, 400 apart, out in open water (420 past every island's edge, clear of the tar and the trench), placed from the day so the same day always has the same: a sunken treasure chest (400 coins), an old anchor with a strongbox lashed to it (250), a golden goblet (200) or a pouch of gold coins (150). With the finder, each still down there shows on the radar as a gold diamond, and when none is in range the nearest shows as a gold arrowhead on the scope's rim pointing the way; close by, it shows on the water as a gold glint with a ripple spreading from it and a twinkle now and then. Tow the net over one (its half width and 10 more) and it snags it: Clunk!, the coins fly to the boat, a toast names what it was (the first time, how many are left today). Each is found once a day; which, and the day, are saved (finder, sunkenDay, sunkenFound; old saves load with no finder). Without the finder there is nothing to snag: a few hundred coins is a fortune to a boat filling sardine orders, so the treasure waits for the finder. The tester's extras have the radar and the finder.
- Island 10's badges for the companions (2026-10-14), the kid's ("badges that power companions up with magic, each badge a different power and movable from one companion to another: armour (an energy shield), +3 health, upgradable to +6; damage; healing"); the rest is mine (data/badges.ts, drawn by render/badges.ts). The old man sells three: the shield badge (1,500), its upgrade (2,500), the damage badge (2,000) and the healing badge (2,000), greyed out until there is a companion to wear one. A badge just bought goes on the first friend without one (the warlock, the cat, the warrior, the skeleton, the naga, the necromancer or the baby Reaper: the ones that fight and can be hurt; the tarling cannot be). The shop's Badges card (beside Boats, once a badge is bought) pins each on one friend, or Nobody; a friend wears one badge, so pinning a second takes the first off. The shield: a blue bubble of light round the wearer that takes its next 3 hits (6 upgraded) for it, fading as it is used and flashing when it takes one, full again every landing. Damage: the wearer's hits count double (the warlock's bolts too). Healing: hurt, the wearer gets a hit point back every 4 seconds, even in a fight. Each wearer has a little round pin over its head in its badge's colour: blue with a shield, red with a sword, green with a cross. Saved (badges: shield 0 to 2, damage, heal, and who wears each; old saves load with none, and a badge is never on two friends or on one not bought). The tester's extras have all three, the shield upgraded. The kid's fourth, invisibility ("for stealth missions to come, never perfect, only good for sneaking past enemies up to three levels above you"), waits: the game has no levels yet, and that is the owner's call.
- Island 10's treasure submarine (2026-10-14), the kid's, with the owner's yes ("it dives anywhere in the far deep into the side-on underwater view, with its own fish and treasure"); the rest is mine (world/subsea.ts, the diver's sub mode in entities/diver.ts, drawn by render/sub.ts in the dive view). The old man sells it for 6,000. Stopped (all but) anywhere out in the far deep's open water (200 clear of every island's edge, not alongside the floe), Dive in the submarine goes down into the side-on dive view. The far deep is cut into patches 900 square, each with its own scene from its own seed, so the same patch is always the same place and the next is different: 1,500 across, a rolling sand floor 400 to 1,230 deep with a hill or a hollow across it and rocks half sunk in it; kelp on the shallower rises, fans and strands in the twilight, glowing tubes, anemones and pens in the dark; two shoals of silver fish up top and a bloom of jellies, and, where it is deep enough, lanternfish, glowing squid and dragonfish (the trench's, so a spear takes them into the log the same way); and three treasures on the floor, 200 apart and away from where it goes down (a treasure chest 300, a gold crown 250, a glowing gem 200, a ship's bell or an old spyglass 150), glinting in the dark. The submarine is round and yellow with a little tower and a periscope, three round windows with the figure in the middle one, a propeller spinning at the back (its bubbles come off it) and a claw under the nose. It drives with the stick, heavier than the diver swims (100 top, slower to answer), coasts to a stop and hovers where it is let go instead of drifting up, needs no air (the gauge shows only the depth), and its lamps light further ahead of it, with a beam out of the nose. Drive up to a treasure and the claw takes it; each patch's are found once a day (saved per patch, today's only, at most 120 patches). The spear fires from it as from the diver. Back up to the boat ends the dive. Saved (sub, subFound; old saves load with neither). The tester's extras have it.
- The far far sea and island 11 found (2026-10-16), the kid's fifth list ("a bigger ocean, past a third line of buoys, the far far buoys") and sixth (island 11, "as big as island 6"), and the owner's calls: island 11 goes past island 10 out in the far far sea, and island 10's crows open it ("let one go and it flies toward land, as sailors once let ravens go"). Round the far deep's end a third line of buoys stands every 400 (world/island.ts FARFAR_BUOY_GAP), tall and white with a black band and a lamp on top that blinks, and past them the far far sea runs on 2,400 more on every side (FARFAR), its water darker again in two bands. The flagship is held at the far far buoys until it has a crow from island 10's feeder ("Past them the sea goes on and on, and nobody knows where land is. Sailors used to take a bird along and let it go, because a bird flies for land. Island 10's crows might know the way."); with one, or once island 11 has been found, they let it by (nothing new is saved for it: it reads crows and isle11Seen). Crossing them outward toasts at most once a minute, and a trip restored out there does not count. At the far far sea's end: "Nothing out here but water. For now." Out past the far far buoys with a crow on the rail and island 11 not yet found, a Let a crow go button: the last crow on the rail lifts off, climbs to 80 and flies straight for island 11 at 230 a second (entities/crows.ts GuideCrow, drawn by render/crows.ts), a crow marker following it at the screen's edge; it comes down on island 11's sand where the pier will be and waits there pecking, and when the boat comes within 300 it flies back to its place on the rail (it is not saved: a reload finds it back on the rail). Island 11 (world/isle11.ts, drawn by render/isle11.ts) sits in the far far sea's north-east corner, 55% of the way across it, past island 10 the way out from home, radius 640 with a wandering shore like island 6's: pale sand, then jungle all over, its floor dark green under 64 great broadleaf trees on flared roots, their crowns heaped and hung with swaying vines, 70 ferns and big-leaved plants with a red flower here and there, and 22 palms leaning out over the sand. On the right of the screen, inland, the black lagoon: still black water with a slow green glint across it, in a muddy rim of reeds. On the far side, the ruins: a square plaza of old grey paving gone green with moss, eight broken columns round its edge, and at its back the portal, a ring of carved stone standing on a round step facing the viewer, a skull at its top, dark inside with a faint stir. Sighted from 760 off its shore with a toast ("Land ho: island 11! A big island, all jungle, with black water in it and old grey stones among the trees"), on the sea map as Island 11 once found (its name over it, island 10's being under its own beside it), and the log counts it (isle11Seen in the save). Its pier came the same day (below). The tester's extras give island 10's three crows. The far far sea has no schools of its own yet.
- Island 11 ashore (2026-10-16): its pier runs out 170 from the sand toward home (+y), square to the world like island 10's, with crates and a barrel at its root on the side away from the crow's perch, where the catch flies when the hold sells in its dock ring (world/isle11.ts PIER11, DOCK11; the dock ring is drawn once island 11 has been found). Tied up there, the figure lands at the pier's end and walks the whole island, the pier aside: up the middle from the pier, which the trees keep clear, round the jungle's trees and palms, across the ruins' plaza and round its broken columns, but not into the black lagoon or through the portal's ring (entities/walker.ts ISLE11_DOCK). Landing the first time each visit: "Ashore on island 11. Jungle all round. The black lagoon is off to the right, and the old ruins are up across the far side." Close to the portal, once a landing: "An old portal of carved stone, a skull at its top. It is dark and cold, and nothing you do opens it. Not yet." On the lagoon's rim, once a landing: "The black lagoon. The water is so dark you cannot see the bottom. Far down, something glints... and something moves." A marker at the screen's edge points to its dock from anywhere past the far buoys once it has been found, as island 10's does. The hearts show while ashore there, for the fights to come. Nothing lived there at first: the Heron's brother and Caterman, and the lagoon's key and its creatures, came after (below), and the way through the portal after them.
- The Heron's brother and Caterman (2026-10-16), the kid's ("a heron, not the Heron: the same as the Heron but two hands, and never defeated"; "Caterman, a technology cat-human, 3 hp and 2 damage, on island 11"; and how the fight ends: "bye, it's hard for challengers, have a good day, oh by the way, where is my brother?"). A clearing on island 11's left (world/isle11.ts CLEARING11, 115 across from its middle, the jungle kept off it), trodden pale grass with bare earth and dark scorch marks; at its back, up the screen, Caterman's workshop (WORKSHOP11): a little riveted metal hut with a door toward the clearing, a round window with two green eyes peeking out of it while he is home, a solar panel and a dish on the roof, and three lights along its top blinking in turn. The brother is the Heron's own fight on the ground (entities/heron.ts, its `hands` drawing two grey arms with little dark hands, one on the staff and one round the gun's grip): he waits in the clearing and wakes when the figure comes within 40 of its edge ("A heron! Just like the Heron, but with two hands. 'Have a good day! Oh, by the way, have you seen my brother?' Then up comes his alien gun. Step off the pink line!"), stalks it, keeping his distance, aims his pink line and zaps for two hearts (as in the nest), and makes copies with his staff; 14 to stop him, as the Heron. Walk out of the clearing and he waits for you, whole again. Brought to nothing he does not fall: "Bye!" over him, and "He stops, and lowers his gun. 'Bye! It's hard for challengers. Have a good day! Oh, by the way, where is my brother?' And off he flies." A moment later Caterman comes out of his workshop door and joins you ("Thanks! That heron zapped my workshop all day. I'm Caterman. I build things! Can I come with you?"; catermanFree in the save, so leaving before he is out still has him at the next landing). Caterman (entities/pals.ts, drawn by render/caterman.ts) is a ginger tabby who stands like a person, in a silver-blue suit with a light glowing in its chest, brass goggles up on his forehead, a backpack with an aerial whose tip blinks, his striped tail out the back, and a little blaster: 3 hit points, and his blaster hits for 2 from 60 off, further than any of the others reach, a bright blue beam leaving its tip. He follows the figure ashore everywhere as the others do, wears a badge, and rides the boat at the bow on the warrior's side ("Caterman is worn out. He goes back to the boat to fix his suit."). The brother stays gone; his clearing is empty after. The field guide has him once island 11 is found, and the log says Caterman joined you.
- Caterman's swan paddle boat (2026-10-16), the kid's ("a swan paddle boat for two: two friends pedal it together"; "who rides it? Caterman, a technology cat-human"). The flotilla's fifth craft (data/crew.ts swan), and the only one with two seats: the crew now keeps a rider to each seat, one seat to each craft but the swan's two, swan and swan2, side by side, right and left. Caterman is a fifth rider, on the swan's right seat to start, the left one empty; it is out on the water with either seat taken by a friend who is yours, and the Boats card shows it once Caterman is with you ("Caterman's big white swan, for two. Pedal together!"), its two seats each with a row of chips, so the kid can put a friend beside him (whoever was there swaps, as on the other boats). Drawn by render/flotilla.ts: a white swan's body, a grey-blue well in its back where the two sit, its wings standing up along its sides and curling up at the back (the near one hides the riders' legs), its neck curving up from the bow in an S to a white head with an orange beak, its black knob and an eye, and its tail over a red paddle wheel that turns faster the faster it goes; its parts are drawn far to near, so the neck is in front of the riders coming toward you and behind them going away. They pedal: the riders' legs go round under way. Four stations are still enough for five craft, since with all five out the airboat tows the banana, which takes none; the swan takes the next one free, as the others do. Joining, Caterman says he'll bring his swan paddle boat ("It's for two!"). A save from before the swan reads its seats as Caterman on the right and nobody on the left (a seat missing from a saved crew gets its starting rider, if he is on no other).
- Caterman's drone (2026-10-21), the owner's, after a short of a man fishing with a drone, and a surprise for the kid: "use a drone to fly through the jungle, a cool obstacle course", seen "more Star Fighter" (behind the craft, flying on into the screen, as Star Fox), "gentle", in custom colours and upgradable. Part one is the drone and its flight; bug traps along the course, fishing with it, and its upgrades are the parts to come. Once the Heron's brother has flown, Caterman's workshop door on island 11 has a Caterman's workshop button: his card (#droneCard) sells the drone (3,000, DRONE_PRICE in data/drone.ts) and has nine colours to pick from, before buying or after (DRONE_COLOURS: red, orange, yellow, green, blue, purple, pink, white, black), the drone drawn in the one picked. Fly it! takes over the whole screen (body class `droning` hides the HUD but the coins; the figure waits at the door): a corridor of jungle 12 wide and 5 up to the canopy, the drone seen from 5 behind it, flying on by itself at 14 a second while the thumb, or the keys, steer it left, right, up and down (entities/droneflight.ts, render/droneflight.ts). Its course is 900 long and the same every flight (seed 1101), the first 40 clear; then a gate every 18 to 26: a trunk with a ring beside it, two trunks with a ring in the gap, a branch out from one side (over or under), vines from the canopy over half the way (duck under or go round, a ring on the open side) or a fallen log right across (hop it, a ring over it); 35 gold rings in all, each 10 coins (RING_COINS). Gentle: a bump costs 8 of the battery's 100 and a moment's speed, and the drone carries on past; the battery also runs down 0.9 a second, so a clean flight ends with about 40 left. Ferns, flowers and patches of sun rush past on the floor, and the drone's shadow under it tells how high it is. At the course's end "Through the jungle!", or with the battery flat "Battery flat!" and it turns for home; either way it is put down at the door, its rings paid, its flights and most rings in one saved. Land the drone ends a flight early, rings still paid. Sounds: droneUp, ring, droneHome, and the whump on a bump. Its way out, Land the drone, flying or fishing, is the pill up top like every other way out (2026-10-23), out of the steering thumb's way; it was a button at the bottom left.
- The drone's bug traps (2026-10-22), part two of the owner's drone ("set traps to catch a certain kind of bug"), the third person view confirmed by her.
