Its level (the save's scanner) rises with the kinds scanned (`data/scans.ts` SCANNER_LEVELS, scannerLevel): at 5 it reaches further (1.5 times the spear's reach ashore, 420 from the boat at sea), at 10 it scans the Reaper's egg, at 18 it shows hit points. Each rise says so in yellow under the count on the scan's screen. The egg is the thirty-sixth kind: once the scanner scans eggs, the egg glows blue (a blue haze and a ring pulsing out) in its nest on island 6 and aboard, until it has been scanned, and the scanner aims at it there when nothing else is in reach. From level 4 each scan shows how much fight is left: Hits left (the harpoon's, its resolve) at sea, Hit points (its hp, rounded up) on foot; on the scan that reaches level 4 it only says so. A game loaded with more kinds scanned than its level is brought up to it.
- Honey (2026-10-16), the kid's ("he stops and shakes your boat and runs splashing") with the owner's calls: both the old man's store (3,000 a jar) and a beehive in island 10's forest (a jar a day, the save's hiveDay), a jar used up each time, five in the bag at most (`data/snacks.ts` HONEY, HONEY_MAX; the save's honey). The hive hangs under the forest's oak nearest the island's middle (`world/isle10.ts` HIVE10), at the forest's edge by the tower, 220 or more from the gorillas: walk up and tap it; bees go round it, more while there is a jar to take. An amber honey button (left of the seine's and the spray's place) shows while the Reaper at sea is after the boat, wherever he circles, or a Reaper on foot is awake within 170 (island 6's, the Battle Barge's). A tap: he stops dead at the surface, shaking all over, for 1.6 s while the boat shakes and the water flies (reaper.ts 'shy'), then he is off and away as if harpooned off, splashing, with no prize, and back to roaming after the same while (`fled`). On foot he stops and shakes for 1.4 s, runs into the sea splashing ('run'), and stays away until the figure leaves his ground, when he is back asleep by his nest, whole (reaperland.ts 'shy', 'run', 'away'): no egg for it. On the Battle Barge one run off counts as off the barge. The scanner's Weakness lines say where honey comes from.
- Star is afraid of anything bigger (2026-10-16), the kid's: "both [a bigger boat and a bigger creature] and he stops and goes to space until he [the bigger one] is gone"; the owner's call: only big creatures near him count, and the walkable big ship once there is one, never the boat's upgrades, which would make Star no threat late in the game. Near island 6 the one bigger than him is the Deep One: while it is up (rising, fighting, dragging) Star (`entities/meteor.ts` scaredOf, from main.js) stops dead in the air (0.6 s, "Too big! Off to space!" over him), shoots straight up head first like a meteor (1.4 s, to 1,600 high, off the screen), and stays up there, off the radar, hunting nobody, until it has been gone 2 s; then he falls back like a falling star over his loop (1.3 s) and haunts as before, whole. Once he is up, a toast says so, after the Deep One's own warning. The scanner's Weakness says it. The walkable big ship joins scaredOf when it is built.
- The observatory at your headquarters (2026-10-16), the kid's ("observatories where every leviathan can be spotted"; looked through, or on the map? "both"). It comes with Gigantis once it is yours: a brass telescope on a tripod on a stone step in the courtyard, to the right of the gate (`data/observatory.ts` SCOPE_AT, clear of the way in, the stairs and the portal). Walk up and Look through the telescope: the screen goes dark round a brass-rimmed lens, and in it the real sea, live, wherever the telescope looks (main.js drawScope draws the world round that spot while the figure stays in the courtyard, and the game goes on). It turns to each leviathan in turn, swinging across, eight in all while they are about (the leviathan at the buoys, the Cthuluviathan, the anglerfish, the gulper, the Skeleton Shark King until he is dead, the Reaper, Star, the Deep One, by the scanner's names), and a card under the lens names it and says what it is doing and where ("Roaming, out in the deep.", "Asleep in its sunken city", "Up in space"), with 1 of 8. The arrows, or a tap on the lens, turn it; Done puts it away. Star is looked at up where he flies. And from then on the sea map marks every leviathan, live, with a red eye in a pulsing ring and its short name (a name that would land on another goes over its mark), and its note says so.
- Your bed at your headquarters (2026-10-16), the kid's ("sleeping there", among what the headquarters has): a four-poster with a dark red canopy at the back of Gigantis's first hall, white sheets and the blanket in your boat's paint (main.js HQ_BED). Walk up and Sleep till morning, as in the igloo: the night passes (the day goes on one), and every heart is back. Not at dawn, when there is nothing to sleep through.
- Asked of the kid the same day (a tab, "headquarters for rowan"): the headquarters' upgrades and their costs, what you keep there, which friends live there and what they do, and how the boat gets past the glass bridge to the tar side.
- The Battle Barge, stage 1 (2026-10-16), the kid's ("a place called the Battle Barge, free; customize the size of the map in there; all enemies can be summoned, a maximum of 10"; "everywhere, an option on islands and out at sea"; "1 coin, but mostly to get better, and fun"; "you spawn in, you customize it with any enemies and bosses, and you choose what and how many"), built in stages, the owner's call; this first one has the on-foot enemies, and the bosses come after. Crossed swords on a round button beside the sea map's, at the bottom left (and a Battle Barge entry in the shop, for when the shop is up), show once there is a spear, wherever a fight could start: aboard at sea or ashore, outside a room, not diving, on the ice, in a cutscene or in a fight. They open its card (data/barge.ts): Small, Medium or Large for the arena, then the eight on-foot enemies, each with a word on how it fights and a minus and a plus (monkeys, the Heron's hired men, skeletons, skeleton archers, zombies, ghosts, necromancers and lagoon creatures, the castle's own undead at their own hit points), never more than ten all told ('7 of 10 picked.'; the plus greys out at ten), and Fight!. The setup is kept (barge in the save), three monkeys and two skeletons in a medium arena to start. Fight puts the figure, and the army with it, at the front of the arena (world/tower.ts ARENAS, rooms 25 to 27, 150, 200 and 260 across from their middles), a round wooden barge on the open sea under a wide sky (render/barge.ts): plank deck, a dark hull with foam round it, a rail of posts and rope round its back half, red and yellow pennants on two tall poles, and a sign between them, BATTLE BARGE. The picked enemies stand in rows round the back (arenaSpots: three, then four, then the rest), the undead in the arena's own horde and the monkeys and hired men in its own two camps (none of them come back once beaten). From the boat, the camera goes straight there, and while the figure is on the barge the sea's hunters leave the boat alone (the game's started flag is off to them; the pirate counts it as safe). Beat every one of them and it is won: 1 coin, hearts filled, and 'You beat them all!'; the crossed swords show again on the barge for another fight straight away, picked afresh. Leave the barge (the leave button) goes back where the figure was, aboard or on the very spot ashore; bonked out, the same, hearts filled, nothing lost ('Bonked out! Off the Battle Barge, and back where you were.'). The wins are counted (bargeWins in the save), and the log shows Battle Barge: N won.
- The Battle Barge, stage 2 (2026-10-16): the rest of those on foot. The card has fifteen now, in order island by island: monkeys, the Heron's hired men, gorillas (a slam for 3 hearts, wound up with the red ring, 7 hits), gators (1 a bite, 10 hits), swordsmen (island 3's, 12 hits, quick, dashing, blocking one spear), red, blue and green demons (4 hits each; the blue keeps back and calls up two skull-mask monkeys at a time, the green slashes and calls them too), skeletons, archers, zombies, ghosts, necromancers, lancers (2 hearts a thrust, 10 hits) and lagoon creatures, still ten at most all told. The barge has its own of each, placed afresh for every fight and gone between fights: the gorillas rear up beating their chests as it starts, the gators come straight out across the deck (no pool to surge from), the lancers come roused, and none of them gives up on the figure however far across the large arena it is; the demons' monkeys come out of the arena's own third camp and must be beaten too. Companions fight them and get hurt by them as anywhere else. The card's list now scrolls on its own between the arena and a bar that keeps the count and Fight! (a proper red button now; it had no button look before) in reach on a phone. A setup saved before stage 2 reads back with none of the new ones picked.
- The Battle Barge, stage 3, the first bosses (2026-10-16): those that fight in a room of their own, under a Bosses heading at the bottom of the card: the leviathan sorcerer (island 2's: 8 hits, purple bolts, a swoop to peck, three bolts at a time once angry), the Forgotten One (his throne room's: 36 hits, skulls, the death ray and his sword), the Heron (island 8's: 14 hits, the pink line and his zap, copies of himself from his staff) and the giant pelican (15 hits, spat fish, the dive at the red ring, hit only on the ground), each counting toward the ten like any other. Each is made for the fight at its own spot, so two or more of one boss fight together: two sorcerers fly their ring apart, each pelican has its own nest of sticks on the deck. The sorcerer fights anywhere on the deck (on its roof it gives up when the figure leaves the roof, and waits whole again). Companions fight them and are hurt by them as anywhere else. Beaten, each is down ('Down!'), no prize but the barge's coin. Then Cthulhu (from the drowned temple under island 6: 10 hits, tentacles bursting up through the deck where the figure is going, one spear each; nobody walks through him) and the Old One (50 hits): he rises out of the sea just off the back of the deck, roars, and fights from the water, his great hand slamming down on a ring and his lagoon creatures climbing aboard five at a time (kept on the deck), and they must be beaten too for the fight to be won. Then the Reaper on land (30 hits): his ground is now something he is given (entities/reaperland.ts, a lair: where he lies, what wakes him, how far he follows, keeping him on it, the sea to flee into), island 6's cove by default and the deck on the barge, where he wakes at once, never gives up, keeps to the deck (his tail may swing out over the side in a sweep), shows his red fan and charge line on the planks, is speared only reared up, and beaten goes over the side and away under the water. Then the Tar Anchorer (80 hits): its water is now something it is given too (entities/anchorer.ts, a moat: its middle, the ring it wades in, where it lurks, what wakes it), island 4's tar by default, unchanged, and on the barge the sea round the deck, where it rises off the side, wades round the rail toward the figure (the long way round, never across the deck), throws its anchor at the red ring and swings it round up close, and beaten melts into the sea. Then the giant spider: his web is now something he is given (entities/spider10.ts, a web between two points, island 10's cypresses by default, unchanged), and on the barge it hangs between two wooden posts on the deck, square to the way in; he comes down at once and never gives up, wraps the figure and hangs it in his web as on the island, a spear bounces off him, and only bug spray beats him (three sprays, the spray can over the throw button as on island 10). The card will not add him without the sprays for him in the bag ('You have no bug spray: the old man on island 10 sells it.'). The being stuck, towed and sprayed is now for whichever spiders are where the figure is. That is every boss on foot. Next: the ones at sea, which fight boats, not figures, and want the owner's word on how they come to a barge.
- The Battle Barge, stage 4, the newest on foot (2026-10-19): the kid wants every enemy there, so the card gets four more after the lagoon creatures, before the bosses: the tar island's tar humans (15 hits, a red ring, 3 hearts) and tar beasts (18 hits, a red line, 4 hearts), and Skull Island's men in bone armour (18 hits, a red fan, 2 hearts) and displacer beasts (4 hits, never where they look, 3 hearts). They are the same as on their islands (entities/tarfolk.ts, bonemen.ts, displacers.ts, each now with a place() for the barge's spots and a wake() that has them all up at once), on the deck instead: a tar creatures' lot takes its ground as a rule (the tar island, or on the barge the arena's floor), so a tar beast lunges across the deck, and, with nowhere to run, a losing one turns and fights after half a second. Beaten there they pay nothing but the fight's coin. The spear, the knife, the companions and the scanner treat them as they do on their islands. A setup saved before this has none of them picked.
- The Battle Barge, stage 5, the tar island's bosses (2026-10-19): the tar leader (25 hits; his sword's red fan and his leap's red ring, 4 hearts each; at half he calls two tar humans up onto the deck) and the tar dragon (18 hits, only when it is down after a swoop; its three red rings of tar and its red swoop lane), last under the card's Bosses. entities/tarleader.ts and tardragon.ts now take where they stand and the ground they leap or swoop onto (the tar island's high ground and roost, and the island, by default; on the barge a spot at the back of the deck, the deck's middle for the dragon to circle over, and the deck) and a wake(), so on the barge they come for the figure at once, as the other bosses there do. They fight as on the tar island; beaten there they pay nothing but the fight's coin, and the tar humans the leader calls up have to be beaten too.
- The Battle Barge, stage 6, Skrill (2026-10-21): the kid wants every enemy there, so Skrill joins the card, last under its Bosses ("With his five monkeys. Sweeps his sword through a red fan, drops orb bolts into red rings from up on his wings, and swoops down a red lane: 3 hearts each. Spear him down low. 20 hits."). entities/skrill.ts now takes where he waits (by his throne at the back of his floor by default; on the barge, his spot) and a wake(), so on the barge he laughs at once and comes for the figure, without waiting for it to come up past the front of his floor. His five monkeys come with him, two of them throwers, as at the top of his tower (SKRILL_MONKEYS, SKRILL_THROWERS); they have to be beaten too. He fights as on his tower, his blows catching the figure's friends as they do there; beaten, he crashes down and lies on the deck (there is no portal on the barge, and no Heron's men to haul him off), and lying there counts as beaten. He pays nothing on the barge but the fight's coin, and the save's skrillBeaten is left alone. Old saves' barge picks read with him not picked.
- The Battle Barge on the tar (2026-10-21), the owner's call ("tar version of the ring", asked whether the tar ocean's bosses should fight the flagship at sea or the chemistry boat on tar): the card's Where row has a third chip, On the tar, once the chemistry boat is yours (stealth mission 1), with the tar ocean's own to pick, ten at most all told: the tar eel, the red tar tentacles, and the tar kraken once it is awake (the eel beaten; one picked before then is let go). Fight! (once the chemistry boat has its harpoon, mission 2) takes you from the flagship, wherever it is on the water, out to a ring of the barge's buoys on a sea of tar of its own (data/barge.ts TAR_ARENA, far north-west of everything, where the tar is drawn as on the tar ocean; the sea ring's sizes), in the chemistry boat with its harpoon and its health bar of 100, as on the tar ocean, the sea's order, hold and driftwood put away. The eels come up one after another, six seconds apart, so not all on the same side of the bow at once; the tentacles come up in the boat's way as it sails, every three seconds or so rather than twelve, two at most at once, until as many as were picked have been harpooned to nothing (entities/tartentacles.ts: an `every`, and a `left` count); each kraken is given a home of its own (entities/tarkraken.ts KrakenHome: the tar ocean's under its middle, as it was; the ring's awake at once, never giving up). They fight as on the tar ocean, the tentacles keeping off while a kraken is up. Win: every eel sunk, every tentacle picked harpooned, every kraken down, for the fight's coin and the boat mended; the crossed swords offer another. Pulled under (an eel's coils, or the health bar at none), you are off the barge and back aboard the flagship where it waited, nothing lost; the Leave button is Leave the barge. The story's own eel, tentacles and kraken, and their prizes, are left alone. With a kraken's eye up, the camera now leans partway toward it, on the tar ocean too, as it does toward the eel, so the eye is not off the edge of the screen.
- The Battle Barge's camera (2026-10-16), the owner's ("bc my character is centered on screen I don't get a good view of the fight"): with the figure in the middle, as everywhere ashore, the bosses that tower up at the arena's back (Cthulhu, the Old One) stood under the hearts and snacks. On the barge the camera now frames the arena: its middle, leaning only 0.3 of the way toward the figure, the zoom 0.82 of ashore's for the small arena, 0.7 for the medium and 0.6 for the large (the whole deck across a phone), and the deck 0.05 of the screen lower (`data/barge.ts` BARGE_LEAN, bargeZoom, BARGE_DROP). It eases in over a moment as the fight starts.
- The Battle Barge at sea, stage 1 (2026-10-16), the kid's ("you customize your map of islands and stuff", all the sea bosses, fought from your own boat with the harpoon), built in stages like the barge on foot; the order the owner agreed puts it after the headquarters. The card has a Where row now, On foot or At sea (barge.where in the save, on foot in old saves). At sea it lists three of the sea bosses with a word on how each fights: Star (4 hits), the Skeleton Shark King (6) and the Deep One (10), ten at most all told, one Star to start (barge.sea, the start's in old saves). Fight! needs the harpoon (the card says so and greys Fight! out without it) and the figure aboard, under way, not docked; it takes the boat, and everything that sails with it, out to open water far west of everything (`data/barge.ts` SEA_ARENA, x -30,000), inside a ring of the barge's red and yellow buoys with pennants and a rope of floats between them (render/seaarena.ts), 800, 1,100 or 1,500 across from its middle for the small, medium or large arena, its water lighter than the deep's darkest. The boat comes in at the ring's front heading in, its hull whole; the bosses start round the back, apart (seaSpots: three, then four, then the rest). Each is made for the fight at its own spot, given a home there (entities: the Deep One's DeepHome, the Shark King's Reef, Star's Haunt, each its own island by default and unchanged), where it wakes at once and never gives up; the Shark King keeps inside the ring, and the Deep One, beaten, sinks on its own instead of dragging the boat to its temple. Star is afraid of the Deep One here too: up in space while one is up, back once they are beaten ('He'll be back once it's beaten.'). They fight as at home, with their own blows and sounds, and the harpoon takes them as it does the real ones, with their pips; harpooned down, each is down ('Down!'), no prize but the barge's coin. The sea's own hunters leave the boat alone while it is out there. The ring holds the boat (the range for its size, the storms, the sea map's fog and the far buoys' toasts are off out there; so are the map, radio, seine, pots and dive buttons). The camera frames the fight (below, the owner's). Beat them all: 1 coin, the hull mended, 'You beat them all!', and the crossed swords for another fight straight away; leave (the leave button, 'Leave the Battle Barge') and the boat is back where it was; sunk, the same, the hull whole, the catch and any charter kept ('Sunk! Off the Battle Barge, and back where you were.'). A save meanwhile keeps the boat where it came from. Next: the rest of the sea bosses, then the kid's map of islands, tar and the bridge.
- The Battle Barge at sea, stage 2 (2026-10-16): the Reaper (5 hits) and the gulper (4) on the card's At sea side, after the first three. The Reaper's water is now something he is given (entities/reaper.ts ReaperWater: his loop, what sets him hunting, how far he follows, what keeps him off the islands and in the sea), the deep and the far deep by default, unchanged; at sea each copy roams a little loop round its own spot (seaWater, 260 out), hunts at once, never gives up and keeps inside the ring, sweeps through his red fan and rears up for the harpoon as at home, and driven off is gone. The gulper's too (entities/gulper.ts GulperWater: its stretch, what sets it hunting, how long a hunt lasts, whether it comes back once driven off, what keeps it in), the deep's east side by default, unchanged; at sea it prowls round the ring's middle, hunts at once, never tires of the hunt (at home it gives up after fourteen seconds), is kept in the ring, and driven off goes down into the dark for good ('away'). Honey works on the sea fight's Reapers: the honey button shows while one is after the boat, and one run off counts as beaten, as on the barge on foot. The fight is now checked won every frame, so a Reaper run off with honey can end it.
- The Battle Barge at sea, stage 3 (2026-10-17): the anglerfish (3 hits) and the Cthuluviathan (3) on the card's At sea side. The anglerfish's water is now something it is given (entities/anglerfish.ts AnglerWater: where it lurks, where its light may drift, by night only or not, how long it stays down after a snap, whether it comes back once driven off), the deep by night by default, unchanged; at sea it starts at its spot with its light coming up, lurks by day too anywhere well inside the ring, is up again 4 seconds after a snap, and driven off is gone. Its jaws still swallow a boat that makes fast for the light, which out there is the boat sunk and home. The Cthuluviathan's city is something it is given too (entities/cthuluviathan.ts CthuluCity: where it lies, whether it wakes at once, how long out of reach before it sleeps, the longest it stays awake, whether it comes back), the corner of the deep by default, unchanged; at sea each copy has its own sunken city, ruins and all, round its spot, wakes at once and never goes back to sleep, its tentacles coming up where the boat is going within 1,000 of the city, and driven off it sleeps for good in its city, which stays. A tentacle's grab there takes none of the catch, only the hull. That is every sea boss but the guard leviathan.
- The Battle Barge at sea, stage 4 (2026-10-17): the guard leviathan (3 hits) on the card's At sea side, last, which brings every sea boss to the ring. Its water is now something it is given (entities/leviathan.ts LevWater: the path it crawls and how fast, whether it rises for a flagship crossing the buoys or for any boat under way, how soon it is ready again, whether it comes back fresh once driven off, and how far ahead of a boat it has room to rise), the rounded square round the island by default, unchanged. At sea it crawls a circle 0.6 of the ring out from its middle, starting on its spot's side, as fast along as at the buoys, and rises across the way of any boat under way, whatever its tier, once it is ready: no nearer than at the buoys (380), and only where the middle of its body lies 120 inside the ring, so a boat heading out at the buoys has to turn back in first. It is ready again 5 seconds after it sinks (20 at the buoys). Its hits are kept from rise to rise there, where at the buoys each rise starts fresh, and driven off it sinks and is gone for good. Running into it shoves the boat back as at the buoys and costs the hull (30), never fish. The first rise in a fight says so once ('Turn away from the spines, or harpoon it while it stands.'); the island's other toasts about crossing are not said out there. The camera frames it only once it has risen (crawling round, it is a shadow under the water). Next: the kid's map of islands, the tar and the bridge.
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 1 (2026-10-17): "you make its map". The card's At sea side has a chart of the ring (`main.js` seaChart): the ring from above, turned the way the game is (up on the chart is up on the screen, as on the sea map), its red and yellow buoys, the boat where it comes in at the bottom, the bosses as dark dots where they start, and the kid's islands. Tap the water for an island there, tap an island to take it away (`data/barge.ts` seaIsleTap). Up to 5, each 0.15 of the ring across from its middle (120, 165 or 225 by the ring's size), its middle no further out than 0.7 so there is a way round between its sand and the buoys, none within 0.38 of where the boat comes in, and water between any two; where one cannot go, the card says why (seaIsleNo: 'that is where your boat comes in', 'leave a way round', 'leave water between them', 'that is 5 islands, the most'). The map is saved with the barge setup (barge.isles, each where it is as a share of the ring's radius, so one map fits every size; a save from before has open water, and islands that could not be where they are read back are dropped). In the fight the islands stand in the ring (render/seaisles.ts: shallows, sand, grass, two or three palms in island 7's style), and block the boat, the friends' boats and the bosses: one that comes up onto an island's sand is put back in the water at its edge, where it slides round (`main.js` seaShore: its middle, where it is making for, the gulper's whole body, each Cthuluviathan tentacle); Star flies over them, and the guard leviathan goes under them and rises only clear of them. The bosses' spots are kept 110 off any island's sand (seaSpotsClear), so the Deep One's body clears it. The sand is drawn over what swims under the water, so a shadow passing under an island is hidden. Next on the map: islands hiding the boat from the bosses, going ashore on them (Stars and Reapers there), then the tar and the bridge.
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 2 (2026-10-17): the islands hide the boat ("islands block the bosses and hide you"). The bosses that chase the boat round the ring (the Shark King, the Reaper, the gulper and the anglerfish) cannot see it with one of the islands in the way (`data/barge.ts` seaBlocked: the line between them passes within 0.75 of an island's reach from its middle; once hidden, the boat stays hidden until the line is clear by 0.9, so it does not flicker at an island's edge). Hidden, a boss is shown the boat where it last saw it, still on the water (`main.js` seaSight), and makes for that; '?' goes up over it when it loses the boat and '!' when it finds it again, and the first time in a fight the game says so ('Hidden behind the island! The bosses that chase you can't see you there.'). A blow of one of theirs (a bite, the trident, the Reaper's sweep, the anglerfish's jaws) lands only on a boat it can see. Star, up in the sky, sees over the islands; the leviathan, the Deep One and the Cthuluviathan do not chase the boat and feel it through the water, so they are not fooled. Next on the map: going ashore on the islands (Stars and Reapers there), then the tar and the bridge.
- The kid's answers (2026-10-17), in his "headquarters for rowan" tab, the owner having put the map's questions there too. His headquarters: upgrades, "yes" (a dock, a forge, a bigger tower, a garden: all of them); what he keeps there, "all" (trophies, treasure, spare weapons and armour, fish); his friends there, "i meant you chose companions there" (the headquarters is where you pick which companions come with you); to the tar side past the glass bridge, "a tar boat not made of tar tho". The map at sea: going ashore, "you dont lose sea boses but ya" (walk the island with the spear and fight what is on it toward the win, but the sea bosses do not lose you for being ashore); who is on which island, "ok" (tap an island on the card to cycle it between empty, a Reaper and a Star); tar, "ok" (slow patches the boat can push through, the bosses cannot go in); the bridge, "bosess cant get under but you dont go under it" (it walls the water for the bosses, and the boat does not sail under it either: read as a bridge you walk across, between islands, to be checked with him when it is built).
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 3 (2026-10-17): tar, his "ok" to slow patches the boat can push through and the bosses cannot go in. The card's map has two chips over the chart, Island and Tar, for what a tap puts down; a tap on an island or a patch of tar takes it away whichever is in hand. Up to 5 patches, each 0.13 of the ring across from its middle; one may go nearer the buoys than an island (its middle out to 0.8), since the boat can push through it, but not within 0.3 of where the boat comes in, nor on an island or another patch, and an island keeps off the tar the same way (`data/barge.ts` seaTarNo, seaTarTap; the card says why where one cannot go). Saved with the map (barge.tar; a save from before has none, and a patch that lies on an island is dropped when it is read back). In the fight each patch is an oily black pool on the water with a wobbling edge, a sheen sliding round and bubbles popping, in island 4's tar's colours (render/seaisles.ts drawSeaTar), drawn under the islands and over what swims beneath. The boat crawls in it, at 0.35 of its top speed (SEA_TAR_SLOW), easing down as it goes in; the friends' boats go through. The bosses go in neither the islands nor the tar (`main.js` seaShore, the spots kept 110 off both, the leviathan rising clear of both); the tar hides nothing. Next on the map: going ashore on the islands, with Reapers and Stars on them, then the bridge.
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 4 (2026-10-17): bridges, the owner's ("do the bridge next and it is a dock on it"; asked where, she picked a short span anywhere) on the kid's "bosses can't get under but you don't go under it". A third chip over the card's chart, Bridge: a tap puts a short span there, up to 3, each 0.2 of the ring each way along from its middle, all lying across the screen (data/barge.ts BRIDGE_U, along x - y) so each is always seen side on, its dock on the side toward the viewer (BRIDGE_N); on the chart a pale bar with the dock below it. Each keeps 0.14 clear all round, for itself and its dock, of the islands, the tar and the other bridges (and they of it), its ends within 0.82 of the middle, its dock's end within 0.9, and 0.3 clear of where the boat comes in (seaBridgeNo, seaBridgeTap; the card says why where one cannot go). Saved with the map (barge.bridges; a save from before has none, and one that would lie on an island or the tar is dropped). In the fight (render/seaisles.ts seaBridgeSolids, drawSeaBridgesWater) each is a pale stone span on piers with arches between, its deck 26 over the water with glass rails along both sides and a lamp at each end glowing like the orb, and on its near side a wooden dock: four steps down from the deck to planks 6 over the water, with posts at its end; its shadow and the foam at its piers on the water. It walls the water (bridgeWalls: the deck end to end, and the dock out to near its end, as capsules): the boat, the friends' boats and the bosses go round it, and the bosses' spots are kept 110 off it all along. Stop at the dock's end and 'Onto the bridge' (the climb button) ties the boat up there, bow in, and the figure steps off onto the planks ('On the bridge! The bosses can't get up here, but they are still after your boat.'); the deck, the steps and the dock are land while the fight is on (entities/walker.ts setWalkLand, cleared when it ends), the camera follows the figure as ashore, and 'Back aboard' at the dock's end takes it back. The bosses keep after the tied-up boat, as the kid said ('you don't lose sea bosses'). Leaving the fight, sunk or won into another, from the bridge brings the figure back aboard. Next on the map: going ashore on the islands, with Reapers and Stars on them. The owner's "plus in the white" is to be checked: read as a bridge at the white buoys too, the glass bridge of the kid's list.
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 5 (2026-10-17): ashore on the islands, with a Reaper on one or more ("Stars and Reapers on them"; going ashore, "you dont lose sea boses but ya"; tap an island to cycle who is on it, "ok"). On the card's chart a tap on an island now puts a Reaper on it (drawn lying on its grass: blue, with his striped fin and yellow eye), and a tap on that island takes it away (`data/barge.ts` seaIsleTap; SeaIsle.who, saved with the map, anyone else read back as nobody). A Reaper on an island is a fight on its own: the card counts them ('1 of 10 picked, and a Reaper on an island.', or 'Just a Reaper on an island.') and Fight! goes with only them. In the ring, stop up against any island's sand (within 40 of it, under 45 a second) and 'Step ashore' (the climb button) ties the boat up there, bow in, and the figure lands just up the beach (seaIsleWay): straight in from the bow if that is in front of the boat on the screen, or else 0.5 of a radian round the shore, on the side in front of it or the most to one side of it, since the boat, coming in from below as it does, stood bow on between the figure and the camera and hid it. The sand is land while the fight is on, round the palms (to 12 short of the water, as at home), the hearts show, and 'Back aboard' where it landed takes it back. Each island's Reaper is the land one, island 6's (entities/reaperland.ts, the Battle Barge's deckLair, which keeps him off a deck's palms now): asleep across the island's middle facing the ring's, up the moment the figure comes ashore, never giving up, and fought as on island 6, his fan, his charge, then up on his hind legs for the spear (30 hits; the harpoon throws as a spear); the camera leans toward him, the helpers fight him, the honey runs him off (off his island for good, out of the fight), and the scanner reads him. Back aboard, bonked out or not, he lies down again whole. Beaten, he goes off into the sea ('Down!'), and the fight is won once he and every sea boss are down. The sea bosses keep after the tied-up boat the whole time, so beating them first, then going ashore, is the safer way. Stars on the islands are next: they fly, and need a way to fight from the sand.
- Fix (2026-10-17): Star in the Battle Barge at sea counted as beaten the moment he first dived. The fight took a boss in the state 'down' as sunk and out of sight (the Deep One's 'down'), but Star's 'down' is him thrashing in the water after a dive, the harpoon's chance; so after one dive he froze out of sight, never harpooned, and with him the last boss up the fight was won (`main.js` seaSunk: only a 'down' that is not Star's). From stage 1 (#221), where Star is the one picked to start; found building Stars on the islands.
- The Battle Barge at sea, the kid's map, stage 6 (2026-10-17): Stars on the islands, the other half of "Stars and Reapers on them". A tap on an island on the card's chart now goes nobody, a Reaper, Star, then takes the island away (`data/barge.ts` seaIsleTap; SeaIsle.who 'star', saved with the map); on the chart he is his rocky head with its glowing crater and a wing either side, over the island. The card counts who is on the islands ('Just a Reaper and Star on your islands.'). In the ring he loops high over his island (entities/meteor.ts isleHaunt: at its edge, 230 up), and leaves the boat alone; the moment the figure comes ashore he hunts it as he hunts a boat (isleStar: Star's prey, the figure on his island, reckoned straight on, since a person turns on a sixpence): he circles over it, his head lights up over a ring where it will be, always on the sand (its middle no further out than 12 short of the water), and he dives into it. A dive on the figure is his hardest hit ashore, 2 hearts ('Crash!'); then he thrashes on the sand for 2.6 s, a puff of sand round him, and only then does the spear reach him (the throw button shows; the helpers go for him too): his four harpoon hits' worth, the spear taking its power off them. Back aboard, he is up on his loop again, whole. Driven off, he flies away, out of the fight ('Down!'), and the fight is won once every one on the islands and every sea boss is down. Afraid of the Deep One here too, as the sea's Star is. The scanner reads him as Star. In the tests the boat's Star is unchanged: his prey is the boat by default, none while it is tied up, as before.
- The abyss, part one (2026-10-17): the kid's 1,000-metre dive at the farthest far buoys ("a dive that goes 1,000 metres down, small on top"; the farthest far buoys being the white ones, it lies out past them, in the far far sea with island 11), with the owner's calls the same day: you go down in a dive cage, a giant glow squid worth 91 in the deep (the trench's 45-coin one stays), the giants swim past and are scary with only a few lunging, and the nibblers wait for his drawing (sent the same day: a little brown fish with a forked tail and a big open mouth, and an "attack nibbler", big and shark-shaped with a fin on its back, two clawed fins under it and a mouth full of teeth, biting the tail of what reads as a gloomer, a long body with little fins under it and a frilled flower of a head; the reading to be checked). A blue hole (`world/abyss.ts` ABYSS, 1,500 west of island 11 in the far far sea's north, too far out to be seen from behind the white buoys and well off the far deep's corner where a boat crosses into the far far sea on its way to island 11; first put 1,050 in from island 11 toward home, where it was seen from behind the buoys before the far far sea was open and its toast took the place of the crossing's): on the sea a round patch of the brightest blue with a small dark middle (render/abyss.ts drawAbyssMark). Coming within 700 of it, out past the white buoys, tells what it is, once (abyssSeen in the save; it goes on the sea map, 'Abyss: 1,000 m down', and an edge marker points the way back to it out there), and without the scuba gear, where to get it. Stopped over its dark middle with the scuba gear, Dive into the abyss. The dive is the trench's engine with a site of its own (ABYSS_SITE, 1,000 across and 3,000 deep, three to a metre, its depth told in metres): a shelf of pale reef under the bright water, coral and kelp on it and silver fish over it, sun shafts coming down, with a hole through it barely wider than the cage; under it the hole's dark walls widen until, 320 m down, there are none, glowing strands on them, jellies in the hole, lanternfish in the dark, specks of light far off; and a floor of black sand with a glowing garden across it, 1,000 m down. The diver rides a cage on the boat's rope (entities/diver.ts cage): down in about 15 s, easing at either end, the stick doing nothing and no spear, breathing from the boat; at the floor the door swings open (abyssDown in the save; the first time a toast says how it works) and the diver swims out over the floor (up to 233 m off it, no further: the cage is the way home), the air running only out of the cage and filling again inside it; the spear works there, at the lanternfish over the floor. Ride the cage up (the Swim back up button's place) pulls the diver back into the cage from wherever it is, as the rope under the ice does, and the cage goes up; so does running out of air; pressed on the way down, the cage turns straight back. Back aboard, 'The cage comes up out of the blue hole... 1,000 metres down and back!'. The view keeps the floor up the screen, clear of the buttons (DiveSite.below). The log has an Abyss chip. Building it found the trench's treasure checked on any dive that was not the wreck's, the cage passing the trench's ship's bell's place on the way down and picking it up; it is checked in the trench only now. Next: the deep fish on the floor, then the Remoro, then the giants on the way down.
- The abyss, part two (2026-10-17): the kid's ten deep fish on its floor, all worth 91 (his answer), species 27 to 36 (the Species table; data/tuning.ts ABYSS_FISH): the giant glow squid (the owner's call: the trench's 45-coin glow squid stays, and the deep has a giant one, teal and glowing, three times its size, with two long arms trailing), the gloomer (his: a flower for a mouth, glowing pink, on a naga's long body with little fins all down its underside), the clown fish, the nibbler (his drawing, sent the same day: little and brown with a forked tail and a big open mouth), the stoplight loosejaw (black, its jaw hanging open, a red light under its eye), the viper fish (fangs too long for its mouth, lights along its belly, a tall fin ray), the owl fish (pale, huge tube eyes looking up), the chimaera (a silver ghost shark, a wing of a fin, a spine, a whip of a tail), the coelacanth (big, deep blue, spotted white, fins on fleshy lobes, a three-lobed tail) and the frilled shark (brown and eel-long, frilly gills, little three-pointed teeth). Each is drawn its own way (render/deepfish.ts, side on, the same drawing in the dive and in the aquarium, where they keep low). Over the floor, where the diver out of the cage can reach, each kind keeps to its own height in a shoal of two to five (world/abyss.ts abyssShoals): the glowing ones (the squid, the gloomers, the loosejaws, the viper fish) shine through the dark, the rest show only in the lamp, though the spear's ring marks one in reach. The spear takes them as it takes the trench's: into the hold, the first of each told, its place empty 30 s. Each has a page in the field guide (where: the abyss's floor, 1,000 m down, in the dive cage with scuba gear and a spear; when: day and night, always dark down there; and a line each, the real ones true: the clown fish far from its warm reefs, the loosejaw's red light that most deep fish cannot see, the viper fish's fangs, the coelacanth caught in 1938 after everyone thought it gone with the dinosaurs, the frilled shark unchanged in eighty million years, the chimaera's family and the sharks' going their own ways 400 million years ago). The kid's other drawing, the big toothy one biting a gloomer's tail ("attack nibbler", as read), waits on the owner: a giant that lunges, or a fish to spear. Next: the Remoro, then the giants on the way down.
- The abyss, part three (2026-10-17): the kid's Remoro (his sixth list's answers: the wizard books' silver fish, as big as Star and worth 190, renamed and named by him), species 37 (the Species table; data/tuning.ts REMORO), one great silver fish cruising slow laps over the abyss's floor (entities/remoro.ts), 180 long, as long as Star, about 110 m off the floor and swaying up and down, coming round at either side. Drawn its own way (render/deepfish.ts, the same in the dive and the aquarium): silver, darker along its back, a dark stripe down its side, long low fins either side of its back half, a jutting lower jaw and the ridged sucker plate on top of its head, as a real remora has. Under the dark only the lamp shows it, but a faint silver line along its back and a gleam running along it show over the dark, so it can be found across the floor (render/abyss.ts drawRemoroGlint). Coming near it out of the cage, the first time each dive: 'Something huge and silver in the dark... the Remoro!', with how to land it. Three spears land it: each flashes it white and sends it darting off away from the diver for a moment, then it cruises on; the spear's ring goes to whichever is nearer, the Remoro or a deep fish (it took the ring from nearer fish at first, which the nibbler smoke test caught). The third goes into the hold, worth 190, and it is gone until tomorrow (remoroDay in the save; it is back the next day, as the giant clam's pearl is); with the hold full, it shakes off the last spear and darts away. Back down the same day, the floor's toast says it is gone for today. It does no harm. The calls taken without asking, to put to the owner: three spears, one a day, 190 as he said. Next: the giants passing the cage on the way down.
- The abyss, part four (2026-10-17): the giants pass the cage on the way down, the kid's (1,000 m down are "the leviathans, Reapers, the Cthuluviathan, Cthulhu, gulpers") with the owner's call: they swim past, scary, huge shapes and glowing eyes in the dark close enough to be thrilling, and only a few lunge, with a warning (entities/abyssgiants.ts). Each ride down all five come past, one at each of five depths from where the hole is wider than the screen (the cage at 700, 1,150, 1,600, 2,050 and 2,500), in an order of the ride's own: the leviathan (620 long, a dark serpent with spines down its back and cyan lights down its side), the Reaper (his own drawing, 420 long, legs paddling), the Cthuluviathan (its green dome leading, a bat wing on top, tentacles streaming behind) and a pair of gulpers (black eels nearly all mouth, a pink light on each tail) swim across from one side to the other, rising past the cage as it goes down; Cthulhu hangs in the water to one side, 290 tall, his own drawing, his eyes going red as the cage sinks past them, then sinks back into the dark (render/abyssgiants.ts). Their bodies are under the dark, so the lamp shows them only close; over the dark go a faint edge of each and their eyes and lights. Each rumbles as it comes. One of the swimmers lunges each ride, never the last, so its slam is over well before the floor: a roar, its eyes flare and its jaw drops as it turns on the cage, then it comes, slams into the bars (a clang, a burst of bubbles, the cage shaking) and turns away. 'The Reaper slams into the cage! The bars hold.' (the first ride, 'nothing can get you in here'). In the cage nothing is lost: the lunge that takes air, as the owner chose, is out on the floor, the next piece. Ridden back up before they come, they never do. Next: a giant over the floor that lunges at a diver out of the cage and takes air.
- Fix (2026-10-17): a diver who let go of the stick in the abyss's cage on the floor drifted up, as divers do everywhere when let go, and out through the cage's roof in about a second, so 'swim back in to fill your tank' left you outside again at once, breathing from the tank. Let go in the cage on the floor, the diver now holds on to the bars, settling in its middle (entities/diver.ts), and swims out again with the stick. Found building the gulper that hunts over the floor, for which the cage is the safe place.
- The abyss, part five (2026-10-17): the lunge that takes air, the owner's call ("only a few lunge, with a warning, and a lunge takes air, like the lurker"), out on the floor where it matters, the cage filling the tank. A gulper hunts over the floor (the trench's lurker's hunt, entities/lurker.ts, which now takes a place to live, LurkPlace: the trench's midnight water as it always was, or the abyss's floor, world/abyss.ts ABYSS_LURK). After 14 s out of the cage it comes out of the dark (a gulper's hiss; the first time each dive, 'A gulper, out of the dark!' and what to do), circles just out of the lamp, then its mouth gapes and it lunges: still there and it bites the tank, 25 s of air bubbling out ('Gulp!... Back to the cage!'). Swim aside and it shoots past; back in the cage and it lets you be, and its wait starts over; a spear stings it off for a good while. Drawn as the passing gulpers are (render/abyssgiants.ts), black and nearly all mouth, its eye and pink tail light over the dark. The spear's ring goes to whichever is nearest: a deep fish, the Remoro or the gulper. Next: the attack nibbler; HQ upgrades; the glass bridge and the tar ocean.
- The owner's calls (2026-10-18), on the three open questions: the attack nibbler hunts the deep fish (his drawing: biting a gloomer's tail), speared as the Remoro is, a few spears and worth a lot, leaving the diver alone unless in its way; the HQ upgrades cost 2,000 to 10,000 each (a full hold of abyss fish sells for about 10,000); and the glass bridge goes on a new edge out past island 11 and the abyss, across the far far sea's outer edge, the tar ocean and the tar island beyond it, sailed under or walked across.
- The abyss, part six (2026-10-18): the attack nibbler, species 38 (the Species table; data/tuning.ts ATTACK_NIBBLER), the kid's drawing: big and shark-shaped, brown as the nibblers it is named for, a fin on its back, two clawed fins under it and a mouth full of teeth, wide open (render/deepfish.ts, the same in the dive and the aquarium; its amber eye glints over the dark, render/abyss.ts). It prowls the abyss's floor (entities/attacknibbler.ts); every few seconds it looks for a gloomer or a nibbler within 340 and goes after it, fast, and snaps it up ('Snap!'; the fish is gone 30 s, as a speared one), then rests. The first chase each dive tells what it is and that three spears land it. It leaves the diver alone, unless the diver is in front of its mouth as it charges: then it bites the tank (25 s of air, 'Chomp! You got in the attack nibbler's way') and darts off. Three spears land it as the Remoro (each flashes it and sends it darting off; the last only with room in the hold), worth 150, one a day (hunterDay in the save). The spear's ring goes to the nearest of a deep fish, the Remoro, the attack nibbler or the gulper. The calls taken without asking: 150, three spears, one a day, as the Remoro.
- The owner's calls on the headquarters' upgrades (2026-10-18), asked what each of the kid's four does: the dock (4,000) is a castle landing on the sea by island 7, to sail straight to Gigantis and walk in through the gate, the castle then on the sea map; the forge (6,000) sharpens the spear, the knife and the harpoon for good, each hitting one harder (refined the same day, on my warning that one harder on the harpoon would cut every sea fight by a third, the Reaper from five harpoons to three: the spear and the knife hit one harder, the harpoon reloads faster instead, 1.2 s to 0.8 s); the bigger tower (10,000) shows the fish schools, from its top and on the sea map; the garden (2,000) grows a free snack a day. What he keeps there ("all": trophies, treasure, spare weapons and armour, fish) and picking companions there come with the castle, free.
- Your headquarters, part one (2026-10-18): the castle's plans and the garden. On a heavy table in Gigantis's courtyard, near the way in (`data/hq.ts` PLANS_AT; render/hq.ts drawPlans: a big sheet unrolled with the castle drawn on it in brown ink, an ink pot and a quill), Look at the castle plans opens a card of the four upgrades, each with what it does and its price; one not in the game yet says Soon. The garden is the first: built (hq in the save, 2,000 coins), it is planted in the courtyard's front-left corner, clear of the flagpole, the telescope, the stairs and the portal: an apple tree with red apples in it, a row of carrots, a banana plant with a yellow bunch (render/hq.ts drawGardenPlant), a star twinkling over each while today's snack is still to be picked. Walk up to one and Pick an apple, Pull up a carrot or Pick a banana: it goes in the snack bag (the old man's snacks: speed, power, two hearts), and that is the day's (gardenDay in the save); another grows by tomorrow. A full bag of that kind says to pick something else. Next: the forge, the tower, the dock, the trophy hall and picking companions.
- Your headquarters, part two (2026-10-18): the forge. On the castle's plans for 6,000; built, it stands in the courtyard's front-right corner (`data/hq.ts` FORGE_AT, clear of the stairs, the portal and the way in; render/hq.ts drawForge: a stone hearth under a hood and chimney, coals glowing in it and smoke curling up, an anvil on its block, the bellows). Walk up to it and Sharpen your weapons, once, for good (sharp in the save): three clangs, sparks off the anvil, and from then on the spear and the knife hit one harder (sharpened: ashore, on the Battle Barge and at the lagoon creatures below island 11; with a carrot it is doubled after) and the harpoon reloads in 0.8 s, not 1.2 (SHARP_RELOAD). The forge's coals glow on after. Next: the bigger tower, the dock, the trophy hall and picking companions.
- Your headquarters, part three (2026-10-18): the bigger tower. On the castle's plans for 10,000; built, a tall square lookout of the castle's grey-green stone stands on the courtyard's back wall, between the way up into the castle and the portal, clear of the portal's ring on the screen (`data/hq.ts` BIG_TOWER_AT; render/hq.ts drawHqTower: arrow slits up it, a wooden door at its foot on the courtyard side, merlons round its top and a gold pennant over them). Its door (BIG_TOWER_DOOR) is far enough from the stairs up that each is used on its own. Climb the tower opens the sea map; and from the day it is built the map shows every fish school (render/seamap.ts mapFish, drawMapFish): one to three little fish in the school's fish colour, outlined dark so the pale ones show, more for a fuller school, a school fished out gone until its fish come back, the night schools only once it is dark enough for them to be up. They are drawn under the fog: the water you have not sailed keeps its fish to itself. The map's note says so. Next: the dock, the trophy hall and picking companions.
- Your headquarters, part four (2026-10-18): the dock, the last of the kid's four. On the castle's plans for 4,000; built, your castle's landing stands on the sea off island 7's north-east (`data/hq.ts` CASTLE_LANDING; render/hq.ts drawLanding: a square stone quay in the water with mooring posts and steps down, a gatehouse on its back half with a turret either side and a gold pennant, its arch glowing with Gigantis's green sky), and on the sea map as a little gold castle named Your castle, its name over it so it keeps off island 7's. A hull is kept off the quay (LANDING_R). Stop the boat beside it (within LANDING_REACH, slower than LANDING_STOP) and Through the castle gate takes you straight into the courtyard, the camera with you at once; Back to your boat puts you aboard again where you left it. Nothing at sea troubles a boat with nobody aboard, so it waits safely. The portal on island 7 still works as before, and goes back to island 7. The owner's call was to walk in through the gate from the dock; the gate is a button at the landing rather than a few steps on a walkable quay, since the quay would need new walkable ground for one doorway. Next: the trophy hall and picking companions.
- The metal bridge, part one (2026-10-18): the outside of the kid's facility, his Part 6 ("tar ocan splat by the metal bridge ... not the bridges more like a facility") and his two drawings of the same day, with the owner's call on where: a new edge to the sea past island 11 and the abyss, across the far far sea's outer edge, with the tar ocean beyond it. The sea now ends to the north at y -5,400 (`world/metalbridge.ts` NORTH_EDGE, the far far sea's outer line); past it the water is tar, black and glossy with slow sheen bands, a seam where the two meet and bubbles rising near the building (render/metalbridge.ts drawTarOcean, drawn over the far far sea's water). Along the edge, its middle at x 7,400 (west of island 11, over the abyss) and 1,800 long, stands the building as he drew it: a long metal body on legs in the sea with foam round each, a row of square windows and one long window band over them, a lattice along the roof, a flag at each end and a purple orb glowing on a pedestal in the middle of the roof; it is drawn in nine sections, each sorted by its own depth, so a boat by one end is in front of it and behind the other end (metalBridgeSolids). In the middle of its sea face is a door with a light over it and four steps down to a landing at the water. Hulls are kept off its sea face (pushOffMetalBridge). Coming within 700 of it aboard, short of the abyss's and island 11's own sightings, it is told once ('Whoa! A huge metal building stands across the sea on legs ... the tar ocean! There is a door on this side.'), saved (bridgeSeen; a save from before has not seen it) and marked on the sea map as 'The bridge', a grey bar along the edge with the orb, its name over it. Stopped at the door (within 170, nearly still), Knock on the door: 'Knock, knock! A voice from inside: "Not yet, not yet! We are still getting the bridge ready in here. Come back soon!"'. Next, part two, the inside, from his answers in his own tab ("the bridge for rowan"): through the door from the boat, your character's room, the round observatory in the middle, the science room with the scientist who gives stealth missions, the missions room with a hologram of the tar eel, the news room with news about the tar and a tentacle for testing, and the dock on the tar side where a tar animal with tentacles stays in the water. Part three: the stealth missions, the tar eel, and the tar boat ("not made of tar tho") to the tar side's island with a tower on a hill and its tar creatures.
- Fix (2026-10-18): on the sea map, a name near its side ran off the edge: the abyss's, 'Abyss: 1,000 m down', out by the far far sea's east corner, read 'Abyss: 1,000 m dow'. Every name on the map (the islands', the marks', your castle's, the bridge's and the leviathans') is now moved in just enough to be whole (render/seamap.ts labelX); its mark stays where it is. Found building the metal bridge.
- Fix (2026-10-23): names on the sea map sat on one another, and on the islands and marks. "Trench: dive here" covered island 6's name for anyone with scuba gear, "Islands 8 & 9" sat over one of its two islands, and with the far far sea open the abyss's name ran across islands 10 and 11 while island 11's sat on the abyss. Each island and mark now keeps its patch of the map, and each name goes where it would rather (under, or over for those that go over), else over or under instead, else to one side, wherever it covers nothing; failing that, where it covers least (render/seamap.ts nameSpots, freeSpot, placeNames). The marks' names go first, then the islands', the most hemmed-in first, so one with room to spare never takes the only place another had. The leviathans' eyes keep their own. Found checking the player's guide against the map.
- The metal bridge, part two (2026-10-18): the inside, from the kid's drawing of it and his answers in his tab ("the bridge for rowan"). Stopped at the door, In through the door (the knock now opens it): the figure goes in as at the castle's landing, the boat waiting outside with nobody aboard, and Back to your boat from anywhere inside puts it back aboard there. Six rooms (world/tower.ts ROOMS 28 to 33, kind 'bridge'; world/bridgein.ts where everything in them stands; render/bridgein.ts), metal walls with a light strip round them and a plated floor: the round observatory in the middle (his "round middle room"), tall windows onto the stars all round it, a big brass telescope in the middle, and five sliding doors round its back wall, each with a sign over it, YOU, SCIENCE, TAR DOCK, MISSIONS and NEWS; each of those rooms has one door back, OBSERVATORY, and coming through a door puts the figure a few steps in, past the button's reach, so a second tap does not take it straight back. Your room ("your character"): a bunk with a blue blanket (Sleep till morning, as in the castle) and a mirror (Look in the mirror opens the character card). The science room: two lab benches of bubbling flasks and the scientist, in a white coat with wild white hair and goggles up on the forehead (Talk to the scientist: a welcome, then that sneaky missions are coming, the tentacle, the telescope). The missions room: a round table with the tar eel's hologram swimming round over it, flickering (Look at the hologram: the missions are coming soon). The news room: a screen on the wall, TAR NEWS over a tar beast's picture with the news running along the bottom (Read the tar news: six headlines in turn), and the tentacle in its tank, kept for testing (Tap the glass: it slaps back). The tar dock: its back open onto the tar ocean behind a rail, yellow and black along the edge, and out in the tar a tar animal with tentacles and glowing yellow eyes, which stays in the tar (Look at the tar animal). Through the telescope, the tar island with its tower on a hill. The first time in, the way round is told (bridgeIn in the save; a save from before has not been in). Part three: the scientist's stealth missions, the tar eel, and the tar boat to the tar island.
- The bridge's orb (2026-10-19), the kid's (his answers: the bridge's orb is island 9's, and "putting your hand in it shows you knowledge, of the boats and the past and more"). A ladder stands in the observatory's front left (world/bridgein.ts LADDER_UP), Up the ladder to the roof: the bridge's roof (ROOMS 38, BRIDGE_ROOF), open to the stars, the sea far below on one side and the tar on the other, a round plated deck with a rail all round and the open hatch the ladder comes up through (render/bridgeroof.ts), and in the middle on its stand the orb from his drawing, glowing purple, light swirling in it. The first time up, the game says what it is. Put your hand in the orb and it glows brighter while its card is open (data/orb.ts): The boats (your own by its tier, the warlock's, the flotilla's crafts with a friend aboard and who sails each, the swan paddle boat, the chemistry boat, the treasure submarine, whichever are yours), The past (every boss beaten so far, in the order you meet them, as its trophy in the trophy hall tells it; nothing yet, said so), and And more (the next boss not yet beaten, and where, the trophy's own hint; all of them beaten, Skull Island). So it also answers the kid's question from before, where to go next, from the far side of the sea. Back to the observatory from the hatch.
- Stealth mission 1 (2026-10-18): the kid's tab 15, "get to the tower of island 2 and kick the sorcerer off and tada get a boat", with a map of rooms and tunnels he found (an archaeologist's plan of four joined Theban tombs, 295, 296, 365 and 178, in four colours), and his answers in his tab "stealth mission for rowan": a new island 2, out on the tar ocean, the same sorcerer; "blue to red"; seen, the mission starts over, "but remember you can backstab em"; kicked "into same portal heron does", the Heron saying "come on"; the boat "a chemistry boat"; invisibility in this mission. At the bridge's missions room table, Start mission 1 (Mission 1 again once won): down a hatch into the tunnels under the tar (world/stealth.ts; render/stealth.ts), traced from his map in its own pixels (MAP_K world to a pixel), his colours kept on the tops of their walls: in through the blue tunnels at the bottom, up through the black hall with its stairs down the west side and the walls inside it, across the long green corridor with its pillar, through the red halls with theirs, up a ladder at the top. Sandstone floors and walls, rubble filling the far ends; walkable only on the floor, off the walls, out of the pillars, walls and rubble (entities/walker.ts onLand). Six guards walk it, four skeleton troops with spears and lanterns and two demons with horns and tails, on rounds or standing and turning their heads, each throwing its light on the floor ahead (150 long, half a radian either side), cut short by walls and pillars; one that sees you for a third of a second puts a "!" up and the mission starts over ('Spotted!'). Close behind one (34) that does not see you, Backstab!: a skeleton falls to a heap of bones, a demon goes up in smoke, down until the mission starts over. The scientist's invisibility potion, given at the first start (invis in the save): Invisible (the button where the bug spray's is) for 5 seconds, 12 to come back, see-through and shimmering, seen then only up close (32): never perfect. Up the ladder: the top of the sorcerer's tower, a round stone roof high over the tar with battlements and two crates, the sorcerer (the chimera) hanging by its far edge looking out at the Heron's portal turning in the tar below, turning round every few seconds to look back across the roof; seen by him, back to the top of the ladder. Close behind him while he looks out, Kick him off!: he tumbles off the edge and down into the portal, the camera going over to it, and the Heron leans up out of it, a wing waving, "Come on!", and it closes. Tada: back at the bridge's tar dock, the chemistry boat tied up in the tar, white with a green stripe, a flask on its side and a chimney puffing green bubbles (stealth1 in the save; Look at your chemistry boat). Leave the tunnels any time, back to the missions room. The scientist sends you to mission 1, and once it is won, praises you. Next: sailing the tar ocean in the chemistry boat.
- Your headquarters, part five (2026-10-18): who comes with you, the kid's ("i meant you chose companions there": the headquarters is where you pick which companions come with you). A roll-call board stands in Gigantis's courtyard, at its right past the telescope, clear of the portal (`data/stay.ts` ROSTER_AT, ROSTER_REACH 30; render/hq.ts drawRoster: a notice board on two posts under a little red roof, a sheet pinned to it with a line for each friend you have, a green tick by each who comes and a red cross by each who stays). Choose who comes with you opens a card listing every friend you have, in the order they join you (the warlock, the tarling, the cat, the warrior, the skeleton, the naga, the necromancer, the baby Reaper, Caterman), each with a line on what it does and two chips, Comes with you (as ever) or Stays at the castle. One that stays comes nowhere with you, not ashore, not riding the boat or its own (the warlock's boat stays with him; a flotilla seat it had sits empty), not in a fight, and is not on the badges card while it is there; it waits in the courtyard at its own spot (WAIT_AT: about the courtyard, clear of everything and of each other on the screen, none behind the portal's ring, the tall ones toward the back), still, facing the way in, wearing its badge. Called back, it is beside you at once. Saved as `stay` (a list; old saves read nobody, unknown names and repeats are dropped). Each friend's being yours is unchanged (main.js HAS_FRIEND, from the same story steps as before), and its `.free` is now that and not staying; the warlock's cage in island 3's tower still shows him freed while he waits.
- Your headquarters, part six (2026-10-18): the trophy hall, the kid's (asked what you keep at your headquarters, "trophies from the bosses you beat, treasure, spare weapons and armour, fish", his answer "all"; the trophies first, the fish, the armour and weapons, and the treasure to follow, each its own PR). The second hall of Gigantis, the way through to the throne room, keeps a stone stand for each boss round its wall (`data/trophies.ts`: 21, STAND_RING 126, gaps either side of the way up and the way in, ten up its left side and eleven down its right, STAND_REACH 20; render/trophies.ts drawStand, a brass plate on its front, TROPHY_SCALE 1.25), and on it, once the boss is beaten, what it left behind, each drawn small: the sorcerer's staff (island 2's tower), the swordsman's sword and a demon's horns (under island 3), the Tar Anchorer's anchor, dripping, the Shark King's jaws, the Deep One's eye in a jar, looking about, a little stone Cthulhu, the sorcerer's spellbook (island 7's tower), the Forgotten One's crown, the Old One's black pearl in its shell, the Heron's feather (beaten for good), the pelican's golden egg, the Reaper on land's claw, the Forgotten One's sword laid on two pegs (his crypt), the sorcerer's hat (stealth mission 1); and the leviathans driven off with the harpoon (the save's `driven`): the leviathan's spine with cyan lights, a piece of the Cthuluviathan's green dome, the anglerfish's lure, a gulper's blinking tail light, a piece of Star with glowing cracks and the Reaper's tooth, the lit ones glowing in the dark hall. Until then a stand is empty, a pale question mark on it. Walk up to one: Look at (its name) tells its story; Look at the empty stand says who to beat and where. Coming in, it says how many of the 21 are filled (every stand full: you beat them all). Nothing new saved: each is read from the story's own flags.
- Your headquarters, part seven (2026-10-18): your fish, the second of the kid's "all" (what you keep at your headquarters). In the first hall of Gigantis, in front of its left window and clear of the bed and the way up, a tall board of dark wood stands on two legs facing the hall (`data/fishwall.ts`: FISH_WALL_AT, 48 either side along its face, six places across, a row every 15 up; render/fishwall.ts drawFishWall): a framed place for every kind of fish in the sea, in the field guide's order from the top left, each drawn in its own colours and markings once caught (the save's lifetime catches), a dark shape until then, the crabs as crabs, a gold plank along its top with a fish on it. Look at your fish (12 of 39) opens the field guide. Nothing new saved.
- Your headquarters, part eight (2026-10-18): the armoury, the third of the kid's "all" ("spare weapons and armour"). In the throne room of Gigantis, round its left side, six wooden stands on stone feet, one for each armour: the shipwright's five in his order (leather, diamond, gold, space, Honor Guard, each in its own plate, edge and shine, the helmet, the space bubble, the plume and the red cape) and the soul armour from the ruins, pale and see-through and glowing; round its right, five racks, one for each weapon: the fishing spear, the long spear, the barbed spear, a spare harpoon and the survival knife (`data/armoury.ts`: STAND_R 150, the armour from 0.8 pi and the weapons from 1.36 pi, 0.21 apart, clear of the throne, its locked door and the way in, ARMOURY_REACH 22; render/armoury.ts). Each holds its own once you have it (the save's armour and spear levels, the knife with the Honor Guard, soulArmour), and stands bare until then. Look at the gold armour says what it does and whether it's the best you have (worn) or outgrown; an empty one says the shipwright sells it, or for the soul armour that something waits in the ruins. Only to look at: the owner's call on switching gear there is still open. Nothing new saved.
- Your headquarters, part nine (2026-10-18): your treasure, the last of the kid's "all". In the middle of the trophy hall, inside its ring of stands, a round stone dais (`data/treasury.ts` HEAP_R 24, too big to walk through, the figure pushed out of it as from Cthulhu; HEAP_REACH 46; render/treasury.ts drawTreasureHeap), and on it a mound of gold that grows with what you have found, every treasure found once laid on it: the trench's spyglass, crown, bell, gem and chest, the five things chipped out of the ice, the chest in the wreck off island 5, and island 6's six chests (17; the giant clam's pearl, back every day, is not one), the chests round its foot and the rest up it, sparkling. Look at your treasure (11 of 17) says how many and what is still out there, and where; with none, the dais says where treasure is. Nothing spent and nothing new saved: read from trenchLoot, iceLoot, wreckChest and chests6. With it the kid's headquarters is whole: the companions picker, and the trophies, fish, armour and weapons, and treasure kept there.
- The tar ocean, part one (2026-10-18): sailing it. The kid's tab 17 ("I want the tar ocean now the size of this oceane and tar fish 250 coins ONLY to the facility") and his answers in his tab "tar ocean for rowan": only the chemistry boat goes on it ("only tar boat chemichcstrey"), as big as the "whole sea" out to the white buoys, tar fish caught with the "spear of fishing rod", "black fish", the scientist buys them, and "all" of the tar island, the tar eel and the tar creatures on it. In the bridge's tar dock, Sail the chemistry boat (once stealth mission 1 is won): out at the end of a jetty from the bridge's back, onto the tar ocean (`world/tarsea.ts`: north of the bridge, as wide as the sea, SEA_SPAN, and as deep again; the boat kept TAR_MARGIN inside it, slowed at its edges; TAR_BERTH 240 out from the edge so the building does not hide the boat; CHEM_SPEED 240). It steers as the flagship does (entities/boat.ts steerBoat and steerBoatRelative, stick and keys alike), drawn with the flagship's hull drawing in white with a green stripe, a green flask and a chimney puffing green bubbles on it (render/tarsea.ts CHEM_LOOK, drawChemTop). The tar: black as far as you can see, slow sheens rolling over it, bubbles rising and popping on a grid that stays put as it scrolls, the bridge and a strip of the sea past it to the south, yellow and black hazard buoys with red lights along its three open edges, the jetty and a green ring at the berth. Back at the ring and stopped, Into the tar dock; or Back to the bridge from anywhere. A scene of its own, as the tunnels are: the sea stands still while you are out, and the flagship waits at the bridge's door. Its fish come next, then the tar island with the tower, the tar eel and the tar creatures.
- The tar ocean, part two (2026-10-18): its fish. Black tar fish (`world/tarfish.ts`, drawn by render/tarfish.ts): glossy black, a pale sheen along the back, pale yellow eyes, only the back and fin out of the tar as they circle in schools of three (PER_SCHOOL, SCHOOL_R 46) with a ring spreading behind each, and now and then one leaps clear in an arc and drops back with a splash of tar. A dozen a day (TAR_PER_DAY, four schools), seeded from the day so they are where they were after a reload and somewhere new each morning: the first north of the tar dock, 1,400 to 3,200 from it (NEAR_MAX), the rest anywhere on the tar 600 inside its edges and 2,600 apart. A lavender arrow at the screen's edge with a black fish on it points to the nearest school with any left, and a green one back to the tar dock (pulsing once the day's dozen are caught and some are aboard). Stopped within 240 (the flagship rod's ROD_RANGE and ROD_SPEED), Cast for tar fish; the chemistry boat's own rod works as the flagship's does for lionfish (fishing/rod.ts): wait, reel in when the float goes under; too soon or too late and it is still there to try again. Caught, a fish goes in the chemistry boat's tar bucket, a chip by the junk with a black fish and the count, shown wherever there are some (hidden diving and on the ice). Nobody buys them but the scientist (the kid's "can only be sold at the facility"): Talk to the scientist with any aboard and the scientist takes them all, TAR_FISH_PRICE 250 each, before anything else is said. The scientist's praise for mission 1 and the toast going out say so; a field guide page shows once the chemistry boat is yours. Saved: tarDay, tarTaken (a bit for each of that day's dozen), tarBucket, tarCaught. The economy is a guess for the owner to tune: at most 3,000 a day, and the sea is big enough that a day's dozen takes most of a day to sail round.
- The tar ocean, part three (2026-10-18): the tar island, the kid's "island 2 (new)" of the tar ocean, where mission 1's tunnels came up (his sixth list's drawing: "on the tar side an island with a tower on a hill"). Out on the tar 3,800 north of the bridge and 1,500 west (`world/tarisle.ts` TAR_ISLE, radius 440, its shore wobbling a little round it), drawn by render/tarisle.ts: black rock with a glossy wet rim where the tar laps it, a hill in its middle (HILL_R 240, 46 high, in bands up to its top), a path of paler stones from the jetty up to the tower's door, five dead black trees and four boulders, which the figure walks round; and on the hilltop the sorcerer's old tower, round and of grey stone as it is from its roof in mission 1 (TAR_TOWER, 56 across, 190 high), battlements round its top, slit windows with a faint purple light, a dark door toward the viewer. A jetty on posts runs out from its south shore toward the bridge. Sailing, a grey-brown arrow at the screen's edge points to it (pulsing until you have been ashore, tarIsleSeen in the save; not shown once you are within 600 of its shore); the chemistry boat is kept off its rock (keepOffIsle) and the day's tar fish 500 off its shore. Stopped alongside the jetty's end (ISLE_BERTH), Step ashore: the figure walks it as it walks the tunnels (setWalkLand with onTarIsle and tarIsleZ, the sea's own update running, the flagship waiting at the bridge's door and the chemistry boat at the jetty), up the hill and round the tower; at the tower's door, Try the tower door: barred from inside, and slow, heavy footsteps up on the high ground behind it (the tar leader's, to come). At the jetty's end, Back aboard, or Back to the chemistry boat from anywhere on it. The Battle Barge button stays away while out on the tar. Its tar creatures and the tar leader come next.
- The tar ocean, part four (2026-10-19): the tar creatures on the tar island (`entities/tarfolk.ts`, drawn by render/tarfolk.ts), the kid's tar humans and tar beasts, with his numbers (3 to 5 damage, 15 to 20 hits) and the owner's rule that every blow is wound up so it can be dodged. Black and glossy as the tar, a pale rim round them, dripping, with yellow eyes that flare as they rise. Four tar humans stand about the shore (TAR_HOMES; 15 hits, 3 hearts a slam, 44 a second), two tar beasts prowl the hill (18 hits, 4 hearts a lunge, 54); none within 250 of the jetty. Within 150 one rises (0.8 s), and the others within 220 with it, then comes on, slower than the figure walks. A tar human from 28 off raises its arms over its head into one glob for 0.9 s while a red ring 26 across shows on the ground 22 in front of it, then slams it; a tar beast from 66 off crouches for a second while a red lane 80 long and 40 wide shows ahead of it, then lunges along it, as far as the island lets it. Each is open a moment after. The kid's wise tar beast: down to 40% of its hits, it runs from the figure, round rocks, trees and the tower if it must, and fights again only when it is cornered, its back to the tar (stuck half a second); cornered once, it never runs again. Beaten, each melts into a puddle: 60 coins a tar human, 90 a tar beast. They are back every landing; away from the island they stand still and are no fight (TarFolks.here). The spear, the knife and every companion go for them (warlock.findTarget), and their blows hurt companions as the figure; companions now come ashore on the tar island and are drawn there. The scanner knows both (data/scans.ts tarhuman, tarbeast). Bonked out on the island, the figure wakes aboard the chemistry boat at the jetty, nothing lost (not aboard the flagship at the bridge, which would leave the tar ocean's scene behind). In the tests a person with the second spear who keeps 80 to 115 off, dodges a third of a second late and throws beats either, every time, losing at most one blow's hearts. The tar leader up on the high ground and the tar dragon come next, then the tar eel.
- The tar ocean, part five (2026-10-19): the tar leader (`entities/tarleader.ts`, drawn by render/tarleader.ts), the kid's boss of the tar island ("tar leader", up on the "higher area"; his drawing's tar man with a sword and 25 hits; 4 damage, in the 3 to 5 he settled on for the tar creatures; the owner's wind-up rule).
