A tar man a head and a half taller than his tar humans: a crown of tar spikes, a cape dripping off his shoulders, burning orange eyes, a long black sword with a purple gleam along its edge. He waits on the high ground beside the sorcerer's tower (LEADER_HOME, on the hilltop on its side toward the viewer); within 190, or struck by a spear, he roars (1 s) and comes down, at 40. From 50 he draws his sword back for 1 s while a red fan shows where it will sweep (64 out, 2.3 radians wide), then sweeps it: four hearts in it. From 95 to 250, no sooner than 5 s after the last, he crouches for 1.1 s while a red ring 38 across shows where the figure stands, then leaps there in 0.45 s: four hearts in it. He is open 1 s after a sweep, 1.2 s after a landing. Hurt to half, once, he roars for help and two tar humans rise out of the rock beside him (TarFolks.summon; gone again at the next landing). Beaten, he melts into the rock: 3,000 coins and his sword, a trophy in the castle's trophy hall ('tarsword', the hall now 22 stands, 11 a side). He stays beaten (tarLeaderBeaten in the save); the tower's door stays barred, and all is quiet up there after. The spear goes for him when he is the nearer of him and the tar creatures; the companions too; the scanner knows him. In the tests a person with the second spear who keeps 90 to 120 off, dodges a third of a second late and throws beats him every time, losing at most one blow's hearts. The tar dragon comes next, then the tar eel.
- The tar ocean, part six (2026-10-19): the tar dragon (`entities/tardragon.ts`, drawn by render/tardragon.ts), the kid's (his sixth list's drawing: a winged tar dragon with lots of legs, 10 hits and 1 damage; his later answer for all the tar creatures, 3 to 5 damage and 15 to 20 hits, which wins, as it did for the tar humans; the owner's wind-up rule). Black and glossy as the tar with a pale rim: two great bat wings, six legs in a row under a long body, a horned head on a long neck with amber eyes, a long tail dripping tar. It circles high (110) over the island's south-east slope (DRAGON_ROOST, 80 round), seen from the boat too; its shadow on the ground shows where it is and how high. Walk under it (within 120 of the middle; no spot wakes it and the tar leader both) and it shrieks (1 s) and comes. It hangs off the figure on its far side from the viewer (so up high it is seen out beyond the figure, not over it), at 70, and goes for a blow once there or after 2.6 s at most, the two in turn: it rears back with its throat glowing for 0.8 s while three red rings 30 across in a row across its line to the figure show where its tar will land, then spits three balls of tar that land 0.6 s later, three hearts in a ring; or it hangs with its wings up for 1 s while a red lane 48 wide shows through the figure, then swoops down it in 0.7 s, low enough to catch anyone in it for the second half, three hearts, and lands 90 past the figure (nearer if that is off the rock). On the ground it pants for 2.4 s, then climbs back up in 0.9 s. Up high no spear reaches it (a spear that arrives as it climbs says 'Too high!'); down low (under 34) the spear, the companions and the scanner all find it, the scanner up high too. A stun holds it down 1 s longer, once a landing. 18 hits; beaten, it falls and melts away for 500 coins. Every landing it is back; beaten, it stays gone while you sail. In the tests a person with the second spear who dodges a third of a second late and throws when it is down beats it every time, without a hit, in about 35 s; dodging a whole second late is still enough, 1.2 s is not. The tar eel comes next.
- Finding the tar island (2026-10-19), the kid's report: he sailed the chemistry boat round and never found it. Its arrow was a grey dock, like the tar dock's green one, and nothing said what it was. Now the arrow carries a little tower (the sorcerer's, on its hill), pulsing until the island is found; the sail-out message says to follow the arrow with the tower on it, or look on the map; and the map button, out on the tar, shows the tar ocean's own map (render/tarmap.ts, 'Tar map'): the tar from side to side, the bridge along its bottom edge with the tar dock's green ring, the tar island named with its tower and a slow pulse, today's tar fish with fish left in them, and the boat. Back at the bridge it is the sea map again.
- The tar ocean, part seven (2026-10-19): the tar eel (`entities/tareel.ts`, drawn by render/tareel.ts), mission 2, the kid's (his answers in his tab "tar eel for rowan": twice the leviathan; all over the tar ocean; it "bites and wraps", "bringing under, which brings you to the nearest area"; 6 hits and "20 dm"; "a harpoon on boat"; beating it is "part of story" and a mission, "plus monkey is in cage"; the red tar tentacles are on "island 1 tar" and there is a tar kraken "you don't see YET"). The owner's call: the chemistry boat gets a health bar, 100, and a bite takes 20 of it. The missions room's table, where its hologram turns, now offers "Mission 2: the tar eel" once mission 1 is won ("Mission 1 again" once the eel is beaten); taking it (eelStage 1 in the save) fits the chemistry boat with a harpoon and shows its health bar on the tar. Out on the open tar, after 7 s it finds the boat (never within 600 of the tar dock or 360 of the tar island's jetty, where it lets go and goes off under): its long shadow comes up under the tar 340 off, bubbling, and it surfaces, black and glossy with a pale rim, a finned head on a long neck with red eyes, its back breaking the tar in humps along the way it swam, 1,520 long (two guard leviathans), laid out behind it from the moment it rises. It swims round the boat 250 off for 2.8 s, its head up, then in turns: it rears back 1.1 s while a red ring 70 across shows where the boat was, and strikes there (20 off the boat's health in it); or it throws its coils round the boat in a red ring that closes from 230 to 60 over 2.4 s and drifts after the boat at 90: out through it, or it pulls the boat under. Pulled under, or bitten to none, the boat comes up at the nearer of the tar dock and the tar island's jetty, whole, nothing lost, and the eel stays off 20 s. The harpoon (range 360, 1.2 s reload) reaches its head while it is up, not while its coils are round the boat; each hit sends it under for 1.2 s and it comes back with its next blow, so every other hit is a wrap to sail out of and the others a bite to dodge or to harpoon it out of. Six and it thrashes and sinks for good: 4,000 coins, mission 2 done (eelStage 2). While it is up the camera frames the fight. In the tests a sailor who keeps moving, dodges a third of a second late and fires when it is up beats it every time in about 24 s, never pulled under; starting the dodge out of its coils as late as 1.6 s still gets out, 2 s does not. The caged monkey and the red tar tentacles and tar kraken come after, asked.
- The evil monkey, caged (2026-10-19), the kid's (his answers in his tab "monkey and tentacles for rowan": the cage is "in the bridge", and "it is the same monkey as island 4 DO NOT LET IT OUT"). Beating the tar eel (eelStage 2) brings up a cage where it sank, with a monkey in it: the evil monkey from island 4's beach, the one that called up the tar monster and vanished in a purple puff. The scientist keeps it in the science room's far corner (world/bridgein.ts MONKEY_CAGE, drawn by render/bridgein.ts): a round cage of iron bars on a plate, a ring on top, a padlock on its door, and inside the monkey in its skull mask with red glints in its eyes, hanging on the bars and grinning. Stand in front of it (CAGE_LOOK) and Look at the monkey rattles the cage, the monkey up to something new each time (rattling the bars, holding out a paw to shake, pointing at the lock and grinning, hanging upside down blowing a raspberry), and the scientist the same every time: do NOT let it out. There is no letting it out. No new save field: the cage is there once eelStage is 2. The red tar tentacles (all over the tar, 2 hits and 4 damage, his answers too) come next; the tar kraken waits on his pick of four ideas, asked in the tab "tar kraken ideas for rowan".
- The red tar tentacles, at sea (2026-10-19), the kid's (his answers in his tab "monkey and tentacles for rowan": on "all" the tar, "tar tentacles that do 4dm and 2hp"). `entities/tartentacles.ts` (drawn by render/tartentacles.ts): out on the tar ocean, while the chemistry boat is under way (40 or faster), one comes up in its way every 12 s or so (9.6 to 14.4), 260 ahead (give or take a fifth) and up to 120 to either side, never within the eel's calm of the tar dock or the island's jetty or close round the tar island, two at most. First the tar bubbles inside a red ring for 1.2 s, then a red tentacle rises, thick and tapering, pale suckers along it, tar dripping off, swaying in a ring of ripples. A boat within 110 it rears back over for 0.9 s while a red ring 46 across shows on the tar where the boat is, then slaps down there: 4 off the boat's health (its bar shows), only if the boat is still in the ring (at most sailing speed it is out of it well inside the 0.9 s). It can slap again after 1.4 s, and sinks back after 10 s up. Two harpoons sink it for good, 50 coins: the harpoon (the boat's since mission 2) aims at the eel first, then the nearest tentacle up, its two pips over it. None rise for a boat sitting still, so fishing for tar fish stays fishing. Slapped to nothing, the boat is dragged under and comes up at the nearest dock, whole, as with the eel. Not yet: on foot in island 4's tar, where 4 would be four hearts (asked of the owner).
- The tar kraken, asked (2026-10-20), the kid's: given four ideas in his tab "tar kraken ideas for rowan", he picked "mix": the first (the boss of the whole tar ocean, its arms coming up out of the tar, "yes good idea but not red": its arms are not the red tentacles), the third (the evil monkey in the cage made the tar spread and woke it; "yes good idea") and the fourth (so huge you never see all of it, one giant eye in the tar and tentacles all round the boat; harpoon the eye when it opens; "ok"); not the second, its rising beside the tar island once its bosses are beaten ("no evantraley their will be more tar islandes"). Nine hits and 10 damage to the boat (the chemistry boat has 100); fought "both", from the boat and on foot; his prize a tentacle and 900 coins. The boat fight is built (next entry). How much it does to the figure on foot, the 10 being the boat's, is the owner's to say.
- The tar kraken, part one: the boat fight (2026-10-21), the kid's (his answers above). It lies under the middle of the tar ocean (`entities/tarkraken.ts` LAIR, (2400, -13200), far from the tar dock and the tar island), awake once the tar eel is beaten and the monkey caged (eelStage 2), and not yet beaten (krakenBeaten in the save). Sail the chemistry boat within 700 of it and the tar boils: its arms come up round the boat 170 off, two at a time, a new one every 2.2 s or so, each bubbling 1 s first. An arm up rears back 1 s while a red ring 104 across shows where the boat was, then slams there: 10 off the boat's 100 if it is still in the ring, nothing if it sailed out. Up 11 s and it sinks back on its own. The harpoon (the eel's, range 360, 1.2 s reload) chops an arm in one, and it sinks. Chop ten and the kraken itself comes up, really mad: the tar heaves 2.4 s and one giant eye rises out of it 230 ahead of the boat (a glossy black dome, its lid sliding open on a yellow eye whose slit pupil follows the boat), and eight of its arms stand up all round the boat 190 off. These are not to be chopped: in turns, one every 2 s, the nearest slams at the boat with its red ring. The eye stays shut 2.4 s, opens for 2.2 s (a ring and its nine pips over it then), and shuts; a harpoon reaches it only while it is open. Nine in the eye and it shuts for good and sinks, its arms going limp, and one arm is left floating on the tar: 900 coins and a new trophy-hall stand, the kraken's arm (24 stands then; 25 with round two's). Sail off more than 1,400 from its middle, or go ashore, and it sinks back down whole, to start over; pulled under by it (its slams taking the boat's health to none), the boat comes up at the nearer of the tar dock and the tar island's jetty like the eel's, and the kraken starts over. While it is up the red tar tentacles keep off and the boat's health bar shows. The tester has a step for it. Its arms are tar, the kid's colour for them (2026-10-22, his answer in his tab "kraken for rowan"; a deep purple stood in until then): black and glossy as the tar folk, with their pale rim and a sheen down one side so they show on the tar, and pale suckers (render/tarkraken.ts); the arm left floating and the trophy hall's coil match. The on-foot fight ("both") is round two, next entry. Asked where, he wrote "Tar iland 13 DOOM ISLAND"; read at first as Doom Island (now the Endless Isle) being a tar island, but the owner says it is not (2026-10-22), and asked again in his tab "doom island for rowan" he said yes, it is the tar island. On foot, its blows take 3 hearts, the owner's (2026-10-21, as Skrill's).
- The tar kraken, round two: on foot (2026-10-23). Beaten at sea, the kraken ends for good there, and the kid (likely past it already) would never have seen it on foot; so the owner chose round two (2026-10-23, of three ways put to her): once it is beaten at sea, it comes back for the tar island. The same entity on other ground (`entities/tarkraken.ts` ISLE_GROUND, ISLE_LAIR, ASHORE; the boat fight is SEA_GROUND, unchanged). Go ashore on the tar island and the tar all round it boils; its arms come up out of the tar 26 off the shore nearest you, within 0.45 rad either side of you, two at a time, never over the jetty or the boat tied up beside it. Each rears back with a red ring 32 across where you stood, a figure's width, and slams the beach: 3 hearts if you are still in it (KRAKEN_HEARTS). An arm standing in front of you on the screen is drawn see-through, so you can watch your feet and its ring. A spear or the knife chops an arm in one; chop six and its eye rises out of the tar 64 off the shore nearest you, within the first spear's throw of the beach, with five of its arms up along the shore either side of it, slamming at you in turns. The eye opens and shuts as at sea; nine hits in it while it is open, whatever hits them (the kid's nine), and it shuts for good and sinks for the very last time: 900 coins and the trophy hall's 25th stand, the kraken's eye (a glossy black dome, its lid nearly shut on a sliver of yellow). Back aboard, it sinks back down whole to start over; bonked out on the island, you wake aboard as ever and it sinks back too. The save gets `krakenFoot`. Thrown spears now show in flight on the tar island, which they did not before. The sea win's message now ends: But it is not finished with you...
- Skull Island, part one: the place (2026-10-19), island 12, the kid's (his sixth list: Skull Island, Skrill's island; his answers in his tab "skull island for rowan": "at bottom of white buyes", "Shaped like skull its SUPER SUPER DUPER GINORMAS", reached "both" ways). The owner's call: shape it like a skull. `world/isle12.ts` lays it out in its own frame (u across the screen, v down it) in the far far sea's bottom corner, just past where the white buoys meet at the bottom of the map: a whole island of bone-white rock, a skull lying face up looking at the sky. Its cranium is 1,900 across and the skull 2,170 from the crown to the chin: 1.4 times island 11 across, 1.7 times its length, 2.4 times its area, the biggest island yet. The corner holds no more: bigger and it crosses the white buoys. Because the view halves what runs down the screen, its eye sockets are twice as long in v as they are wide, so they look round. Two eye sockets, the nose and the mouth: the sockets and nose are bottomless pits with a red glow far down each eye, no ground in them. Across the mouth are two rows of huge teeth with the front ones gone in both rows, a gap-toothed grin; the gap is the way up from the pier, straight up the middle. Off its chin runs a pier 260 long, with crates at its root where the hold is sold, and its dock past the end. On its crown stands Skrill's tower: black stone, five floors of purple-lit windows, a skull over the door. The door is shut for now: at it, from high up inside, "hehe ha ha mwa ha ha!" (his words). Sighted from 900 off its shore, it says so ("Land ho: Skull Island!"). The first time, the camera pulls right back for 5.5 s until the whole skull is on the screen, then comes back in, because from the boat only a stretch of its shore ever shows. On the sea map it is drawn in its own shape, a little skull with three dark pits (MapIsle's new outline and pits), not a round island. Its arrow (a little skull, pulsing until it is found) shows in the far far sea once island 11 is found. The eye socket and the tower each say what they are once a landing. The log gets "Skull Island found". The save gets isle12Seen (missing in old saves: not found). Skrill and his men, the displacer beasts and the portal come next, in parts.
- Skull Island, part two: Skrill's men (2026-10-19), the kid's (his sixth list: "men in skeleton armour", 18 hits and 2 damage, the skeleton guy in his second drawing with a bone weapon; his answers: Skull Island's bad guys, their bone weapons are jawblades, and Skrill "yells at his men"). `entities/bonemen.ts` (drawn by render/bonemen.ts): eight men in armour made of bones over dark leathers (a skull for a helmet with a red glint in its eyes, a rib cage, bone shoulder pads and shin guards), each with a jawblade, a great curved jawbone with teeth along its inner edge. They stand guard in pairs, in the skull's own frame: on the jaw above the pier either side of the way up, out on the left cheek, up on the dome, and either side of Skrill's tower door. Come within 150 of one and he raises his jawblade (0.6 s), his partner with him (within 220), and they come on at 50 (the figure walks at 85); led more than 420 from his post, or the figure gone, he walks back. Within 40 he draws the jawblade back over his shoulder for 0.85 s while a red fan shows on the ground in front of him (48 out, 63 degrees either side of the figure), then sweeps it across: 2 hearts to anyone in the fan, a companion too; he's open 1 s after. A stun stops a sweep being drawn back. Eighteen hits and his armour falls apart into a heap of bones with the skull on top, and 70 coins; the heap stays till the next landing, when all eight are back at their posts, whole. The first pair to come on says what they are, and Skrill yells from his tower ("GET THEM, you lazy bags of bones!"), and again, shorter, on each landing after. The spear, the knife and the companions all go for them, the scanner knows them (one of Skrill's men: eighteen hits, the red fan, slow), and hearts show ashore on Skull Island. In the tests a figure that steps straight back a third of a second after the fan shows always gets out of it.
- Skull Island, part three: the displacer beasts (2026-10-19), the kid's (his answers: Skrill "can keep displaser bestes (4hp&3dm)", the ones from Dungeons and Dragons, and "displaseres are EVRYWHARE"). `entities/displacers.ts` (drawn by render/displacers.ts): eight great cats in a blue-black coat with a violet sheen, six legs, green eyes, and two long tentacles off the shoulders, each ending in a pad of bony spikes. Their trick is their name, as in the kid's game: the figure sees a picture of one standing 38 to one side of where it really is, drifting slowly round it, drawn a shade see-through; where it really is there is only its shadow and a shimmer in the air. They prowl slow loops round their dens, in the skull's own frame: up on the dome either side of the tower, out on both cheeks, on the brow between the eyes, low on the right cheek, and either side of the jaw, each at least 200 from a post of Skrill's men and well away from the landing. Within 170 one snarls (0.5 s) and stalks after the figure at 70; led more than 460 from its den, or the figure gone, it goes back. Within 56 it rears its tentacles back for 0.9 s while a red wedge shows on the ground out from where it really is (66 out, 34 degrees either side), then lashes them out: 3 hearts to anyone in the wedge, a companion too. Lashing shakes its trick loose: for the 1.4 s after, its picture is drawn in onto it, and a hit lands; a hit keeps it showing for 2 s more. The rest of the time a spear, the knife or a companion's blow goes straight through the picture ("Not there!"), and the first time the game says why: its shadow shows where it is, and it can be hit just after it lashes. The spear aims at the picture, which is what the figure sees, and the companions only go for one while it shows where it is. Four hits and it goes down, and 60 coins; the Honor Guard's knife beats one in a single hit, if it lands. All eight are back each landing, prowling, whole. The scanner knows them (four hits, never where it looks, watch its shadow, hit it just after it lashes). In the tests a figure that steps straight back a third of a second after the wedge shows always gets out of it.
- Skull Island, part four: Skrill's tower, its four floors (2026-10-19), the kid's ("5 flores and displaseres are EVRYWHARE"; the questions about what is on each floor went to him in his tab "Skrill for rowan"). The tower's door on the skull's crown now stands open: Climb the tower. Inside are four rooms of black stone lit purple (world/tower.ts SKULL_FLOORS, kind 'skull', each 170 across, 40 more than the other towers' floors), with a skull carved high on the back wall, purple light in its eyes, and old bones about the floor (render/skulltower.ts). `world/skulltower.ts` says who holds each floor: two of Skrill's men by the stairs and a beast on the first, three beasts on the second, two men and two beasts on the third, two men and three beasts on the last. They are the same men and beasts as outside (entities/bonemen.ts and entities/displacers.ts, a lot of each per floor), awake only while the figure is on their floor, all back whole each time the tower is entered. Beat everyone on a floor and its stairs open ("The floor is yours"), and the hearts fill on the stairs as in every tower. At the back of the last floor, where the stairs would be, is Skrill's own door, black iron with a bone skull on it and purple light under it, shut fast; walk up to it and he laughs behind it ("hehe ha ha mwa ha ha! Not yet, little fisher!"). Skrill himself, with the orb and the five monkeys, comes next. On the floors, Skrill yells from upstairs at his men instead of from his tower. The spear, the knife, the companions and the scanner treat them as they do outside.
- Skull Island, part five: Skrill (2026-10-20), the kid's (his sixth list's first drawing, horned and winged, "The spell worked!", the same orb as island 9, the monkeys; 20 hits, 3 damage, "hehe ha ha mwa ha ha"; his answers in his tab "Skrill for rowan": floors 1 to 4 his men and beasts and Skrill on the fifth with the orb and five monkeys, "yes"; he fights with "all and sword", his wings, spells from the orb and a sword, and the spell "it a portal"; the men from the portal shouting "GET SKRILL AND PRIZON HIM" are "THE herons men"; the portal opens "When it starts"; his prize "His horn"; and what else takes you to Skull Island is "Island 13 DO NOT ASK YET", so it is not asked). With the fourth floor beaten, Skrill's own door at its back creaks open (drawn open, stairs going up into a red night) and says so, laughing: Through Skrill's door goes up to the fifth floor, the open top of his tower (world/tower.ts SKRILL_TOP, kind 'skrill', 210 across; render/skrill.ts): a dark red sky with a red moon, black stone with a bone skull set in the middle, battlements with little bone skulls along them, and his throne of bones at the back. `entities/skrill.ts`: he waits by his throne with his five monkeys (a camp that does not come back), and when the figure steps in off the top step he laughs, raising his orb (the long first message says how he fights), and his monkeys come. Round and round: on foot he walks at the figure (46; the figure 85) and draws back his bone sword while a red fan shows where it sweeps (from 48, out to 62, 0.9 s of warning); after two swings, or six seconds on his feet, he takes to his wings, up out of a spear's reach (100 high; a spear reaches under 34), hangs off the figure on the far side, raises the orb while three red rings show where its bolts fall (one on the figure, two round it 62 off, 1 s of warning), then points his sword down a red lane through the figure and swoops along it, landing past it on his floor, where he gets his breath for 2 s, open. Every blow is three hearts, and when one lands he laughs ("hehe ha ha mwa ha ha!" over his head, every 4 s at most). A spear (or the knife) reaches him only down low, on his feet or landed ('Too high!' otherwise); companions fight him then and are caught by his blows; a stun stops his sword being drawn back or holds him down a moment longer. Beaten after twenty hits, he crashes down onto his floor with a horn snapped off beside him: 8,000 coins, hearts full, his monkeys run for it, and your flag flies over his tower. He stays beaten (skrillBeaten in the save; an old save has not): back up there, his throne stands empty. His horn is the trophy hall's twenty-third stand (`data/trophies.ts` skrillhorn), the scanner knows him (up on his wings too), the orb's 'And more' points to his tower until he is beaten and after that says it has seen nothing more yet, and the tester panel has a step for him. Next, from the same answers: the portal opening as the fight starts, "The spell worked!", and the Heron's men coming through it to get Skrill and put him in prison.
- Skull Island, part six: Skrill's spell (2026-10-20), the kid's (his answers: the spell is a portal, "it a portal", it opens "When it starts", and the men out of it screaming "GET SKRILL AND PRIZON HIM" are "THE herons men"). His spell works, and backfires. As Skrill wakes he raises his orb, "The spell worked! hehe ha ha mwa ha ha!", and a portal tears open on the left of his floor (`entities/prisonparty.ts`, drawn by render/prisonparty.ts: standing on its edge, a ring of purple fire round a turning dark middle, opening and shutting over 1.2 s). Out of it come the Heron's hired men, in his midnight blue with their clubs (drawn as his hired men are), one every 3 s, four at most at a time, the first shouting "GET SKRILL AND PRIZON HIM!" (the rest "GET SKRILL!"). They are after him, not the figure, and nothing they do hurts it; nor can it spear them. They run at him (62); while he is up on his wings they wait under him, shaking their clubs; down on his floor, the first to reach him grabs him ("Got him!") and holds him 0.8 s, a stun, a chance for the spear, until he throws them off. His own blows knock them flat for 1.4 s ("Oof!"), his sword, his bolts and his swoop alike, and up again they go straight back at him. Their grabs do not take his hits: his twenty are the figure's to land (my reading, put to the owner). Beaten, he crashes down, and they have him: "GOT HIM! TO PRIZON!" Two take hold and drag him along his floor (44) to the portal and through it, and it shuts behind them; his snapped horn stays where he fell (Skrill.hornAt), for the trophy hall. Back up his tower afterwards, all is shut and quiet.
- The Battle Barge at sea's camera (2026-10-16), the owner's, with a phone screenshot ("At sea battle barge seems to have same issue"): stood back a little and leaning toward the nearest boss, it still kept the boat in the middle, a Shark King half off the side and the bottom of the screen empty water. It now frames the fight (`data/barge.ts` seaFrame): the boat with its net, and every boss still in the fight within 1,100 of it (each with how far round it reaches and how tall it stands, Star where it flies; one up in space or beaten is let go), as a box on the screen; the camera on the box's middle, and the zoom that fits it into the band between the top bar and the buttons (0.86 of the width, 0.44 of the height, its middle 0.015 of the height above the screen's), never out past 0.45 of the sea's zoom nor in past 0.9, easing as bosses come and go. A boss further off is left out (the radar shows it), so the large ring never shrinks everything to specks. And out at the ring, 'Let a crow go' and the radar's arrow to the day's treasure no longer show.
- Messages and the camera ashore (2026-10-16), the owner's, with five phone screenshots on island 10 ("The text covers the action all the time... and when on land I think the camera needs some adjusting so player is closer to bottom of screen same as battle barge seems"): with the bubbles down the top right grown tall (hearts, snacks, the hull, the catch, the time, wood, junk), the message under them came down a third of the screen, right where the figure stood in the middle. Now ashore the camera keeps the figure 0.12 of the screen below the middle (`entities/walker.ts` WALK_DROP; not on the Battle Barge, whose own framing stands), and when the right-hand column stands more than 60 taller than the order and the charter on the left, the message goes beside it on the left under the order (main.js placeToast), left-aligned and as wide as the gap allows (at least 160, at most 360); otherwise it is centred under the bubbles, as before.
- Messages sent away (2026-10-17), the owner's ("I wonder if they should be at the bottom center and be swipeable"; on keeping them at the top, beside a tall column, and adding the dismissing: "either works"). A message showing takes a tap or a flick: tapped, it fades where it is; dragged, it follows the finger, and let go after 40 or more it slides off the way it was flicked (less, and it goes back and stays). The next message waiting may then show, and the same message keeps away 8 seconds, so one the game says again and again does not spring straight back (`ui/toast.ts` dismiss, QUIET). A press on a message does not steer; while a cutscene hides the messages they take no taps.
- The muffin man (2026-10-13), the owner's, in her words: "Cozy games live on NPCs like that, the ones that are completely useless and that everyone remembers." A little bakery stall stands on the home pier's far edge, between its root and the crates (entities/muffinman.ts, drawn by render/bakery.ts with the pier, which it stands on): a pink counter with MUFFINS painted along its front and a row of muffins on top, some with a fish's tail sticking out, under a pink and cream striped awning on four posts. Behind the counter is the baker, round, in a white coat, with rosy cheeks, a big curly moustache and a white baker's hat; he rocks gently from foot to foot. On the sand by the pier's root, along the beach from the stall so the awning never hides it, a street sign on a post says DRURY LANE in black on white, with WC2 in red, as London's street signs do. Ashore, walk up and a little "..." bubbles over him; tap him (or his words) from within 60 and he hops, holds up a muffin and says the next line of his loop in a speech bubble over his head, with a sing-song of notes, up at the end of a question and down, proudly, for the answer: "Do you know the muffin man?" "I AM the muffin man." "Do you know him?" And round again, for ever; it never resolves. From further off a toast says to walk up to the baker. A tap on the dog pottering in front of his counter still pets the dog. The figure walks round the stall and the sign's post. He is there from the start.
- Junk and fish muffins (2026-10-13), the muffin man's trade, on the owner's calls (junk, not sardines; a muffin gives back a heart). Each fish the towed net takes has a one in twenty chance (data/junk.ts) of bringing up a piece of junk with it: an old boot, a tin can, seaweed or a soggy sock, drawn by render/junk.ts as it flies aboard after the fish, with its name over the net. Junk goes in a bucket on deck, not the hold; the dock does not buy it. The bucket holds 12; past that, junk stays in the sea. A chip with a boot on it, under the driftwood, shows how much is aboard, and only when there is some (hidden in rooms, diving and on the ice). The first piece into an empty bucket says the baker on the pier wants it. Tap the muffin man with junk aboard and, as well as his line, he takes a piece for each muffin there is room for in the box and bakes them: fish muffins, a box of six at most, flying from his counter to the figure. A toast says how many and how to eat one; with the box full he says to eat one first. The box is a pink-ringed chip by the hearts, with a muffin on it (a fish tail sticking out of the top) and how many. Tap it to eat one, the oldest first: a heart back, and a crunchy munch (Nom! +1 heart). With the hearts full it says to keep it for a fight. Hearts come back on their own once a fight is over (one every 6 s ashore, all at once aboard), so a muffin is a snack for the middle of one. The tester panel can fill the bucket. Junk and the box are saved (junk, muffins; each muffin the game day it was baked, for what happens to one left uneaten). 
- Leggy muffins (2026-10-13), the owner's: fish muffins "which if uneaten, grow legs and start following you around, like a bad sock with little cloud of stink above them like pigpen", six at most ("an unholy half dozen"). A muffin left in the box a whole game day after it was baked (five minutes of play; day and clock only ever go forward, through sleep too) grows legs and hops out, oldest first, with a squeak and a toast (the first: it follows you about now, Pee-yew). The trade's toast warns not to leave them too long. On foot, anywhere the figure walks, they trot after it in a line like ducklings (entities/leggy.ts): the first 26 behind and the rest 13 apart, along the way the figure actually went, a point every 3, so nothing on an island strands them and they never cut a corner; through a door they come along at once. Each is a muffin on two thin stepping legs with little feet and two dot eyes on its cup, under its own cloud of stink, like Pigpen's: wavy green lines rising and fading, and two flies buzzing round (render/leggy.ts). A tap right on one makes it squeak and hop; they cannot be eaten. Aboard, they ride in a stink crate on the foredeck, across from where the egg rode (aft, the cabin would hide it, and Neil has the stern): their tops peek over the slats, legs dangle over the side, and one bigger cloud hangs over it. When a seventh grows legs, the oldest wanders off into the sea (Splash!), and the count stays six. The tester panel can add a muffin about to grow legs, or all six. The count is saved (leggy).
- Island 7 (2026-10-08), the kid's last island before Gigantis: in his words, the same monkeys and sorcerer as island 2, but with an alien portal. Out in the far deep straight down the map from home (bottom left on screen), radius 300, island 2 again a little bigger: a beach toward home where the boat runs its bow up onto the sand, with a dock ring that sells the hold (the fish fly to a trading post with a purple roof) and a dock camera that frames the landing; palms; an eight-sided tower in the middle, taller than island 2's (176), its slit windows purple at night; a monkey camp on the far side, three huts round a fire with a totem and a chest, six monkeys of whom two throw coconuts, the island 2 camp's rules and callbacks, its chest 800 coins when beaten; and on the side toward the viewer, on a patch of burnt grass, the portal: a heavy ring of dark plated metal standing upright on two struts, facing the viewer, with three fins along its top and ten lights set in it. It is dead for now, its lights dim with the odd flicker; it wakes when the sorcerer is beaten on the tower's roof (the next entry), lights chasing green round it and a turning green and violet light inside, glowing night and day. The figure can walk right through it. A lavender marker points to its dock from past the buoys once Cthulhu is beaten, pulsing until it is found; the first time the boat comes within 700 of its shore it is announced and logged (isle7Seen). Hulls are kept off its sand.
- Island 7's tower and the sorcerer a third time (2026-10-08): in at the door on its face toward the viewer, two floors of monkeys (five with one thrower, then six with two), then the roof, where the leviathan sorcerer waits a third time, with island 3's ring of bolts and a new trick: twice as tough (16), and once angry it calls monkeys up onto the roof, two at a time (one throwing coconuts), at once and every 9 s, while fewer than two are up. They are speared or fought like any; the warlock, the cat and the warrior go for them too. In the tests a person alone who strafes and throws a third of a second late wins about two times in three with the barbed spear or the harpoon, and loses most fights if a beat slower; with the healer cat's two heals, which anyone this far has, every time. Beaten, it pays 2,000 (500 after), the player's flag flies from the tower, and the leave button reads Down to the portal: out at the tower's door, the portal wakes over two and a half seconds, lights chasing green and a turning green and violet light filling the ring (a little see-through, so nobody behind it is lost), and it glows night and day (isle7Stage 1). Walk into it and Into the portal takes the figure to Gigantis (the next entry).
- Gigantis (2026-10-08), the kid's castle standing on the sea, through island 7's awake portal: a courtyard inside its gate, open to a green sky with a pale moon and the sea far below its battlements, the portal home turning at its side; then two halls of dark stone with green-burning torches and tall windows onto the green sky. Each room is full of the kid's undead (entities/undead.ts), every hit one heart as he wrote: skeletons with a rusty sword and a little round shield (4; they raise the sword for half a second, then slash; the shield takes the first spear, once, and breaks), archer skeletons that keep about 115 off and loose a bone arrow every 2.8 s at where the figure will be if it keeps going (190 a second, aimed to meet it, so turn when one flies; they cannot block), zombies (8; slow, 32, arms up for 0.7 s before they swing), ghosts (3; float about 100 off and send a wisp every 3 s that drifts after the figure at 70, slower than a walk, so walk away from it) and necromancers (6; keep about 120 off and raise a skeleton out of a green-lit crack in the floor between them and the figure, one at a time, another 7 s after the last is beaten; it falls with them). The courtyard has three skeletons and two archers, the first hall three zombies and two ghosts, the second two necromancers, a skeleton and two archers. Beat everyone in a room and the heavy door at its back is thrown open onto green light: Through the door fills the hearts, as the tower's stairs do. The thrown spear goes for a necromancer in reach first, then the nearest, and the warlock, the cat and the warrior fight them too, getting hurt by what would hurt the figure. The second hall's great door opens on the throne room (the next entry). Back through the portal is the way home at any time; bonked out, the figure wakes aboard. Nothing is saved: the castle is whole again next time. In the tests a person who circles, turns when a shot flies and steps back from a raised sword a quarter of a second later, and throws a third of a second late, wins every room for about half a heart; one a beat slower wins most, the last hall about two times in three alone; with the healer cat, nearly always.
- The Forgotten One (2026-10-08), the kid's king of Gigantis, in its throne room behind three necromancers, each raising a skeleton as in the hall. Drawn as the kid drew him (2026-10-09): long black hair, a pale face screaming, a black cloak with a ragged hem over long thin legs, and a sword with a cross-guard; 36 to beat (entities/forgotten.ts). He comes on slowly (38) and hurts three ways, a heart each, as the kid wrote: close in, his sword (up for 0.6 s, then swept round in front); further off, by turns every 2.6 s, skulls, blue and trailing purple fire, five flung in a fan with the middle one aimed to meet the figure where it is going (find a gap, or turn), and the death ray, a thin orange line from him toward the figure for 0.75 s (his eyes burn orange while it shows) and then a beam burning along it, which does not follow: step off the line. Hurt to half, every 1.9 s and seven skulls. Beaten, he falls apart and his bones get up, his long black hair still on the skull, on your side: the next entry. The spear goes for a necromancer first, then whichever is nearer of him and the rest. The warlock calls merlocks out of a puddle of sea while the fight is on (entities/allies.ts): two fish-men, three hit points each, chopping the nearest of the dead for 1 (drawn since 2026-10-09 after the kid's picture of one: hunched and green, yellow down the front, a huge round head that is mostly teeth, one big red eye, red spines fanning off the crown, red hands and feet, and a great serrated bone blade held up overhead; the owner keeps the name merlock), and two more 12 s after both are gone; what would hurt the figure hurts them. Beat everyone: 5,000 coins, hearts full, and isle7Stage 2; his throne stays empty after, while the halls fill again each visit. Behind the throne is a door in the back wall, chained, with a padlock as big as a head: the room behind the throne that the kid left for next time. In the tests a person who circles, finds the gaps, steps off the line and back from the sword a quarter of a second later, and throws a third of a second late wins nearly every time, alone or not, in about half a minute; one a beat slower wins most times with the healer cat, and about half alone. The warlock, the warrior, the merlocks and the tarling make it easier still in the game.
- The Forgotten One's bones (2026-10-09), the kid's: in his words, "upon death of the Forgotten One he drops a skeleton that summons one ranged ghost and one skeleton melee, 2 damage, 4 hp; he is a companion." (Built first, by misreading, as one more enemy.) When the Forgotten One falls, his bones get up where he fell and kneel to you, and from then on (isle7Stage 2, so nothing new is saved) they come along as the cat and the warrior do, riding at the bow aboard (entities/pals.ts, kind 'bones'): four hit points, his long black hair on the skull, green where his eyes were red, his sword with its cross-guard, a swing for 2 every 1.5 s. In any fight they raise a ghost and a skeleton of their own out of a green-lit crack (entities/allies.ts, with the warlock's merlocks): the skeleton slashes close in, the ghost keeps 80 off and sends a wisp, each for 1 every 1.3 or 2.4 s, three and two hit points, with a green ring at their feet so they are not taken for the castle's dead. They crumble back after 6 s with nothing to fight, and a new pair comes 12 s after the last is gone. The throne fight without the old enemy bones, in the tests: a sharp person wins 30 in 30 with the cat and 28 alone, in about 25 s; a beat slower, 23 with the cat and 14 alone.
- The ruins behind the throne and the lancers (2026-10-09), the kid's next room: "you see a room behind the throne, he gives you the key and you walk in; there you see the lancets, 10 hp, slow, 2 damage, they DO NOT engage you, and ruins." Walk the Forgotten One's bones to the locked door behind his throne and they give you his key: the padlock falls open, the chains drop (isle7Stage 3), and Through the door leads to the ruins (world/tower.ts RUINS; world/gigantis.ts for its pool, columns and posts; render/gigantis.ts drawRuinsBack and drawColumn). A round room open to the green sky, its walls broken down to stumps and gone in two places, two green torches, rubble, five snapped columns in the way, and a black pool in the middle that nothing walks into: where the Old One will rise. The kid's "lancets" are read as lancers (entities/lancers.ts): three tall knights in green-black armour with lances and ragged pennants, standing guard facing in. They leave the figure alone and nobody on its side goes for them; the spear only goes for one when there is nothing else to throw at, and that rouses it. Roused, one walks at 30, draws its lance back for 0.9 s (the warning) and thrusts, two hearts within 46 in front of it, then is open 1.2 s; ten hits to put one down. Every visit they stand at their posts again, whole and quiet.
- The Old One and soul armour (2026-10-09), the kid's boss of the ruins: "you see the old one rise, he wants you dead. Cutscene: your skeleton charges at him, he knocks him away and roars at you. You get soul armour, heals you when you hit an enemy. Boss level: now he summons 5 creatures from the black lagoon, all 2 hp and 1 damage." (Named generically in the game: lagoon creatures.) Into the ruins before he is beaten, the figure stands while it plays: the black pool boils, he rises out of it (entities/oldone.ts) enormous and stays in it from the waist up, black and running with water, branching horns, a ring of pale eyes, a maw; the bones run at him and are knocked flying (back to the boat to rest, Pal.knockOut); he roars; and the skeleton's soul stays with the figure as soul armour (soulArmour in the save, a pale shield in the hearts with three dots: every third enemy the spear hits gives a heart back). Then the fight: five lagoon creatures climb out of the pool (a 'lagoon' kind of the undead's Horde: 2 hit points, 66 a second, a claw for a heart after 0.5 s, no block), five more every 9 s while fewer than four stand; his hand comes down anywhere in the ruins every 3 s on a ring where the figure stood (1 s warning, a heart; it does not follow). The spear goes for a creature within 80, else him, else any creature, and never rouses a lancer while he is up; 50 to beat. Beaten, he sinks with his creatures, 8,000 coins, hearts full, isle7Stage 4, and after that the ruins are quiet and the pool still. Seen again before he is beaten, the scene is short: he rises, roars, and the fight is on. In the tests, alone and without helpers: a sharp person wins every time in about 42 s; a beat slower, every time in soul armour in about 90 s, and 24 in 30 without it. The helpers make it easier still in the game.
- Island 2 (2026-10-07), the first island past the leviathans: in the far corner of the deep opposite the Cthuluviathan's city, 600 past both lines of buoys, straight across the screen from home and 4,200 from it, so only the flagship reaches it. Radius 230, with its own shallows, palms, a trading post with a green roof and a fish over the door, and an eight-sided stone tower in the middle that nobody has climbed yet, whose top windows show a purple light at night. Its dock ring faces home; entering it sells the hold as at home (the fish fly to the trading post) and opens the shop, and the dock camera frames island 2. Step ashore and the boat runs its bow up onto the sand and the figure walks the island; the dog stays home. Three schools of parrotfish ring it, 400 to 460 from its centre, built from their own seed. While the flagship is past the buoys a green marker at the screen's edge points to its dock, pulsing until it is found; the first time the boat comes within 700 of its shore it is announced and logged (isle2Seen). The anglerfish keeps 450 off its shore.
- 36 schools in rings by value. Fish respawn 8–17 s after being caught (plus 1.5 s per species index). Schools visibly thin.
- Ambience (2026-09-17): waves that breathe and hiss with speed, a calm pad rooted on F that warms at dawn and dusk and thins at night, wind chimes within 400 units of the shore or pier, gulls crying from birds that are around and awake, and dolphins chirping when a pod is within 350 units. All procedural on one bus that ducks 40% under every cue for half a second, behind the sound toggle. Levels are a first guess and want ears.
- Day cycle, 300 s: dawn (12%), day (43%), dusk (12%), night (33%). Night is a multiply mask with light-shaped holes (boat, net, hut, crates, treehouse, driftwood, salvage, glowing schools). Dawn and dusk tint warm.

**Creatures and hazards**
- Seven day species, two night-only glowing species, sharks, two sparkle rares. Table below.
- Gulls circle schools that still have fish (3 = full, 2 = thinning, 0 = fished out), trail the boat as the hold fills, sleep at night. Two perch on the island.
- Two dolphin pods roam. Pass one at speed and it escorts you for up to 30 s; while escorted, sharks will not charge.
- Six sharks orbit tuna, goldfin and lanternfish schools. They charge a moving net within 330 u. Net level < 4: net torn for 6 s, up to 3 fish spilled. Net level ≥ 4: the shark is caught (40 coins).
- One pirate, unlocked at 60 lifetime coins. Never follows a boat past the buoys: the deep is the leviathan's. Prowls, chases when you carry ≥ 4 fish, steals half the hold (highest species index first), leaves, returns in ~30–50 s. Never comes within ~400 u of the dock. Slightly faster than a level-1 engine.
- Manta rays: a squadron glides the middle rings in a V and, with the boat near, one leaps now and then and lands with a whump. The net slides off them. A sighting is logged.
- Whales: a mother and calf circle the far water, surface to breathe with a spout, and sing when in earshot (further at night). Not catchable. A sighting is logged.
- Leviathan: a 28-segment serpent shadow on a squircle path ~2,180 u out. Spines surface, spots glow at night. Passing within 300 u rumbles the screen and logs a sighting. Not catchable yet. It guards the buoys (2026-09-28): the moment a flagship crosses them, either way, it dives off its path and rises across the boat's way, 1.6 s of sailing ahead (380 to 700 u), spines first over 1.1 s with a growl, lit at night. It stays up while the boat keeps coming (up to 9 s) and sinks 2.4 s after the boat turns away or stops; then it will not rise again for 20 s, so the way is open. While it is up it is a wall: the first touch knocks a third of the hold overboard, most valuable first, shoves the boat back to its own side and stops it; after that it only holds the boat there. Never the boat, never coins.
- The Cthuluviathan (2026-10-06), the kid's leviathan, named by him, and drawn by him (2026-10-09): a green octopus head with two bat wings up on top, yellow-green eyes and a beard of curling tentacles, asleep in a sunken city of broken columns in one corner of the deep, 650 past both lines of buoys. Go slowly and it dreams on, snoring, with Zs rising. A boat within 750 going faster than 170 wakes it, and it stirs as a warning above 120; any boat within 360 wakes it. Awake, its eyes open yellow-green and every 0.9 s it sends a tentacle up where the boat will be in a second, within 1,000 of its city; the water boils there for that second first, so the counter is to steer off the bubbles. While a tentacle stands, its arm shows as a long shadow under the water all the way back to the head, as he drew it reaching for the boat. A tentacle that catches the boat takes a fifth of the hold, most valuable first, and slows the boat; never the boat, never coins; it cannot grab again for 1.5 s. Out of reach for 5 s, or awake for 30, and it goes back to sleep, soundly for 12 s. Within 1,800 its place is marked at the edge of the screen, a z while it sleeps and an eye awake, because a phone in portrait shows little to either side. In the tests a person steering off the bubbles a third of a second late is never caught, half a second late in one encounter in ten, and one who runs straight for open water without minding the bubbles about once an encounter. A sighting: a toast, a gold chip, a guide page and cthuluSeen in the save.
- The anglerfish (2026-10-06), out in the deep at night: it comes up once it is dark (0.5) and sinks at dawn (0.3), somewhere in the deep clear of the buoys, the deep's end and the Cthuluviathan's city, and drifts at 22 a second. Its lure is a bright bulb on a stalk with ten fish circling it drawn as lanternfish to the life; there are no glowing fish in the deep, which is the tell, with something large and dark beneath. It edges the light toward a boat within 1,000, no nearer than 300. A boat making for the light at more than 60, within 1.7 s of sailing (380 to 600), opens its jaws: a dark mouth ringed with teeth and the bulb flickering red, with a rising creak. They snap shut on the boat the moment it is over them (140 plus the hull), or on nothing if it turns away or 3 s pass. Caught, it swallows the boat whole (since 2026-10-07, the kid's: before, it tore the net and took a quarter of the hold), and the boat is spat out at home without its catch, as for any boat eaten (the Boat health entry). Either way it sinks and stays down 35 s, then comes up elsewhere, at least 1,300 from the boat. It is not marked at the screen's edge: it is a trap, and its light is what it shows you. In the tests a boat holding its course is always caught, and a person turning back or a quarter turn away up to two thirds of a second late never is (a probe caught some at eight tenths). A sighting the first time its jaws open: a toast, a gold chip, a guide page and anglerSeen in the save.
- The gulper (2026-10-07), the kid's leviathan that eats boats: a giant gulper eel, black with a mouth like a pelican's pouch and a pink light at the tip of its tail, which prowls the east side of the deep (180 in from the buoys and the deep's end, 500 short of each corner) deep down at 70 a second. A flagship within 900 in or near its water sets it hunting, with a groan and a toast and a red marker at the screen's edge: it surfaces, mouth widening as it closes, and chases at up to 290 (a flagship does 350) turning at 1.5 a second, slowing to turn so a boat inside its turning circle cannot keep it circling. A bite takes 34 of the boat's health and it lunges straight on for 0.9 s; three bites and it eats the boat. It gives up after 14 s, or when the boat crosses home or leaves its water by 600, and rests 20 s. In the tests a person running from it a third of a second late is bitten three times in two hundred hunts and never eaten; a boat that sits still is eaten nine times in ten. A sighting: a toast, a chip, a guide page and gulperSeen in the save.
- Boat health (2026-10-07), the kid's: 100, shown as a heart and a bar in the right-hand chips whenever the boat is past the buoys or not whole. It only goes down in the deep: the leviathan's hit takes 30, a tentacle 20 and a gulper bite 34, on top of the fish they take. Home water mends 15 a second and a dock (home or island 2) makes it whole. At none, whatever did it swallows the boat: the view closes on it, stays dark a moment and opens again in the home shallows, where the boat is spat out whole with an empty hold and full health. Never coins, upgrades or the boat. The anglerfish swallows the boat whole outright. data/health.ts holds the numbers.

**Progression**
- Boat tier = `floor((net + hold + engine levels) / 3)`, 0–5: dinghy, skiff, cutter, trawler, seiner, flagship. Each tier scales the hull, adds detail (wider cabin → second deck → stern crane), zooms the camera out 2%, unlocks paint, and raises the sailing range.
- Range per tier is a dashed ring; beyond it the boat is pushed back with a toast. The buoys hold every boat but the flagship, whose range runs on into the deep.
- Upgrade level cap = `min(5, 2 + buildStage)`. The base gates the boat.
- Driftwood (22 logs, 65% spawn inside current range, and respawn 18–40 s after pickup with the same bias) builds the treehouse palace in five stages. Each stage adds 15% to all sale prices, and stages 4 and 5 open the vendors below. The dock camera zooms out to frame the island once the boat settles.
- Orders: "12 mackerel pays 40". Filling one pays a bonus and rolls a new one, restricted to species inside your range. The gold edge arrow points to the nearest school of the order species.
- Vendors: the fishmonger (from the watchtower) picks a species at dawn (or on load, if he is open and has never asked) from what the boat can reach, leaning deeper like the orders, and pays double for it that day; the HUD chip and the sign on his stall show the pick. The smokehouse (from the palace dome) pays half again for tuna, goldfin and shark. Both stack with the palace bonus, and fish sold to a vendor fly to its building on the beach south of the pier.
- Pets: the dog comes with the tree platform, potters on the pier between the root and the crates, and when the pirate sails in it runs to the end of the planks and barks for four seconds: the early warning, with a toast at most once a minute. Its shape is a stand-in until the kid's drawing: a charcoal dog with a red collar and a pale muzzle, after the husband took the first, brown one for another box on the pier. Ashore (2026-10-07) it comes to say hello and follows at heel, 18 behind, along the way the figure actually walked, so nothing on the island can strand it; it sits by the figure facing it, barks where it stands if the pirate prowls, and goes back to its planks when the figure boards. A tap on it with the figure within 40 pets it: three hearts rise, it wags hard and yips. Both the owner and her kid went looking for a way to pet it the day they could walk past it, so until the first pet ever a faint heart floats over it while it sits, a toast says how, and the save remembers (petted).
- The shipwright: from cutter up, a Shipwright row in the shop sells one-off gear for coins (the Gear table has the prices): the fine mesh, which lets jellyfish slip through the net, and the strongbox, which holds the pirate to a quarter of the hold. It is the coin sink the endgame lacked, and each piece is the buyable counter to something with teeth. Once all three upgrades are maxed their cards fold away, so the panel is shorter with the shelf than it was without.
- The Ten Cent Bridge: named for a bridge the owner's family crossed to reach the beach. It leaves the island's west shore at 200 degrees, in the gap between two sardine schools, and runs 232 units on pilings and two stone abutments to a little beach with an umbrella and a towel. A painted sign at the beach end gives the toll (the palace tree hides the island end from the camera), and a lamp on the first abutment lights it at night. It is drawn in six parts along the span, each depth-sorted on its own, since one object that long cannot have one depth (sorted round the boat like the pier, it painted itself over the palace tree). Hulls are pushed off the span and the beach as they are off the pier; flotsam, the rare fish and the jellyfish keep clear of both. It is a place for now; the snook comes later.
- The snook: the first fish taken on a line, from a boat tied up south of the Ten Cent Bridge when the light is low, at ten coins a cast. One cast in ten lands one, one in fifty is big enough to keep and hangs on the hut, and the rest end with "The snook is still under the bridge." Hold to pull, let go to ease off: it shakes its head before every run, and a word over the tension bar says Hold or Let go (2026-09-24, after the owner found it could not be caught, twice). The one that was never coming in says so as it goes (2026-09-26). The roadmap entry has the whole of it.
- Salvage crates (10) give instant coins, scaled by tier.
- Catch log chips in the shop; rares get a gold ring; leviathan sighting is logged. Tap the chips or the Field guide card to open the guide: a page per species with where, when, worth, count and the day of the first catch. Days count dawns from 1.
- Ten hull paints, unlocked as the boat grows, and an eleventh, Mermaid scales, that only the mermaids give (2026-10-22); net floats and palace flags match the hull.
- Flags (2026-10-07), the kid's: a Your flag card beside the field guide in the shop opens a designer. Pick a colour (ten), a pattern (plain, stripe, cross, diagonal, halves, border), a second colour and an emblem (none, star, fish, skull, anchor, heart, crown); every tap flies it at once. It replaces the pennant on the boat's mast and on the palace and watchtower poles, and will go up on every tower taken. data/flag.ts turns the four numbers into shapes that both the canvas and the designer's SVG previews draw. Saved as flag; null in old saves, which keep the pennant in the hull's paint until a flag is designed. The first flag, which the designer starts from and the towers fly until one is designed, is gold with a navy fish (2026-10-10; it was red with a gold star, which the owner saw read as a country's flag). A save holding exactly that old first flag, which the designer saved the moment it opened, goes back to none on load, so the new one shows.
- The spear (2026-10-07), the kid's, a fishing tool first: a third card on the shipwright's shelf sells it in levels, once island 2 has been found (before that a new player could spend 500 on a spear with nowhere to use it; the owner hit exactly that in a fresh game, so the card stays hidden until then, and the island 2 sighting says the spear is on sale), the fishing spear (500: reach 95, 0.9 s between throws), the long spear (1,500: 125, 0.7 s) and the barbed spear (3,500: 150, 0.55 s, hits twice as hard, for the monkeys). Ten parrotfish nose slowly round island 2 between 20 and 70 off its sand, coming back 25 s after they are taken. Ashore with a spear, a round Throw button appears at the right edge whenever one is in reach (Space on a keyboard; a tap on the fish works too), and the figure throws: the spear arcs out and always lands, following the fish as it swims, and since the boat is tied up at the trading post the fish is sold on the spot and flies to it. The figure carries the spear over its shoulder and raises its arm to throw. data/spear.ts holds the levels, entities/shallows.ts the fish and entities/spears.ts the throws; the save gained spear.
- The monkey camp (2026-10-07), the kid's evil monkeys in little skull masks: three thatched huts round a fire, a skull on a pole and a chest, on the far side of island 2 from its landing. Five monkeys mill about until the figure comes within 150 of the camp, then three run at it to bonk it and two keep a throw away and lob coconuts at where it stands, a shadow marking where each will land. All real time, never turn-based: they run at 60 and the figure walks at 85, so the fight is to keep moving and throw; they give up past 280 from the camp. Ashore on island 2 the figure has three hearts (shown top right); a bonk or a coconut takes one, knocks it back and leaves it blinking a moment, and at none it wakes up aboard the boat with nothing lost. Hearts come back one every 6 s out of the fight, and all of them aboard. The Throw button prefers a monkey to a fish; two hits beat a monkey (one with the barbed spear), and a beaten one drops its skull mask (kept, and counted: masks in the save, a chip and a guide page) and runs off, back a minute later. Beat all five and the chest opens with 400 coins; the camp fills again 90 s later. In the tests a person with the first spear who backs off from the nearest monkey and throws whenever ready beats the camp without being bonked out at least fifteen times in twenty.
- The tower and its boss (2026-10-07), the kid's mini dungeon: island 2's stone tower is climbed from the inside, real time and never turn-based. Walk to the door on its front face and a green Climb button appears; inside are two stone floors lit by torches, three monkeys on the first and four on the second (two of them throwing coconuts), and the glowing stairs at the back of each floor open once it is beaten. Climbing the stairs refills the hearts. On the roof, under the sky, waits the leviathan sorcerer, the kid's naga-ish chimera: a finned serpent tail, a feathered purple body, two wings and a bird's head with a hooked beak. It has 8 health, flies a ring round the roof throwing slow purple bolts at where the figure stands (so moving is the counter), and every 5 s swoops in to peck, which is when a spear reaches it; a hit on the swoop drives it back. At half health it gets angry: faster, red-eyed, three bolts at a time. A bonk, a coconut, a bolt or a peck each take a heart, and at none the figure wakes aboard with nothing lost, as in the camp. Beaten, it falls in a burst of feathers: 2,000 coins the first time and 500 every time after, the tower is taken (towerTaken), your flag flies from its top for good, and a Back to the boat button takes the figure straight to its boat, the kid's teleport. A Leave button walks out of the tower from any floor. Hearts do not come back while the camp or the sorcerer is fighting. In the tests a person who strafes round the roof and throws whenever the sorcerer is in reach wins 13 in 30 with the first spear, 27 with the long spear and 30 with the barbed spear, so the tower asks for an upgrade or two without walling the first spear off. The floors are rooms in a pocket of the world far from any sea (world/tower.ts), so the walker, the monkeys, the spears and the camera serve unchanged; that is a change from the proposal of stairs up the outside, which would have needed the camera to follow height. The sorcerer's shapes are a stand-in: he is the kid's to draw.
- Plants (2026-10-08), for the kid who wants the game to look richer and more real: both islands now grow flowering bushes (hibiscus red, frangipani cream, bougainvillea pink, plumeria yellow, lavender), patches of small five-petalled flowers in the grass, and tufts of beach grass round the sand and on the beach, and under the water there are meadows of sea grass in the shallows and swaying kelp further out. They are placed once from a seed (world/plants.ts), kept 14 off anything built at the most built the island gets, 34 off the landings and the tower's door and well off the camp's fire, and out of each other's way; none of them stops the figure, since the bushes are knee high and it walks through them, sorted in front or behind. The sea grass and kelp draw under the waves and the fish, the flowers and tufts flat with the island, and the bushes with the solids. Everything sways a little, the kelp slowest. The owner's steer on "more realistic" was noted the night before: richer rather than photographic, since the flat cosy look is what the renderer does well; this is the first step of that.
- Island 3, the sunken island (2026-10-08), the owner's design for the kid's boss fight: an island that went under long ago, in the north corner of the deep (the last empty one, as far from home as island 2 and 6,000 from both it and the Cthuluviathan's city). Its old town lies on the sea floor inside the drowned shore (radius 300): sixteen houses placed once from a seed, some with their roofs on, some fallen in, some only walls, with streets, coral and sea grass, all washed toward the sea's colour and with light playing over them, and the boat sails right over it. Above the water stand only its tower (round, pale sea-worn stone stained and crusted with barnacles to 30 up, 190 tall, a ring of top windows lit purple at night) and a floating town beside it on the side toward home: three rafts on barrels, each with a shack at its back (a net loft with a stovepipe and a net drying, the trader's with a grouper on a board over its door, and a bait shack), plank walks between them and to a mat of seaweed gathered round the tower's foot, thick enough to stand on, lamps lit at night and a washing line. The boat ties up bow in at the end of a jetty pointing home; its dock ring sells the hold (the fish fly to the trader's) and opens the shop. Step ashore and the figure walks the planks and the seaweed right round the tower; the dog stays home. The tower door says it is barred from inside, every time the figure comes to it, until the next piece. A hull is kept off one convex outline round the seaweed and every plank (a hollow corner between a walk and the seaweed had a boat pushed back and forth), but the water over the drowned town is open. Three schools of grouper (new, 44 a fish) swim over the drowned shore on the far side from the town. A pale blue marker points to its dock while the flagship is in the deep, once island 2 has been found, pulsing until island 3 is (it waited for island 2's tower to be taken at first, and the owner, whose save had found island 2 but not taken its tower, could not find island 3; the deep's greeting now says to follow the blue marker up to the top of the map); the first time the boat comes within 700 it is announced and logged (isle3Seen). The anglerfish keeps 450 off it.
- Island 3's tower and the sorcerer's return (2026-10-08), the owner's fake-out: the door on the seaweed opens with the same Climb button as island 2's, and inside are two wet stone floors with weed hanging down the walls and puddles on the floor, four monkeys on the first (one throwing coconuts) and five on the second (two throwing), a little busier than island 2's since anyone here has a better spear. On the sand-coloured roof the leviathan sorcerer is back, with one new trick: once angry, every third cast is a ring of eight bolts out all round it, the first aimed at the figure, so the counter is to step into a gap. In the same person-model as island 2's (30 fights a spear), the ring barely moves the odds: 18 wins with the first spear, 25 with the long one and 30 with the barbed, against 8 to 24, 20 and up, and 30 without it; it is a new thing to dodge, not a harder fight. Beaten, it leaves scuba gear where it fell and says it was not the real boss: something is down there, under the tower. 1,000 coins the first time, 500 after; your flag flies from island 3's tower and its purple light goes out. Saved as isle3Stage 1, a number for how far through island 3's story the player is, so every stage stays done. world/tower.ts holds both towers' rooms and which are roofs; main.js keeps each tower's door, boss and floors together.
- The dive and the real boss (2026-10-08), the kid's: with the scuba gear, a gap in the seaweed round from the tower door shows a Dive button, and the figure goes down in a brass diving helmet with a glass port, a tank on its back and bubbles going up, to the hall at the tower's foot under the sea (blue and dim, light coming down in shafts, the foundation stones crusted and weedy, kelp, fish going by). There the swordsman waits: a man in a long red cape with a sword, 12 hits, nearly as quick as the figure (78 to its 85), so running only buys time. When he is close his sword goes up, glinting (0.45 s, the warning), then he slashes, a heart if the figure is still in reach and in front of him; then he is open for 0.8 s. Kept away from, he crouches and dashes at where the figure is heading. He blocks the first spear that would hit him, with a clang, once and never again. Beaten, he heals the figure to full, drops a key, and the floor splits open: a red flash and the figure is in the demon dimension (red-black sky, rising embers, black rock cracked with glowing lines), where he has become three little demons, four hits each, a bit smaller than the figure: red slashes like he did and blocks once; blue keeps away and calls up two skull-mask monkeys at a time, two more six seconds after the last pair is beaten; green does both, more slowly. Summoned monkeys never come back once beaten. At the back stands the cage with the warlock in it (part man, part bird, a bird's head with a hooked orange beak, a robe the green of deep water and a staff with a green flame; stand-in shapes for the kid to draw). Beat all three and the key opens the cage: the bars lift and the warlock steps out. The swordsman pays 1,500 and the demons 1,500, the first time. Each stage stays done (isle3Stage 2 and 3): die to the demons and the next dive finds a red portal turning where the swordsman stood, which goes straight back. Swim up leaves from either room, back onto the seaweed. In the person-model (a player who keeps away, steps aside a reaction time of 0.3 s after a sword goes up or a crouch starts, and throws at the nearest thing in reach), the swordsman is beaten by 20 to 29 in 30 with the first spear at a cost of more than a heart a fight, and 28 or more with the barbed; the demons and their monkeys by 12 or fewer with the first spear, 8 or more with the long one and 26 or more with the barbed, so the real boss wants the barbed spear. A first pass with him slower than the figure was won 30 times in 30 with every spear: a sword-fighter you can outwalk is no boss. entities/fighter.ts is one fighter set up four ways; render/depths.ts draws both rooms, the portal and the cage; render/warlock.ts the warlock.
- The warlock on foot (2026-10-08), the kid's companion: once he is free he comes ashore with the figure wherever it goes, every island, the towers' floors and roofs, the hall and the demon dimension, appearing beside it as it lands in a puff of green and following its steps a little behind (28, walking up to 105 to keep up); left far behind by a door, the stairs or a portal (over 200) he blinks to its side. Every 1.6 s he points his staff at the nearest enemy within 150 that is in the fight (a chasing monkey, a sorcerer that is up, the swordsman or a demon) and a green bolt flies and always lands, for 1, the same as a spear; it can use up a block. He has three hearts, shown in green beside the figure's, and mends them the same way, one every 6 s out of a fight. What would hurt the figure hurts him if he is in the way: a monkey's bonk, a sorcerer's bolt, a slash. Worn out, he flies back to his boat to rest and is back the next time the figure steps ashore, or after 40 s. Nothing new is saved: he is free once isle3Stage is 3. entities/warlock.ts holds him; the game finds his targets and decides what hurts him.
- The warlock's boat (2026-10-08), the kid's: once he is free and the figure is back aboard, he comes alongside in his own boat (deep-water green, a gold trim, a purple sail, him at the stern with his staff) and keeps station 70 behind and 62 off one side of yours, scaled by your hull, slowing as you slow and holding off when you tie up or go ashore; if you are suddenly far away (spat out at home) he catches up at once. Every 2.4 s, while he is aboard and you are at sea, if a grown fish of a school is within 140 of his boat and your hold has room, a green flash goes from his staff to it and it leaps into your hold, exactly as if your net had taken it (the night schools only once it is properly dark, as for the net). That is about 25 fish a minute when he is over a school, which helps most when the net is between schools. Islands, the pier, the bridge, the beach and island 3's town keep his hull off as they keep yours. When the figure goes ashore he goes too and his boat waits; worn out, he rests on it. entities/warlockboat.ts holds the boat; the game finds the fish and does the catching.
- The tar island and the cutscene (2026-10-08), the kid's: island 4 sits in the south corner of the deep, the last empty one, nearest the viewer, clear of the gulper's water. Until the cutscene it is a plain little island no one lands on (sand, palms, rocks), and hulls are kept off it. The cutscene plays once, the first time the figure is back aboard after the warlock is freed: letterbox bars come in, the HUD and the markers step aside, the shop folds away, and the camera flies down to island 4 (2.5 s), where an evil monkey in a skull mask on its beach raises its arms and a purple swirl turns on the water, a tar monster rises out of the sea (a great dome of tar with glowing yellow eyes, dripping arms, a purple sheen; stand-in shapes for the kid to draw), the water round the island spreads black with an oily sheen and bubbles, and the island goes black, its palms dying to drooping strands and tar puddles on the sand; captions say each step, the last that going there will need a chemistry suit. Then the camera comes home (12 s in all; a tap anywhere skips it). Saved as isle3Stage 4: from then on island 4 is tar, no hull goes into its black water (440 from its centre; trying says why), a dark marker at the screen's edge shows the way while in the deep, the log says Tar island seen and the field guide has the tar monster. The anglerfish keeps clear of it. world/isle4.ts holds where things are; render/isle4.ts draws every moment of the change from four numbers (tar, spread, rise, summon), so the cutscene plays it and the game holds it finished. The test draw view now throws on a negative radius as a real canvas does, after the spreading water's sheen threw in the first look at the cutscene.
- The chemistry suit and the tar (2026-10-08), the kid's: it lets you walk and swim in tar. The shipwright sells it (5,000) once the tar island has been seen; before that it is not on his shelf. With it, a flagship that sails up against island 4's black water and all but stops (under 60, within 70 of the edge plus the hull) gets an Into the tar button where the climb button sits; the boat ties up just outside the tar wherever it stopped, bow in, and the figure hops off into it. In the black water it swims, sunk to the chest with rings round it and its arms reaching over in turn, at 0.6 of walking pace, leaving no prints; on the island's sand it walks. All of it, out to 12 inside the tar's edge, is dry land for the walker, which can only get there off the bow; the monster and the evil monkey stand in the way. In the tar the figure wears the suit, yellow from boots to hood with a dark visor and a filter, and the warlock floats a hand above the black on a green shimmer. Nothing is sold at the tar's edge (it is not a dock), but the boat counts as tied up for the creatures while nobody is aboard. Back aboard from where it landed, as at the docks.
- The Tar Anchorer and the tarling (2026-10-08), the kid's (first called the tarbaby; renamed tarling on 2026-10-09, the owner's and the kid's call, because "tar baby" is used as a racial slur in the US, and this game is public): island 4's boss is the tar monster from the cutscene with a ship's anchor on a chain. Once the tar has spread it lurks where it rose, sunk to its glowing eyes (they follow the figure), and the evil monkey stands on the beach. The moment the figure sets foot on the island's sand it rises (1.2 s) and the monkey vanishes in a purple puff. It never leaves the black water: it wades round the shore to stay by the figure (50 a second, slower than a walk) and keeps between 46 off the sand and 40 inside the tar's edge. Every 2.6 s, within 300, it whirls the anchor over its head (1 s) and throws it at where the figure will be when it lands: a red ring on the ground shows the spot 0.45 s before the throw and the anchor flies 0.5 s, so there is about a second to get out of a ring 24 across; then it lies 0.9 s and is dragged back 0.5 s. A figure within 72 of it gets the swing instead: drawn back low for 0.7 s, then round itself to 80, then 1.2 s before it swings again. Each hit costs a heart; the warlock takes the same hits if he is in the way. It takes 80 hits' worth of spear and bolt (a bar over it shows what is left); at half it gets angry, its eyes go red, the whirl is 0.75 s and the next throw 1.2 s after. Beaten, it melts back into the tar (2.2 s), 2,000 coins, and a tarling climbs out beside the figure; it stays beaten (isle4Stage 1). Leave the island or be bonked out and it sinks back to its eyes, healed. Nothing walks through it. In the tests a person who stands and throws with the barbed spear and the warlock, and steps out of each ring a third of a second after it shows (give or take a quarter), wins every time in about 30 s at almost no cost; one who takes about half a second loses about a heart a fight and the odd fight; one who never minds the rings never wins.
