The tarling is a little glossy drop of tar with yellow eyes: aboard it rides on the deck by the stern; ashore it hops at the figure's heel along its way, every island and every room, popping over when it falls 200 behind, and every 2.4 s it spits a blob of tar at whatever the figure is fighting within 120, one hit each. Since 2026-10-09, as her kid wrote when he asked what it does, the tar lands in the face and sticks: whatever it hits is stuck for a second, a black splat dripping on its face with little stars going round, doing nothing (monkeys, the swordsman and the demons, the undead, the sorcerer, the Tar Anchorer, Cthulhu and its tentacles, the Forgotten One), and a sword it was raising, a whirl or sweep the Anchorer was winding up, or a ray the Forgotten One was aiming comes to nothing; what is already in the air lands as it was going to. Nothing hurts it. The log gains a chip and the tar monster's guide page tells the fight.
- Sea turtles (2026-10-08), the kid's: five green turtles paddle the inner rings, 420 to 1,150 from the island, never up onto it, at 18 a second, and come up to breathe every 9 to 19 seconds for about two and a half, the shell and head breaking the water with a little puff. Ease the boat to within 150 of one at 110 or slower and it comes alongside on whichever beam it is on, close under the surface where you can see it, matching the boat for up to 25 s; speed past 170 and it ducks away, and come at one that fast from within 220 and it dives off before it ever joins. After a swim or a fright it keeps to itself for 40 s. The first sighting (a turtle at the surface within 320) and every swim are logged (turtleSeen and turtleSwims), with a chip, a guide page and a soft two-note cue when one joins. The net never takes them: real nets have a door that lets turtles out, which the guide says. They are drawn at about twice a real turtle's size against the boat, since at true size they read as fish on a phone. The swims are there for the shipwreck to lean on later.
- The harpoon (2026-10-07), the kid's way to take out a leviathan: the shipwright's fourth spear, 6,000 coins, mounted on the bow (a post and a harpoon on it while it is loaded). Ashore it throws as a spear a touch better than the barbed one (160, 0.5 s, power 2). Aboard, when one of them is up and within 360 of the boat, the round throw button turns blue and fires a harpoon on a line from the bow at the nearest; it always lands, and the button dims while it reloads (1.2 s). Space fires it on a keyboard. What it hits: the leviathan at the buoys while it stands across your way (three hits), a standing tentacle of the Cthuluviathan, which goes straight back down and cannot grab (three since 2026-10-09, her kid's call: five was too hard), the anglerfish's light while it shows, which also shuts open jaws on nothing (three), and the gulper's head while it hunts, which makes it flinch and slow (four). Since 2026-10-09 also Star, the meteor serpent, while it is down in the water after a dive (four), and since 2026-10-11 the Reaper, reared up after a sweep (five). Each in reach shows its resolve as red pips over it, and a ring marks the one the harpoon will fire at. Harpoon one to none and it is driven off, never killed: it goes away for 120 s (the gulper rests, the Cthuluviathan sleeps, the anglerfish's light stays out, the leviathan sinks and leaves the buoys alone) and leaves a trophy (a leviathan spine, a jar of ink, an anglerfish lure, a gulper tooth, a meteor shard, a Reaper fin-spine), with 1,000 coins the first time and 200 after. The trophies show as chips in the shop's log and on the guide's pages, which also say how many hits each takes once you have the harpoon. In the tests, a person who keeps the gulper on the beam and fires whenever it is in reach drives it off in about two hunts in three and is never eaten; standing still to fire drives it off more often but can be eaten. A person who circles the Cthuluviathan's city dodging the bubbles and fires at the tentacles drives it off nearly every time, and is still grabbed now and then.
- Armour (2026-10-09), the kid's: "leather, diamond, gold, and space armour, each one giving you more hearts". The shipwright sells it a level at a time beside the spear, once there is a spear to fight with (data/armour.ts): leather 1,200 for four hearts ashore, diamond 3,500 for five, gold 7,000 for six, space 12,000 for seven; three with none. Space armour is the one bit of space the kid kept after agreeing the game stays on the sea: it comes from the far side of island 7's portal, so it is only on the shelf once the portal is awake (isle7Stage 1). The figure wears it: a chest plate over the shirt in the armour's colours with an edge and a shine, and from diamond up a helmet in place of the sou'wester (a white dome with a dark visor and an antenna for space armour); never over the chemistry suit, and the brass helmet still goes on to dive. Every fill of the hearts (bonked out, a boss beaten, the stairs, the cat, soul armour) fills to the armour's count. armour in the save, 0 to 5 (5 is Honor Guard, since 2026-10-15), none in older saves. look in the save (2026-10-16): `{skin, hair, hairColour, hat, hatColour, face, honorGuard}`, the character picked; missing in older saves and read as Skipper. The hearts bubble wraps now, its own hearts on top and the companions' under them, inside 168 px so it never runs into the order bubble at 390 wide.
- Honor Guard armour and the survival knife (2026-10-15), the kid's sixth list and his drawing: "new character armour Honor Guard, 9 hp, plus survival knife, 4 damage". The shipwright's fifth armour (HONOR_GUARD, data/armour.ts), once the Heron is beaten on island 9 (heronStage 2) and space armour is on: 15,000 coins, nine hearts ashore. It is gold, with a tall red plume swept back along the helmet and a red cape from the shoulders down the back, swinging as the figure walks; the cape is drawn behind the body when the figure faces this way and over its back when it walks away. The survival knife comes with it and hangs at the belt (the kid: "you can do both"): the Throw button stabs a foe right up close (within 34, KNIFE.reach, again in 0.45 s) at once, the arm thrust straight out with the knife in hand and a flash of the blade; a foe a little further off (within 100) has the knife thrown at it, turning end over end like a spear in flight (again in 0.8 s); past that the spear is thrown as before. The knife hits for 4 either way, twice the barbed spear, and a carrot doubles it like the spear; the spears still reach further. While the knife is what Throw would use, the button shows a knife and says "Use the knife". Picking Honor Guard as a look on the coming character screen (his other half of "both") is for that screen.
- Choose your character (2026-10-16), the kid's sixth list and his drawing of the pick screen: three people to pick, or your own made from colours, a cap or a fishing hat, hair and faces. He said the people are only looks and that we name them (they look like people he knows; the game uses no real names). data/look.ts: four presets, Skipper (the figure as it always was, yellow sou'wester, so old games keep their look), then his three, named Finn (spiky fair hair, a big grin), Coral (long dark hair, a smile) and Hank (short hair under a brown cowboy hat). Your own is any mix of five skins, five hairs (tucked in, short, spiky, long, pigtails) in six colours, five hats (sou'wester, cap, fishing hat, cowboy hat, none) in six colours, and five faces (eyes, smile, big grin, freckles, glasses); change anything on a preset and it is your own. The Honor Guard armour can be worn here as a look only, over whatever armour is on (his "both, one is just a cosmetic"); the hearts are the armour's. The card (#charCard) opens from the start screen (Choose your character, under Cast off) and from the shop (Your character), with the figure turning slowly in a little preview (drawn by the walker itself, Walker.drawPortrait, on its own canvas). The figure's skin, hair, face and hat are drawn from the look everywhere it walks, and the ice fisher wears its skin and hat colour. Helmets (diamond and up, Honor Guard's) still take the hat's place. The save's look (missing in old saves, read as Skipper; anything not understood, Skipper again). The armour bought from the shipwright can be hidden (the owner's report the same day, with her screenshot: in space armour the card showed only the helmet, which covers the whole head, so nothing picked could be seen): once there is armour, the card has a row for it, Show it or Hide it, saying its hearts still count either way; hidden, the figure is drawn in the look as picked, on the card and everywhere it walks, and its hearts are the armour's still (data/look.ts hideArmour, in the look's save, read as shown in an old one; picking a preset keeps it as it was). The Honor Guard look, if picked, is worn over the look either way.
- The sea map (2026-10-09), the kid's: "a map that shows everywhere you've sailed". A round map button bottom left (and a Sea map pill on the shop, where the button would sit under it) opens the whole sea on the planks, turned the way the game is so up on the map is up on the screen but square rather than squashed, so an upright phone has room (render/seamap.ts): the sea you know, the deep and the far deep in their blues with their buoy lines, every island, the boat as a red arrow the way it points with a pulse round it, and fog over everywhere not yet sailed, soft at its edges. Islands are named once found. The sea is cut into 200-unit cells (state/sailed.ts, 78 a side since the far far sea, 54 before), and each frame aboard the boat opens those within 450 of it; it goes in the save as base64 (sailed, about 1,000 characters; a save from before the far far sea has the old 54-a-side square, and every cell of it is kept, moved in to where it now sits). Until the far far sea is open the map shows the sea out to the far deep's edge as it always did, and its note counts that much; once it is open the map takes in the far far sea too, with its buoy line, and everything on it is a little smaller. A save from before the map gets the water round home and every island it has found, so a long-time player does not open a blank map. The note under it says how much of the sea has been sailed. While the map is open the game waits. The owner's call (2026-10-09): the far deep's empty corner gets filled before the sea grows. Places to go back to that are not islands are marked over the fog with their names (2026-10-11, the owner, who could not find the trench again): the trench, as a dark ring with bubbles coming up and "Trench: dive here", once it has been found or once there is scuba gear to dive it with. The ice floe is on the map as an island, named once found. The wreck at island 5 is not marked: at the map's scale it sits on island 5's own shore.
- Lionfish (2026-10-09), the kid's, part one: "they can sink your boat, so you'll need a repair tool", which he and the owner settled as: they cut your net and it needs mending. Five small groups of four, red and white with a fan of long spines, hover in the outer sea 1,700 from home (entities/lionfish.ts), a cutter's reach, drawn under the water. They turn up once the shipwright is open, so the kit can be bought by the time a net is first cut, and the first sighting is a toast and a guide page (lionSeen in the save). Tow the net through a group (moving, whole) and their spines cut it: three fish fall out, the net is drawn torn and catches nothing, and unlike a shark's tear it stays cut until it is mended, at any dock or at sea with the shipwright's mending kit (1,500, on the shelf once a lionfish has been seen; kit in the gear save). With the kit a Mend the net button comes up; it says Stop to mend while the boat moves, and stopped it mends the net in 2.5 s. The group scatters for 6 s and comes back. True to life: real lionfish came to Florida's water from far away and people there are asked to catch them, which the guide says. 
- The fishing rod (2026-10-09), lionfish part two, the kid's "catch them with a fishing rod": the shipwright's (800, on the shelf once a lionfish has been seen; rod in the gear save). Stopped within 240 of a lionfish, a Cast for lionfish button comes up; cast and the float sits out by the fish (fishing/rod.ts) for 0.9 to 2.2 s, saying Wait for it, then a lionfish takes it: the float goes under in rings, the button turns gold, pulses and says Reel in! for a second. Reel in then and it is caught, for a 40-coin bounty (lionCaught in the save, on the guide page), and its group is a fish smaller for 90 s; too soon and it is Too soon!, too late and It let go. Moving off or the fish going ends the cast. Simpler than the snook on purpose: lionfish want the bait, and the only trick is to wait for it.
- The lionfish net (2026-10-09), lionfish part three, the kid's "maybe a new net specific for lionfish eradication": the shipwright's (4,500, on the shelf once a lionfish has been seen; lionnet in the gear save). Fitted, a guard on the net turns the spines: towed through a group it is never cut, and every lionfish it passes is swept up for the 40-coin bounty (counted with the rod's in lionCaught), and is gone from its group for 90 s. It is the late, easy answer; the rod is the early, patient one, and the mending kit is for before either.
- The aquarium (2026-10-09), the kid's "aquariums ... and a way to play with your fish", the home half of it (bases on other islands are a separate thing). Once the tree platform is up, a plot is staked out on the sand south of the palace tree, with a rope round it and a sign with a fish on it; the shop sells the building for 25 driftwood and 1,000 coins (a card under the palace's, there from the tree platform on), and then a glass tank stands there on a wooden base with a few of your fish going to and fro behind the glass, a board over it with a fish on, lit from inside at night. Walk up to its glass and Look in the aquarium opens it full screen; once it is built the shop card says Look too. Inside, side on: sand and pebbles, sea grass and kelp, a stone that bubbles and a treasure chest whose lid lifts to let bubbles out, light coming down, and a fish of every species ever caught (three of the little ones, two of the middling, one of the big), the lionfish once one is caught and the snook once one is landed, each drawn as itself: the ray a diamond with a long tail, the marlin's bill and sail, the shark's fin, the koi's patches and whiskers, the lionfish's fans of spines, the tuna's finlets, the glowing ones glowing. They wander, each at its own depth. Tap the water and food falls from the top for them to chase and eat; hold a finger in the tank and they all come and follow it; tap a fish and its name shows, and the pufferfish puffs up spiky for a moment. A kind caught since the last look drops in from the top with its name showing. The game waits while it is open. Nothing in it costs or pays: it is somewhere to keep what you caught, and a reason to catch a new kind. aquarium in the save; the tank's fish are not saved, as they come from the catch log. world/aquarium.ts has the plot, render/tank.ts the tank.
- The naga (2026-10-10), the kid's blue sea-warrior, from his drawing: a fish head with its jaws open on little teeth and a yellow eye, an orange crest with red spines fanning back, orange shoulder plates, a blue body on a long spotted snake's tail with red spines down it and a purple fin at its end, and a long pole with a great serrated orange fin-blade (the owner said yes to a trident; the drawing has the fin-blade, so that is what he carries). The monkeys keep him in a bamboo cage under a thatch at island 2's camp, beside the totem, a solid thing the figure walks round. The first time the camp is beaten its chest pays as before and the cage's bars lift: he comes out and is yours (nagaFree in the save; an old save that beat the camp before him finds him still caged, and beats the camp again, as it refills, to free him). Ashore he is a companion like the cat and the warrior (entities/pals.ts, kind naga): at the figure's heel on every island and in every room, going for whatever is near the figure and striking it for 2 with the longest reach of any of them (36), five hit points, worn out back to the boat. At sea he does not ride the boat: he swims beside it, off the side nearer the viewer so the hull never hides him (ducking under to the other side once a turn has clearly put him behind it), alongside the cabin and clear of the net's lines, his tail under the surface and a ripple round him, keeping up at any speed. Worn out, he rests in the water there. "Naga" is a working name, as Star was; the kid may name him.
- Islands 8 and 9 (2026-10-10), the Heron's, the first of four parts (the hired humans, the Heron's fight at the towers, and the necromancer who joins you follow). Twin islands out in the far deep off its east side, the side nothing else was on, straight across the map from island 5, either side of home's line: island 8 north of it and island 9 south, 240 across each with a strait between, and a sandbar across the strait that the figure walks and no hull crosses (so the boat goes round). One dock, on island 8, facing home; the boat runs up on its sand and a trading post by the landing buys the catch. Island 8 is green, with palms, and a tower of blue-grey stone flying the Heron's banner, a white heron on midnight blue. Island 9 is the Heron's own: darker marsh grass, dead trees and boulders, and a taller tower of dark stone banded with alien metal and its green lights, crowned with a great nest of sticks with two grey feathers in it, under a purple light that never goes out (brighter at night). Reeds with brown heads grow at both shores. Sighted from 700 off, a toast says so ("Islands 8 & 9" on the sea map, one name for both since they are close); a blue marker points the way once island 7's sorcerer is beaten. Both towers' doors are barred with planks for now, and walking up to one says so. isle8Seen in the save. world/isle8.ts has both islands and the sandbar, render/isle8.ts draws them.
- The Heron's hired men (2026-10-10), part two of four, the kid's "hires humans to come after you", the owner's "humans chase in boats and fight on islands". At sea, once island 7's sorcerer has failed him (and the far deep is open), two boats in his midnight blue with white flags keep the water round the twins (entities/hired.ts): they patrol a ring 760 out from between the islands, and a boat that comes inside 1,400 of the twins and within 650 of one is chased ("The Heron's hired boats are after you!"). They go 300 to the flagship's 350, so running gets away; caught, they ram for 14 hull at most every 2.4 s and bounce off, and at no hull the boat goes down as to a leviathan ("The Heron's hired boats sank your boat!"). The harpoon takes them like a leviathan, the nearest one, three hits to sink it (60 coins; planks and two swimmers on the water), and the Heron hires another 45 s later on the far side of the twins. A boat tied up at a dock is left alone, and a chase gives up 400 past their water. Lanterns at night. Ashore, they camp on both twins round a fire: five on island 8 by its tower's door between two tents, four by the Heron's door on island 9 with one tent. They fight as the monkeys do (the camp is the monkeys' Monkeys class with a look of its own: helmets, clubs, midnight-blue tunics with a white sash, the throwers slinging stones), three hits each to the monkeys' two; each beaten drops his pay (25 coins) and runs, a camp beaten drops a purse (300), and they come back as the monkeys do. Hearts show ashore on the twins. Nothing new in the save. Once the Heron is beaten for good on island 9 his hired boats go home and the water round the twins is quiet; the men in his camps on islands 8 and 9 stay (the owner, 2026-10-10: "boats leave (for now), camps stay").
- The Heron, round one (2026-10-10), part three of four, the first half: his fight on island 8's tower, from the kid's drawing. Once island 7's sorcerer has failed him, island 8's tower door is open (island 9's stays barred, and says so). Inside, two floors of his hired men, five and then six, two of each throwing stones (world/tower.ts rooms 17 to 19; each beaten drops his 25 as outside), and the roof (ROOF8), where the Heron waits (entities/heron.ts): a great grey heron towering over the figure, on long black legs, with scalloped wings held up behind him, a tuft on his head, a long yellow beak and a fierce yellow eye, an alien gun at his chest and a staff of illusion with a pink gem. Fourteen hits. He wakes when the figure steps onto the roof and stalks it on his long legs, keeping about 95 off and inside the roof's edge. His gun draws a thin flickering pink line from his chest to where the figure stands; 0.8 s later a zap goes down that line, 300 long, and standing within 10 of it costs a heart ("Zzzap!"), so the counter is to step off the line. The first aim waits 3.2 s, so the warning has gone before it. Every 2.6 s he aims again. Every 11 s he raises the staff (0.7 s, the gem glowing) and there are three of him: two copies round the roof and the real one swapped into one of their places. The copies stalk as he does but never fire, so the one that shoots is the real one; a spear (or a helper's blow: they cannot tell either) pops a copy in a puff of pink, and only the real one is hurt. Copies last 9 s. Hurt to half he is angry: quicker, two zaps at a time (the second re-aimed 0.5 s after the first), every 2 s, and three copies. His zaps hurt a helper in their line as they would the figure. Beaten he does not fall: he screeches and flies off, wings beating, to his nest on island 9 (2,500 coins), and island 8's tower flies the player's flag. Climbed again later, the floors have been rehired and the roof is empty and yours. The bestiary has a page for him once the war has begun, and the log says Heron driven off. heronStage in the save (0 to 2: driven off island 8, beaten for good on island 9). Part 3b is island 9's tower: the orb of power shields him there, with the necromancer chained to it.
- The Heron, round two (2026-10-10), part three of four, the second half: his nest on top of island 9's tower, from the kid's drawing of the orb of power and the necromancer chained to it. Once he has fled there (heronStage 1), island 9's door opens. Two floors of hired men, six and then seven (world/tower.ts rooms 20 and 21), then the nest (NEST9, a room of its own kind): night sky, a woven floor of sticks with a rim heaped round it and two of his grey feathers lying in it (render/nest.ts). Over its back hangs the orb of power (entities/nest.ts): a great magenta ball scribbled round like the kid's, glowing, on blue chains like his, two running up out of sight and one down into the sticks, and a fourth to the necromancer, a little pink figure in a pointed hat with a purple cape flaring like wings, chained at the nest's right-hand edge. While he is chained the orb is lit and the Heron (14 hits, HERON9_HP, the same as island 8's since the kid's sixth list; it was 18, and his zap here takes two hearts) is inside a shimmering pink bubble: a spear or a helper's blow glances off him and his copies alike ("Shielded!", and a toast says to spear the necromancer first). The necromancer raises skeletons out of the sticks between him and the figure, the first 3 s in and then every 6 s, never more than two standing (they are a horde like Gigantis's, hordes[NEST9]); the spear goes for him first unless a skeleton is right on you, and helpers go for him and not the shielded Heron. Eight hits beat him: his chain snaps ("Snap!"), what he raised falls, the orb goes dark and cracked, and the shield goes with it. He kneels where he was, free. Then the Heron is as on island 8, zaps and copies, angry at half; beaten, he tumbles out of his nest and flaps off over the sea (4,000 coins, heronStage 2), and once the figure is back aboard he gets away through a portal (the escape, below). The orb over island 9's tower, the purple light seen from the sea, is dark from then on, the player's flag stands in the nest, the log says Heron beaten for good, and the bestiary's page tells it. Climbed again, the floors are still held (unpaid) and the nest is empty and yours, the necromancer kneeling by the dark orb. Part 4 is the necromancer joining: he summons a skeleton hamster.
- The necromancer and his skeleton hamster (2026-10-10), part four of four, the kid's "the necromancer becomes a companion that summons one skeletal hamster that does 1 damage but has a ton of HP". When the Heron is beaten for good the necromancer whose chain you broke comes away with you (the nest is empty after; heronStage 2 is all the save needs, so a save that beat the Heron has him). He is one of the pals (entities/pals.ts, kind necro), drawn as in the nest, a pink robe, a pointed hat and a purple cape: ashore at the figure's heel on every island and in every room, four hit points, worn out back to the boat; aboard he rides amidships, a little smaller. He does not fight up close. When a fight is on he keeps back by the figure and raises his skeleton hamster out of the ground ("Squeak!"), an ally like the bones' ghost and skeleton (entities/allies.ts, kind hamster): a little round skeleton with a big skull, round ears, green light in its eye, two long front teeth and a curl of tail bones, quick (96), biting for 1 close in, every second, and twelve hits to put down (it squeaks when hurt). One at a time: once it falls he raises another 10 s later, and with nothing to fight for a few seconds it crumbles back. The owner was unsure about the kid's 30 hit points; 12 is the start, a number in ALLY.hamster to change.
- The Heron's escape (2026-10-15), the first of the kid's sixth list: "you defeat the Heron, which now has 14 hp and 2 damage; the Heron falls in the water and the Reaper hunts him, but the Heron casts a portal and saves himself". A cutscene (kind heron, world/heronescape.ts for the timeline and render/heronescape.ts for the drawing), owed once the Heron is beaten on island 9 (heronStage 2) and played the next time the figure is back aboard and not diving, 1.5 s in, as the tar monster's is; the tar's goes first if both are owed. The boat stops, the bars come in, and the camera flies out over the open water off island 9's east shore (ESCAPE_AT, 230 past its sand), framed a little back toward the island so his tower and nest are in the picture. The beaten Heron flaps in weakly from the island's side, too hurt to fly, and tumbles down into the sea with a splash. He flounders, sunk to his chest, wings beating, what is under the surface hidden (a clip at the water line at his feet). The Reaper's striped fin and dark shape come in from the screen's right, straight at him (the Jaws notes). He raises his staff, its pink gem flaring; a purple portal opens on the water under him; he sinks through it ("The spell worked!") a moment before the Reaper's jaws shut on the spot (a snap, a roar, a shake). The portal shuts, the Reaper circles once and goes down, and the camera comes home: a toast says he got away through a portal of his own and will be back, the log's chip says "Heron beaten, and fled by portal" and the bestiary's page tells it. A tap skips it, as the others. 12.5 s. The horned, winged boss in the drawing that has the words was read at first as the Heron come back; the kid says it is Skrill, Skull Island's boss, and whether the Heron ever comes back (and whether "The spell worked!" is his line or only Skrill's) went back to him. Island 9's fight changed with it: 14 hits, not 18, but two hearts a zap.
- The otter (2026-10-10), the kid's most-asked-for pet, the owner's design: "a pet that rides along the boat, but gets on the boat in deep waters, or when there is something in the water". He lives wild just north of the home dock (OTTER_HOME), floating on his back in a slow circle with a pink shell on his tummy; the first time the boat comes near, a toast says to stop beside him and tap him. Tapped with the boat within 140 of him and fish in the hold, he is tossed the commonest fish (a toast counts down); on the third he is yours (otterFed in the save, 0 to 3; the log says "Something off the dock?", then the count, then Otter befriended). From then on he goes where the boat goes (entities/otter.ts): in home water he swims in the water off the bow on the side nearer the viewer, clear of the naga and the net, tummy down with a little wake, ducking under now and then between breaths, and floats on his back beside it when the boat is stopped. Out past the buoys, or whenever something is in the water within 420 of the boat (a shark, a lionfish group, or any of the beasts the harpoon knows: the leviathans, the gulper, Star, the shark king, the Deep One, the Heron's boats), he climbs up onto the cabin roof (0.45 s) and sits up there, up on his toes with a "!" when it is danger ("Eep!" over the boat; a toast the first time for each); 3 s after it is safe again he slips back in with a splash. So his scrambling aboard is a warning. Tap him to pet him (hearts). He is drawn bigger than life (1.6 in the water, 1.25 on deck) so a small player finds him. A stand-in until the kid draws him.
- Companions keep apart (2026-10-10, the owner's report: "they all stack on top of each other in one clump"). Each companion follows the figure along its steps and stops a few steps behind, and with several along they all stopped in the same few steps. Now, ashore, those near the figure (within 60) are nudged a little each frame (entities/spread.ts): any two closer than 20 apart, any within 15 of the figure off it, and any standing just in front of it (within 80 of its depth toward the viewer and 24 across the screen) aside, so the figure is never hidden; at most 70 a second, so they drift rather than jump, and through each one's own walking, so nobody is nudged off the sand. One still walking up from further off is left to follow the steps. And a companion that can make no way at all along the figure's steps for 0.8 s (STUCK_T) pops over beside it, which a nudge onto a pier's end could otherwise cause. That held for two or three, but not for the whole army (the owner, 2026-10-12, with everything unlocked: "I thought we fixed the companions piling on top of each other"): they are bigger than 20 (the baby Reaper is 54 long), and with eight along only the near ones were nudged, so they still piled on the figure. So outside a fight the army forms up (formation, assignSlots and formUp in spread.ts): each has a place of its own in rings behind the figure on the screen and out to either side, 110 degrees from straight behind, never in front of it; the first ring 34 out, each next 38 further, places 42 apart round a ring, a place the figure could not walk to (the sea, a wall) passed over. The small ones take the near ring and the big ones the far ones (tarling, cat, warrior, naga, bones, warlock, necromancer, baby Reaper), and within a ring each takes the place on its own side, matched by angle round the figure, so none walks across another or in front of the figure to get there. Each stops following once it is out at its own ring (heelAt, in the companions, the warlock and the tarling), so on the move they string out behind the figure; once it stops (under 15 a second) they walk in to their places at 60 a second. In a fight they go for whatever it fights, as before, and only keep apart. The owner and her kid were weighing a limit of four hand-picked companions; she was wary of taking the army away by rule, since walking about with all of it is something no other game lets him do, so this makes the army read instead of shrinking it.
- Bases on other islands (2026-10-10), the kid's idea and the husband's fishing styles in one, the owner's mapping: each island with a base is built up a stage at a time from its own dock, with driftwood and coins as the palace is, and the stage after the first opens that island's own way of fishing (island 2 seining, the floating town on island 3 crab pots, island 6 charters; the coast guard comes as a radio at a base, protected animals as seining's bycatch rule). Island 2 is first, and its first stage is a hut: docked there, a card in the shop ("Build a hut here", 30 driftwood and 1,500 coins) builds it on a roped plot on the sand beside the tower, in sight from the dock: plank walls, a pitched roof in the boat's roof paint, a door with a lantern, a window lit at night, and a pole flying the player's flag. A hut makes the island a home port: when the boat goes down (sunk, swallowed) nearer that base's dock than home's, it comes back up 177 off that dock facing it, rather than in the home shallows; the hold is lost as ever. Selling was already there, which is why the hut's perk is the home port. world/bases.ts holds the bases (BASES, the stages, homePort), render/base.ts the hut; bases in the save, a stage per base. Kept in mind, not built: the kid wants companions given jobs at a base, and a job would sit beside the stage in the save.
- Seining (2026-10-10), island 2's verb, the husband's and the owner's: "drop a buoy, drive a loop around a school, come back to the buoy, and everything inside is yours". Island 2's second stage, the gear shed (50 driftwood and 3,000 coins, the card after the hut's), stands between the hut and the tower: open toward the viewer, a seine hung up inside with its corks, a roof in the boat's roof paint and a spare buoy by the door. From then on the boat carries a seine everywhere. At sea a round button over the throw button (a buoy and a ring of corks) drops the buoy at the stern; the seine pays out after the stern as a rope with a cork every 16, and a yellow ring round the button shows how much is left (1,500 in all; the corks go red in the last fifth). Once 400 is out a ring pulses round the buoy, and bringing the stern within 60 of it closes the loop: every fish that is up and inside comes aboard one after another (0.05 s apart, through the usual catch, so the combo sound climbs), until the hold is full. Skill, not volume: a loop up to 3 times the area of the schools inside it keeps every fish, a wider one keeps that share (never under 40%), and the toast says so ("Some slipped out under the loose loop: a tighter loop keeps more" or "Not one got away"). 3 is about the tightest a flagship can turn at full speed round the smallest school (parrotfish, 80), so perfect driving keeps them all. Out of seine before the loop closes, it comes back empty ("A tighter loop!"); tap the button again to haul it in empty. While the seine is out the towed net is lifted (drawn faded, catches nothing): circling a school otherwise swept it up with the towed net before the loop could close, which made the seine pointless. Docking, stepping ashore or being swallowed hauls it in. fishing/seine.ts holds it (Seine, keepShare), render/seine.ts draws it.
- Island 3's base (2026-10-10), the floating town's: a fourth raft, the base raft (BASE_RAFT in world/isle3.ts), is lashed on north of the bait shack's, with a plank walk out to it past that shack's back corner, roped off with a sign from the start and in sight from the jetty. Its first stage is the same hut as island 2's (30 driftwood and 1,500 coins, the card when docked at the jetty), standing up on the raft's planks, and makes island 3 a home port: the boat comes back up 177 off its dock along the jetty, facing it, when it goes down nearer there than home or island 2. Bases now carry the height they stand at (z, PLANK_Z here); render/base.ts lifts the drawing by it.
- Crab pots and the spider crab (2026-10-10), the husband's "drop, leave, return", the owner's design: "crazy crabs with long spider legs and colorful bodies", three pots, "no crab traps in the deep". Island 3's second stage is a crab shed beside its hut on the base raft (50 driftwood and 3,000 coins), with crab pots stacked by it. From then on the boat carries three pots, and at sea a round button with a crab on it, over the seine's, has a badge saying how many are aboard. Tapped in home water it drops one astern; not past the buoys, not by the island, the beach, the bridge or in the dock, and not within 90 of another (a toast says why). A pot shows as a float in the boat's paint with a white band, a yellow top and a tall yellow pennant, a bubble at the waterline for each crab inside; a spider crab climbs in every 60 s near home, faster further out, down to every 25 s from 2,200 out, up to 6, and a full one has a crab sitting on its float, a ring pulsing round it and an edge marker when it is off screen. Come back past a pot (within 55) and its crabs fly into the hold, and it stays down to fill again; stopped by one, the badge turns to an arrow and the button lifts it back aboard to move it. The spider crab is species 16 (25 coins, sold as any fish at any dock, never asked for by orders or the fishmonger): a coral shell with purple spots, two claws, eyes on stalks and eight long gold legs jointed high like a spider's, drawn from above flying aboard and on full pots, side on walking the aquarium's sand on its legs (and following food that sinks there), and as a little crab in the catch log and the field guide. The pots, where they are and their crabs, are saved (pots). Button taps on the seine and the pots now answer at once, clearing any message in the way, since a queued refusal could otherwise be lost behind a long one. fishing/pots.ts holds the rules, render/pots.ts the floats, render/crab.ts the crab. A stand-in until the kid draws one.
- Island 6's base (2026-10-10), the big island's, where charters will come from: its first stage is the same hut (30 driftwood and 1,500 coins, the card when docked at the end of its pier), on the beach at the pier's root across the way up from the trading post, the first thing ashore and clear of the palms, rocks and chests. It makes island 6 a home port out in the far deep: a boat that goes down nearer its dock than home or the other bases comes back up 177 off the pier's end, facing it.
- Charters (2026-10-10), the husband's: "take people out for coins and bring back no fish", with the creatures as the destinations, from island 6 as the owner mapped it (fishing/charter.ts). Island 6's second stage is the charter office (50 driftwood and 3,000 coins), a booth between the hut and the pier's root with a counter, a red and white striped awning and a board with a whale's tail. Docked at island 6's pier, the shop then offers a charter: two or three passengers, each wanting to see a different thing, out of the whales (one of them up at the surface within 500, 300 on the fare), a manta leap (within 750, 400), a sea turtle swimming alongside (250), a ride with the dolphins (an escort, 250) and the Cthuluviathan asleep, from a safe distance (asleep, within its 1,100 sighting ring, 500). Take them and they stand at the bow rail and aft in sun hats and bright shirts, and a card under the order lists their wishes, ticking each as it is seen ("Ooh!" rises off the boat and a toast says what it adds). Tie up at any dock after leaving island 6's and they pay: 100 for the trip and each sight seen, half again as a tip if every wish was seen, halved if they had a fright (once: the boat hurt, or a shark charging within 220). Seeing nothing they wanted, they pay nothing, or a hop out of the dock and back would be a fare every few seconds. Being swallowed ends the charter unpaid: they go home. Nothing is lost but the fare. The next charter waits at the office when the boat next docks there. charter (who is aboard and what they have seen) and chartersRun in the save.
- The trench (2026-10-10), the kid's "THE DEEP biome: scuba diving and spear fishing, bioluminescent fish and plants", part one, the owner's choices: side on, one trench worth sailing to, cosy first. A crack in the sea floor in the deep's north side (TRENCH, between island 3 and the north mahi-mahi), a patch of black-blue water with bubbles coming up; a toast on first sight says what it is and, without the scuba gear from island 3's tower, where to get it (and the boat stopped over it says so again). With the gear, a boat stopped over it shows Dive into the trench, and the view turns side on (render/dive.ts, in place of the sea; the sea waits, as it does while the aquarium is open): the boat's hull at the surface overhead with a rope down, the diver in the brass helmet with a tank and yellow flippers, drawn half again life size, swimming where the stick points (or the keys) and drifting gently up when let go. The trench (760 across, 1,500 deep, world/trench.ts) goes from sunlit water with light shafts, kelp up the walls and shoals of silver and gold fish, through twilight with sea fans and glowing pink jellies, to midnight below 820, where its walls close in and the dark closes round the diver's lamp and nothing shows but what glows: lanternfish, violet squid, and a garden of glowing tube worms, anemones, sea pens and strands on the walls and the floor, in cyan, magenta, green and violet. Air is ten bubbles at the top, 75 s of it, the depth beside them; at a quarter left a toast says head back up, and when it runs out the diver just floats back up to the boat, no harm done. Swim back up (bottom left) does the same on purpose; swimming up at the surface ends the dive too. Back aboard, a toast says how deep you went, or that it is the deepest yet. trenchSeen and trenchDeep in the save; the log has a chip once the gear is found (A trench in the deep?, Trench found, Trench dived N deep), and an edge marker with bubbles points the way to it past the buoys. Spearfishing down there is the next entry.
- Spearfishing in the trench (2026-10-10), THE DEEP part two, the kid's "spear fishing". Diving with a spear from the shipwright (any level), the throw button shows over the stick's side as it does ashore, and the hold stays on the HUD. The throw button acts on the press, not the click (2026-10-11): the owner speared nothing on her phone, because a diver keeps a thumb on the stick and an iPhone makes no click of a second finger's tap; the same button throws ashore and fires the harpoon at sea. A keyboard click still throws, and Space throws in a dive as it does ashore. It aims itself at the nearest catch ahead of the diver and within the spear's reach (1.4 times its throw on land; a pulsing ring marks it), or throws straight ahead with none, at 380 a second, and takes the first catch whose middle its path passes within 11 plus half the fish's size (fishing/underwater.ts). The catches are the midnight shoals: the trench's lanternfish (the same species 6 as the far corners' schools), the glow squid (species 17, 45 coins, violet, three of them) and the neon dragonfish (species 18, 65, down by the floor: black with a row of blue lights and a glowing lure on its chin, three). The silver fish and the jellies are never aimed at. A speared fish goes into the hold (room permitting: a full hold says swim up and sell), "+1 glow squid" rises off it in its glow colour, the first of a kind is told, and its place in the shoal stays empty 30 s. Each spear level's reload holds between throws (the button greys). Both new species are in the log, the field guide (where: the trench; when: always dark down there, glows) and the aquarium, drawn as themselves: a squid swimming tail first with fins at its mantle's tip and trailing arms, and a long black dragonfish with teeth, blue lights and its lure pulsing, glowing blue in the tank's dark. Without a spear the first dive's toast says to bring one.
- The big air tank (2026-10-10), THE DEEP part three's first piece, the owner's go-ahead: a card on the shipwright's shelf once the scuba gear has been found (3,500, the Gear table), and with it fitted every dive starts with 120 s of air, not 75. The gauge stays ten bubbles, each worth more, and the low-air warning comes at a quarter of whichever tank it is. It is the counter to what comes next: the lurker's bites take a fixed gulp of air, so a bigger tank takes more of them. gear.tank in the save; older saves read it as not fitted.
- Treasure in the trench (2026-10-10), THE DEEP part three's second piece (world/loot.ts, render/loot.ts): six things lost down the crack, drawn half again life size. An old spyglass (200) on a ledge in the twilight at 700 on the right wall; a gold crown (500) at 1,100 on the left, a ship's bell (250) at 1,250 on the right and a glowing gem (400) at 1,320 on the left, each on a little lip of rock; and on the floor a giant clam and a treasure chest (600, the most). They are drawn under the dark, so the lamp shows them up close, and each one still to find glints over the dark, a soft gold glow with a star in it twinkling on its own beat (the gem glows pink too), so they can be spotted from a way off. No plant grows within 34 of one (clearOfLoot), or the glowing garden would hide them. Swim within 30 and it is yours: the coins rise off it, a toast names it and counts (3 of 5 treasures found), and it is found for good, the chest left open and empty, the rest gone. The giant clam is the one that comes back: open on a pearl (150) when it has one, shut when it has not, and the pearl grows back every dawn (pearlDay, the game day it was last taken). Each dive's toast says to look out for things glinting until all five are found, and the log has a Trench treasure N of 5 chip once the trench has been dived. trenchLoot (one bit each) and pearlDay in the save; older saves have found none and have a pearl waiting.
- The lurker (2026-10-10), THE DEEP part three's last piece, the kid's "something that lurks in the dark", with the owner's choice of teeth: it bites air (entities/lurker.ts, render/lurker.ts). A long black fish, 230 from jaw to tail, with an underslung jaw of needle teeth, a fin on its back, a row of faint cyan lights down its side and two pale green eyes; its body is drawn just lighter than the deep water under the dark, so the lamp picks out its shape close to and the dark hides it further off, while its eyes and lights are drawn over the dark. It lives below the twilight (820). A diver six seconds in the midnight water sees its eyes come out of the dark (a toast the first time says what it does and what to do; after, Eyes in the dark), from 460 off on whichever side has more of the dark, coming in at 60 a second, then circling at 150 for 2.5 to 4.5 s. Its warning: the eyes flare orange, its jaw drops and its teeth catch the light, for 0.85 s, with the anglerfish's opening sound. Then it lunges at where the diver is, at 300 a second for 0.75 s: its mouth within 30 of the diver is a bite, 25 s of air bubbling out of the tank in a burst (three bites empty the scuba gear's own 75; the big tank's 120 takes four), a shove along its way, and it goes back into the dark and waits for the diver to stay six seconds more. Swim aside in the warning and it shoots past, then circles again the other way. A spear passing within 30 of its head or its middle stings it: Stung! rises off it and it flees for 25 s plus the six. The throw aims at it first when it is out, ahead and in reach (which is why it circles at 150, inside the second spear's 175). It never follows a diver more than 60 above the twilight or one on the way up, and nothing is lost but the dive: run out of air and the diver floats back up, as ever. The tests play it with a diver holding still to spearfish who, when the eyes go orange, swims across its line toward open water: at 0.23, 0.33, 0.43 and 0.5 s late it is never bitten in a minute of attacks; in a probe, 0.7 s late (most of the warning) was still never bitten, and 0.8 s was bitten two lunges in five. A diver who stays put is. lurkerSeen and lurkerStung in the save; a log chip (Eyes in the dark?, Met the lurker, Lurker stung N times) and a guide page once the trench has been dived.
- The wreck dive (2026-10-10), the kid's "the wreck's treasure" from his first list, the owner's choices: island 5's wreck, side on as the trench, swim into the hull for the chest, keep the bone sharks off with the spear. The fishing boat wrecked on island 5's rocks lies on the sand in clear shallow water (320 deep, world/wreck.ts, render/wreck.ts), the island's pale rocks rising on the left, ribs and skulls on the sand, sea grass, and its broken mast lying past the bow. With the scuba gear, a boat stopped within 150 of it shows Dive to the wreck (the same button as the trench's, saying where), and near it a toast says so while its treasure is still there. The dive scene is the trench's engine with a site of its own (world/divesite.ts: width, depth, where the diver goes in, how close it is seen, and its water): seen closer than the trench (300 across, not 420), so everything is bigger and the sand fills the bottom of the screen. The boat sits upright, drawn cut away so its hold shows, with its deck torn open over the bow half: the way in is from above, and the chest (1,200, the most of any treasure) is at the back of the hold under what is left of the deck, glinting. Its timbers are solid to the diver, so it is swum into, not through. Groupers (the same species as island 3's) keep round the bow and can be speared; silver fish play up in the light. Two bone sharks (entities/boneshark.ts), the island's skeleton sharks under the water, all ribs and grin, cruise a slow loop over the wreck; a diver in the open within 170 of one is noticed, it turns and its jaw opens (0.7 s, the warning), then it rushes where the diver is at 300 a second: still there, it bites the tank (25 s of air, as the lurker's bite) and swims off and comes round again. They cannot get into the hold: inside it the diver is safe, and the first warning's toast says so. A spear stings one off out of the scene for 20 s. The tests play a diver holding still who swims aside a third of a second into the warning; it is never bitten. wreckChest in the save; a log chip (Treasure in the wreck?, Wreck treasure found) once island 5 has been seen with the scuba gear.
- Storms (2026-10-10), from the dinner list and the owner's first plan, with her call: past the buoys only, so the home water stays cosy (world/storm.ts). Every six to ten minutes spent out past the buoys a storm comes: a warning first, 15 s ("Dark clouds over the deep: a storm is coming! The gulls have gone quiet"), as the sky darkens and the gulls fall silent; then the storm, 60 to 90 s, of dark sky, rain blowing across the screen and lightning every 5 to 12 s with thunder after it, as the swell builds in the ambience and the pad fades out. In it the boat's top speed is 72% of itself and its turn rate 60% (steerBoat and steerBoatRelative take the turn's share, 1 everywhere else, so the legacy feel is untouched outside a storm and the legacy test still holds), and the schools out past the buoys go deep, out of sight and out of the net (Sweep.sunk). None of it reaches home water or a boat tied up, and how much of it the boat feels fades in over the first 150 past the buoys. Nothing is lost. Then the boil, 60 s: the fish come back up with splashes round every school out past the buoys, and each fish caught there counts double (x2 rises off it, room permitting). The first storm puts ballast on the shipwright's shelf (2,500, the Gear table), which keeps 92% of the speed and 85% of the turn. With reduced motion the lightning does not flash and the rain does not move. stormSeen and gear.ballast in the save; the storm's own timing is not saved, so a new session starts the wait afresh.

**Persistence**: `localStorage["netprofit.v1"]` = `{coins, earned, muted, lv:{net,hold,engine}, paint, log[], order, wood, build, market, day, first[], levSeen, whaleSeen, mantaSeen, cthuluSeen, anglerSeen, petted, isle2Seen, flag, gulperSeen, reaperSeen (the Reaper sighted, missing in old saves and read as not), reaperLandSeen and reaperEgg (the Reaper on land sighted, and his egg: 0 he is not beaten, 1 beaten and the egg in his nest, 2 the egg taken and aboard, 3 hatched and the baby Reaper yours; missing in old saves and read as not and 0), eggDay (the day the egg came aboard; it hatches at the next dawn; missing in old saves and read as 0, so an egg already aboard hatches at once), junk and muffins (the junk in the bucket on deck, 0 to 12, and the fish muffins in the box, at most six, each the game day it was baked; missing in old saves and read as none), leggy (the muffins that grew legs and follow you, 0 to 6; missing in old saves and read as none), crew (who rides which of the flotilla's seats, `{airboat, catamaran, windsurf, banana, swan, swan2}`, each a rider or null, swan2 the seat beside the swan's; missing in old saves and read as the start, a seat missing from it (one from before the swan) given its starting rider if he is on no other, a rider named twice kept on the first, an unknown one dropped), iceSeen and iceRuns (the ice floe found, and the drops through its hole; missing in old saves and read as not and none), neilFed (ice fish tossed to Neil the seal, 0 to 3; missing in old saves and read as none), iceLoot (the things frozen in the ice under the floe that have been chipped out, a bit each; missing in old saves and read as none), spear, masks, towerTaken, driven, turtleSeen, turtleSwims, isle3Seen, isle3Stage (0 to 4: tower taken, swordsman beaten, warlock free, tar island seen), isle4Stage (0 to 1: the Tar Anchorer beaten and the tarling yours), isle5Seen, isle5Stage (0 to 1: the Skeleton Shark King dead), isle6Seen, chests6 (island 6's opened treasure chests, a bit each), isle6Stage (0 to 2: the Deep One dragged the boat under, Cthulhu beaten), isle7Seen, isle7Stage (0 to 4: the sorcerer beaten on island 7's tower and the portal awake, the Forgotten One beaten, the key to the door behind his throne given, the Old One beaten), soulArmour, heronStage (0 to 2: the Heron driven off island 8's tower, beaten on island 9's), heronFled (his escape through a portal has played; missing in old saves and read as not, so a game that beat him before the scene existed sees it once, and only true with heronStage 2), isle10Seen (island 10 sighted; missing in old saves and read as not), isle11Seen (island 11 sighted, out in the far far sea; missing in old saves and read as not), catermanFree (the Heron's brother has flown and Caterman is yours; missing in old saves and read as not), skullKey (the skull key fetched up from island 11's black lagoon; missing in old saves and read as not), cryptWon (the crypt behind the skull door won, the Forgotten One yielded; missing in old saves and read as not), castleHQ and scanner (the Forgotten One's ending played: Gigantis your headquarters, and the portable scanner he gave you, by its level, 0 for none, rising with the kinds scanned and brought up to them on load; missing in old saves and read as not and none), scanned (the kinds of creature the portable scanner has scanned, each once; missing in old saves and read as none), honey and hiveDay (jars of honey in the bag, 0..5, and the day the forest's bees last gave one, 0 for never; missing in old saves and read as none and never), saveCode and codeTold (the game's save code, four of state/cloud.ts's words, and when it was last shown to write down; missing in old saves, or not a code, and read as none, and the game makes one), barge (the Battle Barge's setup, `{size, pick, where, sea}`, the arena's size, how many of each on-foot enemy, on foot or at sea, and how many of each sea boss, ten at most each side; missing in old saves and read as a medium arena on foot with three monkeys and two skeletons, and one Star at sea, unknown kinds dropped, counts made whole) and bargeWins (fights won on it; missing and read as none), bases (`{isle2, isle3, isle6, floe}`, how far each base is built: 0 nothing, 1 its hut (the igloo on the floe), 2 its gear shed and the seine on island 2, its crab shed and the pots on island 3, its charter office on island 6; pots (`[{x, y, crabs, t}]`, the crab pots in the water, none in old saves); trenchSeen and trenchDeep (the trench found, and the deepest dive in it, 0 to 1,500); missing in old saves and read as nothing built), gear, snook, trip}`. `gear` is `{mesh, strongbox, suit, kit, rod, lionnet, tank, ballast, white, radar, radio}`, and `radioDay` is the day the coast guard was last called (0 for never; missing in old saves and read as never); a save from before the chemistry suit reads it as not bought. `keys` is `"drive"` or `"point"`, the keyboard feel, missing in old saves and read as drive. `market` is the fishmonger's pick of the day, a species index or -1 when there is none, missing in old saves and read as -1. `day` counts dawns from 1 and `first[]` is the day each species was first landed (0 for never); both missing in old saves and read as day 1 with nothing landed. `trip` is `{x, y, h, clock, hold[]}`: the boat's position and heading, the time of day and the hold. It is written on every save, when the tab is hidden, on pagehide and every five seconds while sailing, and restored on load, so a phone that discards the tab does not lose the haul (added 2026-09-17; legacy does not have it, and old saves without it load unchanged). A flagship saved out in the deep is restored there; the position is clamped to the deep's end, not the buoys. Entity state is not saved. `drone` is `{owned, colour, flights, bestRings, traps, beetles, up}` (2026-10-21), the colour an index into DRONE_COLOURS, `traps` the day a trap was hung at each of the five flowers (-1 for none, never more than five), `beetles` the jewel beetles brought home; missing in old saves and read as none, and traps and beetles missing in saves from before the traps (2026-10-22) read as none, as `up` (each upgrade's level, 0 to 2) is in saves from before the upgrades. An untouched drone (not bought, no colour picked, never flown) is left out of the save, `mer` (2026-10-22, `{seen, pearls, given, night, charm, sirens}`: the mermaids off island 2 seen, the pearls held and given in all, never more held than given, the day the last was given, -1 for never, the siren charm strung from five, and the sirens heard) until they have been seen, `garden` (the Garden Isle's, described in its own paragraph below) until it has been sighted, and `endless` (2026-10-23, `{seen, pirate, ninja}`: the Endless Isle sighted, and the sides joined, either or both; a save from part one has only seen, and reads as nobody joined; saved as `doom` the day it was built, before the rename, and read from there when `endless` is missing, so the key `doom` is not to be used again, the new Doom Island included; and its `battles`, `{fought, won, marks}`, once one has been fought: the battles played to the end and won, and the marks for each side and role, `{pirate: {tank, damage, healer}, ninja: {...}}`; missing reads as none, and anything that is not a count as 0; and its `slalom`, `{runs, best: {bunny}}`, once a run has been skied: the runs skied to the finish and the best time on each run in seconds, to a tenth; missing reads as none skied, and a time that is not a time as none; and its `climbs`, `{climbs, top, best: {1..4}}`, once the great tree has been climbed: the climbs made (a few seconds in or more), the highest stage got to the top of, and each stage's best time in seconds, to a tenth; missing reads as none, and anything that is not a count or a time as none) until it has been sighted: the save travels in the copy-my-game link, kept short; missing reads as untouched and not seen. pets (the pet system's, data/petsys.ts: the names given in the pet book, `{nm: {baby: 'Chomp'}}`, and the pets drawn on the pad, `drawn: [{id, nm, d, got}]`, four at most, each drawing a few hundred characters at most, and `walk`, the one that walks with you, and `eggs`, three at most, each `{k, sp, from, at, due}` in real-time milliseconds; missing in old saves and read as none, left out while empty).

**Persistence, the Garden Isle** (2026-10-24): `garden`, `{seen, beds?, seeds?, bag?, stock?}`, left out of the save until the isle has been sighted. seen: the Garden Isle sighted; the six open beds, each null or `{crop, planted, ripe, next, w?, born?}`, the times in milliseconds since 1970 and next 0 while a full plant waits, w the sizes of its ripe fruit and born how many it has ripened in all; the seeds held, by crop; the basket as `bag`, a list of `{c, m, v?, mu?}`, each fruit's crop, size, Gold or Rainbow and weather marks (`wet`, `shocked`, `moonlit`, `twilight`); a bed's ripe fruit's Gold or Rainbow in its `v` and their marks in its `mu`, a list for each (a save from before sizes, with `basket` as a count of each crop, reads as normal-sized fruit); and the seed stall's `stock`, `{day, n}`, the game's day it came in and the seeds left of each crop, a crop sold out kept as 0; each optional, an unknown crop or a bad time read as an empty bed, counts clamped to 99 seeds and a basket of 50. Kept in its own paragraph so that new fields elsewhere in the save do not touch it.

The guardrails (`GUARD`, the owner's "rival the boat"): a full plot of 24 beds of the best crop that keeps fruiting, left overnight, pays 30,000 to 50,000; by hand, no more than 2,000 a minute; both with size in. "Sells for" is a normal-sized fruit: each fruit is 0.8 to 1.2 of normal and sells by the square of that (never under 0.9 squared), and one in 200 is a giant, three to four times normal. One fruit in 100 is Gold (20 times as much) and one in 1,000 Rainbow (50 times), never both. The weather's marks add on top: Wet and Moonlit twice, Twilight three times, Shocked ten (only while the game is played). No one fruit sells for more than 100,000. The basket carries 50; seeds, 99 of a kind. Six beds can be planted.

Other constants: pirate speed 188, pirate unlock 60 lifetime coins, shark charge speed 235, shark orbit speed 95, dolphin escort speed `max(150, boat·1.15 + 50)`, rare swim speed 58, driftwood yield `1–3 + tier`, salvage `[5,8,10,15,20,35] × (1 + floor(tier/2))`.

**Balance status: lightly tested.** The owner has played to flagship. She asked for faster early upgrades once (costs were roughly halved). Everything past cutter is numbers picked to look sensible. Expect to retune.

Retuned 2026-09-16 after the owner reached flagship with 3,600 idle coins and 20 of 70 driftwood. Driftwood yield now scales with the full tier (was half of it) and respawns favour the current range, because the spawn disc grows almost six times in area from dinghy to flagship while the same 22 logs had to cover it, which left wood per minute at the top at about 40% of a dinghy's. The watchtower and dome cost 2,000 and 5,000 coins (were 250 and 600). A first pass at 1,200 and 3,500 was banked before the wood was; a second at 4,000 and 12,000 assumed a flagship docking pays one to two thousand, which one big lanternfish sale had suggested. Measured over ten minutes of real play at stage 4 on 2026-09-17 (screenshots 18:12 and 18:22), casual income near the island is about 115 coins a minute, so the dome was a hundred-minute grind. At 2,000 and 5,000 the stages are roughly eighteen and twenty-seven minutes of casual play, or a few good trips to the goldfin and lanternfish rings. A carpenter selling wood for coins was considered and rejected: at flagship income it would make driftwood optional exactly when the map is biggest. Where these tables and `legacy/net-profit.html` disagree, the tables win and the port reproduces the tables.

## How the prototype is built

One IIFE, plain canvas 2D, DOM for HUD and shop, Web Audio oscillators for sound, Grandstander from Google Fonts.

**Projection.** `K = √½`. Screen x = `((wx−camX) − (wy−camY))·K·Z + W/2`. Screen y = `((wx−camX) + (wy−camY))·K/2·Z + H/2 + viewDY − wz·Z`. A world circle of radius r draws as an ellipse `r·Z` by `r·Z/2`. Joystick screen vectors are inverted back to world with `a = dx/K, b = 2dy/K → ((a+b)/2, (b−a)/2)`. `Z` is a zoom derived from `min(W,H)/500`, clamped 0.62–1.35, eased by tier and by the dock view.

**Solids.** `extrude(poly, z0, z1, side, top)` draws only viewer-facing side quads (normal·(1,1) > 0), shaded by normal, then the top face. Ships are hull polygon + inset deck + cabin boxes rotated by heading, so they foreshorten correctly. Depth sorting is by `wx + wy` for a short list of drawables (hut, palms, tree, tower, boat, pirate). The pier is a special case that sorts relative to the boat's side of it.

**Update order.** clock → input → boat physics → island/pier push-out → world clamp → range limit → net tow → schools + catching → dock + selling → rare → leviathan → sparks → pirate → gulls + dolphins → sharks → salvage → driftwood → zoom → wakes/particles/texts → camera.

**Draw order.** sea (abyss, lagoon diamond, depth rings, glints, range ring, island flats, dock ring) → leviathan → fish → rare → sharks → dolphins → wakes → net → buoys → salvage → driftwood → depth-sorted objects → birds → flying-fish particles → night mask → glow pass (bioluminescence, sparkles) → floating text → edge indicators → joystick.

**Night.** A half-resolution offscreen canvas is filled with the ambient colour, lights are punched out with `destination-out` radial gradients scaled by `dark`, and the result is multiplied onto the scene. Glow species then get a `screen` halo and per-fish bright dots. This replaced an additive glow that washed the boat out; do not go back to adding light on top of sprites.

**Steering around land.** `around(x, y, tx, ty, R)` returns a tangent waypoint when the straight line to a target would cross the island. Dolphins and the pirate use it, with `pushOut` as a backstop. Any new roaming entity needs the same.

**Debug hook.** `window.__np` exposes boat, net, schools, pirate, sharks, pods, rare, lev, and setters for clock, wood and build; `hold` and `coins` are getters, `wood` is a setter only, so read the wood count from the HUD pill `#wood`. The smoke test drives the game through it, and so do balance probes in headless Chromium (max the levels, set `build` and `wood`, drop a log on the boat, read the shop). Keep an equivalent in dev builds.

## Known debt (fix during the port, not before)

- Global mutable state in one closure. No entity interface, no layers, no event bus.
- Species are coupled by array index (`SHARK = 7`, night = 8–9, rares = 10–11, `RING_R` parallel array). Move to string ids and data files; migrate the save's `log[]` to a map.
- `refreshShop()` rebuilds paint swatches and log chips via `innerHTML` on every sale tick.
- Toasts (fixed 2026-09-17): `ui/toast.ts` is a queue with three priorities; danger interrupts, the rest wait up to four seconds and give up, and a message never repeats back to back. Pirate and shark warnings are danger; dolphins, tier, palace, the leviathan and the range edge are events; everything else is routine.
- Pier depth-sort hack. Tall palace pieces can overlap the hut at some camera positions.
- Fish heading is derived from frame-to-frame position delta; schools that come on screen snap for a frame.
- Leviathan path speed is non-uniform (squircle parameterisation).
- No pause beyond clamping dt to 50 ms. The trip survives a discarded tab (see Persistence); entities do not, so schools, sharks and the pirate reset on reload.
- Sound is raw oscillators by design. `audio/sfx.ts` (2026-09-17) names the twenty-five cues and locks each to the legacy numbers by test; the catch, sell, driftwood blip and dolphin whistle are keepers (owner and kid, 2026-09-16 and 17). Music and ambience are separate layers and never replace a cue.
- Dead code: `isDark`, `mq` (night used to follow the OS theme).
- The palace is small. It reads as a treehouse wearing a hat. It deserves its own art pass.
- Accessibility: canvas has a label and the shop is real buttons, but there is no reduced-motion path for screen shake or the day-cycle tint.

## Target architecture

Vite + TypeScript (strict), no game framework. Keep the custom canvas renderer: pillar 4 needs full control over how the look evolves, and the bespoke projection/extrusion code is small. Deploy to GitHub Pages. Vitest for pure logic, Playwright for the smoke test.

```
src/
  main.ts            boot, resize, loop
  core/              iso.ts  math.ts  rng.ts  color.ts
  data/              tuning.ts today, a verbatim move of every balance table; Phase 2
                     splits it into species.ts zones.ts upgrades.ts tiers.ts stages.ts paints.ts
  state/             save.ts today: reads and writes netprofit.v1 exactly as the prototype did,
                     with a legacy save in its tests; store.ts and the versioned format are Phase 2
  input/             joystick.ts  keys.ts  (done)
  world/             daycycle.ts, island.ts and schools.ts done; range.ts to come
  entities/          entity.ts (the contract), ship.ts, boat.ts, net.ts, dolphins.ts, pirate.ts, sharks.ts,
                     leviathan.ts, rare.ts, flotsam.ts, gulls.ts done
  render/            layers.ts done (the scene: one order, one call per layer, depth items for the solids);
                     sea.ts  solids.ts  ship.ts  fish.ts  night.ts
                     particles.ts  indicators.ts
  ui/                toast.ts done (queue with priorities); hud.ts  shop.ts
  audio/             sfx.ts (done; named cues locked to legacy by test)
legacy/net-profit.html   the reference build, untouched
index.html               the shipping game; identical to legacy until the port starts
tests/smoke.spec.ts      Playwright, drives the game through window.__np at 390x780
tests/unit/              Vitest, pure logic; first tests arrive with the first module
vite.config.ts           relative base, dev 4830, preview 4831, tailnet hostname allowed
playwright.config.ts     chromium (CI) and msedge (local fallback) projects, own server on 4831
biome.json               lint and format: LF, single quotes, 100 columns
.github/workflows/       ci.yml (typecheck, lint, unit, smoke) and deploy.yml (Pages)
```

`src/main.js` is the prototype script moved out of index.html so Vite serves and bundles it; it is excluded from Biome and not type-checked, and shrinks as Phase 1 splits it into the modules above. `core/iso.ts` is the first, with unit tests in `tests/unit/`; main.js keeps `px`, `py`, `dirToWorld` and `onScreen` as thin wrappers so call sites do not change.

Entity contract: `update(dt, world)`, `draw(ctx, view, layer)`, optional `depth()`. Render layers replace the hand-ordered draw list: `underwater, surface, solids(sorted), air, mask, glow, overlay`.

## Migration plan

**Phase 0, scaffold.** Repo, Vite, TS strict, lint, Pages deploy, `legacy/` copy. Port the Playwright smoke test. Done 2026-09-16: Vite + TypeScript strict, Biome, Vitest, Playwright (`tests/smoke.spec.ts`), CI and Pages workflows. The Pages source is set to GitHub Actions, so nothing deploys until `deploy.yml` reaches `main`; from then on the site is `dist/` only, which is the game and none of the docs.

**Phase 1, parity port.** Move code into modules with no behaviour changes. Step 1 done 2026-09-17: the inline script became the ES module `src/main.js`, verbatim, and index.html loads it with `type="module"`. Safe because the prototype was already a strict-mode IIFE with no inline handlers or load-event dependence. Step 2 done 2026-09-17: the projection became `core/iso.ts`, pure and unit-tested, behind wrappers in main.js. Step 3 done 2026-09-17: every balance table moved to `data/tuning.ts`, and a unit test reads the tables in this document and checks the code against them. Step 4 done 2026-09-17: `core/math.ts` (clamp, angDiff, smoothstep, the seeded rng, locked to the prototype's sequence), `core/color.ts`, `world/daycycle.ts` and `data/progression.ts` (tier, hull scale, range, paints, level cap), each unit-tested, the last two against this document's tables and percentages. Step 5 done 2026-09-17: `state/save.ts` parses and serializes `netprofit.v1`, with a real pre-trip save in its tests so old saves provably keep loading. Step 6 done 2026-09-17: `world/island.ts` holds the island, pier and dock geometry with `around` and `pushOut`, tested for the detour, its mirror and the push to the rim. Step 7 done 2026-09-17: the tow physics became `entities/net.ts`; its test runs the tow block from `legacy/net-profit.html` itself against the module on five hundred random states and requires bit-identical results, which is the side-by-side check for this move. Step 8 done 2026-09-17: `input/joystick.ts` and `input/keys.ts` hold the floating joystick and the keyboard vector with their DOM bindings, tested with fake event targets; the steering that consumes them is step 9. Step 9 done 2026-09-17: `entities/boat.ts` holds the steering, throttle, easing and move, with the same legacy-slice parity test as the net, so both halves of the feel are locked to the reference. WASD tuning shipped as a setting. Step 10 done 2026-09-17: `audio/sfx.ts` holds the oscillator plumbing and every cue by name, each locked to the legacy `tone()` lines by a test that runs those lines through a recorder. Done when: the smoke test passes, an existing `netprofit.v1` save loads, and side-by-side play at 390×780 feels identical (tow physics especially).

**Phase 2, data-driven content.** Species, zones, upgrades, tiers, stages, paints become data with string ids. Save v2 with migration. Balance values live in one place.

**Phase 3, systems.** Entity interface, render layers, toast queue, sfx cues, pause/visibility, reduced motion. Started 2026-09-17: the toast queue and sfx cues are done, and `entities/entity.ts` holds the contract (`update(dt, world)`, `draw(view, layer)`, optional `depth()`) with the dolphins as the first entity on it, moved verbatim and tested for roaming, joining, escorting and giving up. The game keeps one `world` and one `drawView` object in step each frame for entities to read. The pirate followed as the second entity: its whole state machine moved verbatim with tests for waking, spawning off an edge, chasing, refusing, stealing half the hold, breaking off and leaving; it draws itself on the solids, mask, glow and overlay layers through the draw view, and the game keeps the consequences (the toast, the cue, the fish actually leaving the hold) as callbacks. The sharks came third: the warning throttle lives in the entity, and the catch, the tear and the spill stay in the game as callbacks. The world grew the net, its width and level, the hold cap and the escort flag, and entities now read the live game through getters on the world object rather than a copy made each frame, so a callback that changes the hold is seen by the next shark in the same frame, as in the prototype. The entity tests share one world factory in tests/unit/helpers. The leviathan came fourth and was the simplest: it reads only the boat and tells the game when it passes beneath; the draw view gained the iso ellipse primitive so entities can path shadows on the water. The rare fish came fifth: the entity owns the wander, the eighty-second clock and the hold-full nudge throttle, and the day cycle asks it to spawn at dawn and dusk; the world learned the range, and the draw view the fish and star primitives. The entity draw tests share one counting draw view in tests/unit/helpers. Crates and driftwood came sixth as one Flotsam entity with two kinds: the kind carries the count, the ring bias, the pickup reach, the respawn window, the reward rule and the look, and the game pays out through a callback. They are placed once the world exists, since placement reads the range; the world learned the tier and the draw view the coin colour and the box and extrude solids. The gulls came last of the creatures: the four that trail a laden boat and the ones that circle healthy schools by day, drawn in the air layer through the bird primitive, asking nothing of the game. Every creature the prototype had is now on the contract; the schools themselves, the boat and the net stay in the game until world/schools.ts. Then the render layers: render/layers.ts holds the scene, one list of entities in one order (the prototype's update order: rare, leviathan, pirate, gulls, dolphins, sharks, crates, driftwood). The game calls scene.update once and scene.draw once per layer where the frame needs it, and takes the scene's depth items into its own sorted solids; crates and driftwood got their own layer, afloat, over the net. The one thing that is not verbatim: dolphins now draw beneath sharks on the odd frame they overlap, because one order serves both updating and drawing, and updating dolphins before sharks (so a shark sees this frame's escort) mattered more. The glow layer draws after the night mask with each entity setting and restoring its own composite, so the leviathan and the pirate set screen and the rare's sparkle does not. Content began with the jellyfish pools (the roadmap entry has the numbers): the first thing built from the brainstorm and the first entity written on the contract from scratch. The game gained a sweep gate on the count in the net, a docking shake-out, two soft cues, and a world flag, netFouled, that the rare reads. Then the vendors: data/vendors.ts holds the rules, pure and tested; the game holds the two buildings, the HUD chip, the sale hook, the dawn pick and the build toasts; the save gained market. Then the pets, with the dog first: the pirate gained an onProwl callback for the bark, the world the palace stage, and the pets sort with the pier through depth(). Then the field guide: data/guide.ts holds the pages and is held to the world builder's ring calls by a test; the save gained day and first. Then the whales, the first creature whose sound matters as much as its shape: the entity swims and breathes, the ambience sings from where they are, and the save gained whaleSeen. Then the shipwright: data/gear.ts holds the shelf and its rules, the world gained fineMesh and stealShare for the jellies and the pirate to read, and the save gained gear. Then the schools, the last big piece of the world still in the script: world/schools.ts builds them and sweeps them, held to the prototype bit for bit by two parity tests; the rings became data in tuning.ts, which the field guide now reads instead of pattern-matching the script. Then the mantas, the second spectacle creature, on three layers: a shadow under the water, the ray itself in the air while it leaps, and the spray on the surface. Then the first piece of the snook's arc, the Ten Cent Bridge as a place: geometry in world/island.ts, with the beach kept clear of everything that floats, sparkles or drifts. Then the snook itself and the second verb: fishing/snook.ts holds the cast, the odds and the fight, pure and tested; the game holds the button, the rod on the stick, the drawing, the trophies and the log. Then the deep, the first of the kid's list (the roadmap has it): world/island.ts holds how far it runs and what counts as past the buoys, tuning.ts the mahi-mahi and its ring, world/schools.ts builds the deep's schools from their own seed, and the pirate reads the buoys. Then the leviathan's guard, on the entity it already was: it watches for the crossing, rises, waits, walls and sinks, and the game keeps the fish, the toasts and the growl as callbacks. Then the kid's Cthuluviathan, a new entity on the contract: it sleeps, stirs, wakes, strikes, grabs and settles in entities/cthuluviathan.ts, with its city, its head and its tentacles drawn on four layers and marked at the screen's edge on the overlay; the save gained cthuluSeen. Then the anglerfish, on the contract the same way; the world gained dark, so a creature can keep to the night, and the save anglerSeen.
