Then the plants: world/plants.ts places them and render/plants.ts draws them, the bushes handed to the game's depth-sorted solids like the scene's. Then the chemistry suit and the Tar Anchorer: world/isle4.ts learned the tar's edge and the way in off the bow, the walker learned to swim, entities/anchorer.ts holds the boss on the contract and entities/tarling.ts the companion, render/isle4.ts draws the monster anywhere in any pose, and the save gained isle4Stage. Then the cat and the warrior: entities/pals.ts holds both as one kind of companion set up two ways, and main.js keeps what hurts them with what hurts the warlock. Then the Deep One: entities/deepone.ts holds it at the surface and entities/cthulhu.ts below, world/tower.ts the drowned temple as a room, the walker learned to be put ashore anywhere at once, and the save gained isle6Stage. Then island 7: world/isle7.ts holds where it is, its landing, post, tower, camp and portal, render/isle7.ts draws them with the portal dead or awake, the walker learned a fifth dock, main.js keeps a second camp of monkeys on the first one's callbacks, and the save gained isle7Seen. Then island 7's tower: world/tower.ts gained its three rooms, the sorcerer learned to call monkeys and to be tougher, main.js keeps the monkeys it calls on the roof and wakes the portal, and the save gained isle7Stage. Then Gigantis: entities/undead.ts holds a room of the undead as one entity on the contract (five kinds set up from a table, their swings, arrows and wisps, and the necromancers' skeletons), world/gigantis.ts who stands where and the way out, world/tower.ts the three rooms, render/gigantis.ts the courtyard, the halls and their doors, and the portal learned to be drawn anywhere. Then the throne room: entities/forgotten.ts holds the Forgotten One on the contract (his sword, skulls and ray), the undead learned his bones, a hit worth two and raising more than one, entities/merlocks.ts the warlock's merlocks, world/gigantis.ts the throne and the locked door, render/gigantis.ts both, and the save's isle7Stage reached 2. Then island 6: world/isle6.ts holds its wandering shore, its pier, its dock and what stands on it, render/isle6.ts draws it, the walker learned a fourth dock, and the save gained isle6Seen and chests6. Then island 5 and the Skeleton Shark King: world/isle5.ts and render/isle5.ts hold the reef, entities/sharkking.ts the King on the contract with the harpoon's mark and harpoon like the four, the game his last words, and the save gained isle5Seen and isle5Stage. Then the far deep: world/island.ts holds how far it runs, what counts as past the far buoys and how far a hull may go, tuning.ts the marlin and its ring, and world/schools.ts builds them from their own seed. Then island 3: world/isle3.ts holds where it is, the rafts, walks and jetty as boxes, the seaweed, the shacks, the drowned houses and the outline that keeps hulls off; render/isle3.ts draws it under the water, flat on it and standing up, handing the tower, the shacks and the lamps to the game's sorted solids; the walker learned a third dock and the planks and seaweed as dry land, plants.ts sea grass and kelp for it, tuning.ts the grouper and its rings, and the save gained isle3Seen. Then the tar island and the cutscene: world/isle4.ts and render/isle4.ts, the cutscene's timeline and captions in main.js. Before it, his boat: entities/warlockboat.ts keeps station with ship steering and fishes through the game's own catch. Before it, the warlock: entities/warlock.ts follows, blinks, casts and rests, and main.js finds his targets and what hurts him. Before him, the depths: entities/fighter.ts holds the swordsman and the demons as one fighter set up four ways, the monkeys learned to be called up and not come back, the walker a diving look, and world/tower.ts the hall, the demon dimension and its cage. Before them, island 3's tower: world/tower.ts learned its rooms and a list of towers with their first floor and roof, the sorcerer learned its ring, and main.js keeps each tower's dock, door, floors and boss together, with the save gaining isle3Stage. Before them, the turtles: entities/turtles.ts holds them, their breathing, the swim alongside and the fright, on the contract like the mantas; the game keeps the toasts, the cue and the counts, and the save gained turtleSeen and turtleSwims. Before them, the harpoon: data/harpoon.ts holds its reach, the resolves, the trophies and the prizes; each of the four leviathans learned mark (where a harpoon would strike it now, or nothing) and harpoon (take a hit, and say whether that drove it off), keeping its own resolve; spears.ts learned the line back to the bow; the game picks the nearest target, fires, draws the pips and the ring, and pays out; the save gained driven. Before it, the tower and its boss: world/tower.ts holds the rooms, the door and the stairs, entities/sorcerer.ts the boss, its ring, its bolts, its swoops and its fall; the monkeys learned a home and a count so each floor is a small camp of its own; the walker learned the rooms' floors as dry land; the game keeps the floor the figure is on, the climb, the prize, the flag on the tower and the way back to the boat, and the save gained towerTaken. Before it, the monkey camp: entities/monkeys.ts holds the monkeys, their chasing, bonks and coconuts, the hits and the chest, drawing each monkey for the game's sorted solids; world/isle2.ts the camp's layout; the world gained figure, the walker knockOut and a blink; the game keeps the hearts, the masks and the prize, and the save gained masks. Before it, the spear: data/spear.ts holds its levels, entities/shallows.ts island 2's shore fish and entities/spears.ts the throws in flight, which always land; the game sells what they take, and the save gained spear. Before it, boat health and the gulper: data/health.ts holds the numbers, entities/gulper.ts the eel, its hunt and its body, and the game does the damage, the swallowing and the spitting out as callbacks; the save gained gulperSeen. Before them, flags: data/flag.ts holds the palette, patterns and emblems and draws a flag as shapes for the canvas and SVG alike; the save gained flag. Before them, island 2: world/isle2.ts holds where it is and what stands on it, the walker learned more than one dock (each with its berth and landing) and island 2's dry land and props, the schools builder a centre other than home, and tuning.ts the parrotfish and its ring. Before it, the dog ashore: pets.ts follows the figure's trail with the walker's own slide and sorts the dog among the solids with the walker's rules when it is off the planks; the world gained ashore and the save petted. Before it, the walk ashore: entities/walker.ts holds dry land, what stands on it, the slide along walls, the berth, the hops and the footprints, and works out where the figure sorts among the solids (in front of a solid when it is past a face that looks at the viewer, behind when past one that looks away, with the pier and the bridge's parts on their own depths); the game draws it in its own sorted list rather than through depth(), since that needs the pier's depth and the dog's. world/island.ts gained the bridge's parts and their depths, the umbrella and the toll sign, so the drawing and the walker share them.

**Phase 4, new content.** See roadmap. One feature per PR, playable at every commit.

## Working on it

- `npm run dev` serves the game at http://localhost:4830/ with hot reload; `npm run preview` serves the production build on 4831. Both ports are pinned strictly because other projects on the same machine use Vite's defaults. Never fall back to 5173 or 4173.
- Phone testing: `npm run dev -- --host`, then open the Network URL Vite prints. A tailnet hostname works too, since `*.ts.net` is allowed through Vite's host check. Reaching the dev server over the LAN on Windows needs an inbound firewall rule scoped to TCP 4830 on the private profile, not a blanket rule for node.
- `npm run test:e2e` runs the smoke test in Chromium against the production build on its own server. `npx playwright test --project=msedge` uses the Edge already on Windows when the Chromium download will not complete.
- Balance probes beat playing to flagship: drive the game in headless Chromium through `window.__np`, exactly as the driftwood retune was checked.
- The tester panel, added 2026-10-11 because the owner could not get past the Skeleton Shark King and wanted to see what lies beyond him: open the game with `#tester` at the end of its link and a dashed Tester button appears at the bottom of the shop, on that device only (`localStorage["netprofit.tester"]`), until `#tester-off`. It lists the story's steps in order (`src/ui/tester.ts`); tapping one marks it and every step before it beaten, never takes a stage back, saves, and reloads, because companions, bosses and doors all follow the stages on load. Its extras add 10,000 coins, 100 driftwood, clear all the sea map's fog at once (added 2026-10-13, by popular demand: islands not yet found show on it unnamed), the Reaper's ice fishing kit, the Reaper's egg (or the egg ready to hatch at once), the otter, Neil and the naga, and mend the boat. The egg buttons leave a Reaper on land who was never beaten asleep by his nest, and another button brings one already beaten back there for a rematch (both kept in the save as reaperRematch; beaten again he goes, with his prize again), added 2026-10-13 when a game rebuilt with the tester after the kid's home-screen game was lost had his egg but no Reaper to fight. The extras sit at the bottom of the panel, where a toast would be hidden behind it, so each button says itself that it worked (Added ✓, green, for a moment), and one you already have says Have it (added 2026-10-12, when the owner could not tell an Add had done anything, and found the otter and Neil missing). It is a real save change: a skipped game is that game from then on. A skip never skips a cutscene (added 2026-10-13, the kid: "cutscenes are important"): island 3's step leaves it at stage 3, so the tar monster's scene plays the next time the boat is out and moves it on to 4; and a skip past the Old One owes his rising (oldOwed in the save), which plays in full, soul armour and all, the next time the figure goes into the ruins, ending with him sinking back into the pool ("you beat the Old One long ago") instead of the fight, with no prize. The egg's hatching already plays from its extras. Past the Heron (added 2026-10-19, when the steps had stopped at island 9 and everything since could only be played to): island 10's giant pelican and its three crows, which open the far far sea; island 11's Heron's brother, freeing Caterman and his swan paddle boat; the skull key and the Forgotten One beaten again in the crypt; the bridge's stealth mission 1, for the chemistry boat and the invisibility potion; the tar leader; and mission 2's tar eel, with the evil monkey caged. These steps mark only who is beaten and what was won, never a place seen, so island 11, the bridge, the tar island and Skull Island still say what they are when first found; and the crypt's step leaves the Forgotten One kneeling there, his castle tour (a cutscene, and the scanner after it) still to play.
- Cutscenes lost (fix, 2026-10-21), the owner's ("I used #tester to skip to content but it didn't show me cut scenes"). Two ways one was lost. A tap skipped the tar monster's, the Heron's escape and the Shark King's last words the moment they began, so a thumb still steering the boat, or firing the harpoon at the King, ended one before it was seen: now a tap skips one only once it has been on a second (main.js CINE_HOLD; the egg's already waited until it was in). And the tester's Shark King step marked him dead, so his last words never played: now a skip past him owes them (kingOwed in the save, missing in old saves read as nothing owed), and the next time the boat sails within his reef's notice (700 of island 5) he is up dying 150 ahead of it and says them, as if just beaten, with no prize again (entities/sharkking.ts dieAt). The tester panel lists the cutscenes still to see and where each plays (ui/tester.ts owedScenes): the tar monster and the Heron's escape the next time you are out on the boat, the King's last words at island 5, the Old One rising in his ruins, and the Forgotten One showing you his castle in the crypt.
- Play on another device, added 2026-10-11 because the owner's dad wanted his phone game on his laptop. There are no accounts, so the game travels in a link: the shop's "Play on another device" packs the save into `#game=…` (`src/state/share.ts`: deflate-raw through `CompressionStream` and base64url, a code starting `z`; plain base64url starting `j` where a browser cannot compress). A late-game save makes a link of about 1,200 characters. Opened, the link shows both games (day, coins, the last boss past) and asks before it replaces the game on that device; loading stops the old game saving over it as the page goes. The panel also takes a link pasted in, because an iPhone's home-screen game keeps its own storage apart from Safari's and opens no links; `#tester` pasted there works too. The `#…` is read on load and on `hashchange`, and tidied from the address bar once read.
- Online saves, added 2026-10-16, the owner's call: anyone who finds the game, so no accounts and nothing personal (the save holds nothing a player types), and a save code, with the free plan's pausing accepted. Each game has a save code, four words from 256 short, easy-to-spell ones from the game's world (`src/state/cloud.ts`, about four billion codes), made once and kept in the save (`saveCode`); a game from before codes makes one on its first load. The game puts itself online under it, squeezed as for a link: a game already here at once on load, then at most once a minute while it is played (every save hands it on), and as the page goes. The other end is in the owner's waterways Supabase project (her pick: the free plan runs two projects, and waterways already runs), kept apart from that app: a table `net_profit.saves` (code, game, earned, when) in a schema the API does not serve, with row security on and no policies, and two functions, `public.np_save_game` and `public.np_load_game`, the only way in (`supabase/migrations/net_profit_saves.sql`, applied there as the migration net_profit_saves). The table refuses a game that has earned less than the one it holds, so an old copy on another device cannot write over a newer one; refused, the game offers the newer one (Played on further on another device), once, and asks before it loads it. The Play on another device card shows the code first, with Copy and how the copy online is doing (saved just now, minutes ago; played on further elsewhere; cannot be reached, and it keeps trying), then the link as before, which needs nothing online; its box takes a code, typed any way (case, spaces, dashes), or a link, and what comes of it is said under the box (a toast would be behind the card). Once a week, the kid's yes to a reminder, starting the game toasts its code to write down (`codeTold`); a new game waits its first week, a game from before codes hears it at once. Offline, or with the project asleep, nothing changes but that the copy online waits. The page's key is the project's publishable key, public by design. The database's security advisor flags the two functions as callable without signing in (meant: they are the only door) and the table as having row security but no policies (meant: nobody reads it directly). A game is at most 64 KB online (a real one is a few). The smoke tests never reach the project: each is cut off from it, and the one about it answers for it.
- Installable, added 2026-10-11 because the owner wanted the game as an app on her phone: `public/manifest.webmanifest` (standalone, with the 192, 512 and maskable 512 icons), the iOS home-screen meta tags (full screen, under the status bar, which the safe-area insets already allow for), and `public/sw.js`, registered in production builds only. The service worker fetches the page from the network first, so a deploy arrives on the next open, and falls back to its kept copy with no signal; the hashed scripts come from the cache, and each fresh page prunes the ones it no longer uses. Lookups ignore `Vary`, because `vite preview` varies by Origin and a module script request would otherwise miss. It never touches saves. On an iPhone the home-screen app keeps its own storage apart from Safari's: carry a game across with "Play on another device". Updates (2026-10-12, after the owner asked whether the home-screen app would pick up new versions): a phone pauses the app in the background and brings back the same page, so coming back to the front, and every quarter of an hour while visible, the game fetches its page with `cache: 'no-store'` (which the worker passes straight to the network) and compares the hashed script it names with the running one (`src/state/update.ts`). Newer: on the title screen it reloads at once; mid-trip a toast says so and the shop offers "A new version is ready" at the next dock, saving before it reloads, so nothing at sea is cut short. The worker revalidates the page with the server on every navigation (`cache: 'no-cache'`), so a page held a few minutes in the browser's own cache after a deploy cannot bring the old game back.
- The app icon is the picture the owner and her kid made for it together on 2026-10-11 (`art/app-icon-source.png`): a white and red boat on bright blue waves, a buoy beside it, three fish below. It replaced a night scene drawn the same day, which it beats for being theirs. The picture draws its own rounded tile on white; `scripts/icons.mjs` cuts the tile square, fills its corners with the blue just inside them (iOS rounds the corners itself and fills anything see-through with black, which is why the very first icon showed on black), and writes every size into `public/`: the 180 home-screen icon, 192, 512, the maskable 512 (the same picture; the boat and fish sit well inside Android's crop) and the 32px tab icon. Run `node scripts/icons.mjs` after changing the picture (`CHROMIUM=` points it at a Chromium).
- CI runs typecheck, lint, unit and smoke on every PR. Merging to `main` builds `dist/` and deploys it to GitHub Pages. Only the game and its player's guide ship; none of the docs in the repo are served.
- The player's guide (`public/guide.html`), added 2026-10-23 because the owner asked whether there was one, with her calls: for her kid and for grown-ups alike, the spoilers folded, and shipped with the game. It is one plain page in the game's colours and font, light and dark, opened over the game in a frame from "Player's guide" on the title card and in the shop (the game waits under it, and the installed app, which has no back button, keeps its way back), or on its own at `guide.html`, where it has a link back. The service worker keeps it for playing with no signal. Every place, boss, friend and side quest is a fold: open it for the hint, then "Show the answer" for the rest. The surprises (the drone and all that comes of it, the mermaids, their pearls and paint, the siren charm, the sirens, Grampy's Snook) live only in the Secrets fold at the bottom, each folded again, and are never named anywhere else in the guide, not even in a fish table or a list of daily resets. A smoke test reads the page with every fold shut and fails if one of those words shows; add a new surprise's words to it. **Every PR that changes something a player meets updates the guide in the same PR**: a new place, boss, friend, price or button. Things not built yet go under Coming soon, never as if they were there. The guide says "you" to the player and names no real people.
- Where the tuning tables above and `legacy/net-profit.html` disagree, the tables win and the port reproduces the tables.
- Keyboard steering has two feels and a setting, because on 2026-09-17 the owner picked drive and her kid picked point, and both were right for how they play. Legacy points with the keys, unsmoothed; neither feel is that, so this is the one deliberate difference from legacy in how the boat is driven. The joystick and the physics under all three are bit-identical to legacy by test.
- Questions for her kid go in a tab of their own in his document of lists, where he types his answers himself: the owner's standing word, 2026-10-15, so add the tab and put them there without asking. Keep them short and plain, grouped, one per line, in his words where possible, and read his spelling generously. Adding the tab needs the Google Docs connector; with only Drive's, the doc can be read but not changed, so hand the owner the questions to paste in instead.
- The people in this doc are "the owner" and "her kid" on purpose. No real names or personal email anywhere in the repo, commits or PRs; the repo commits as the GitHub noreply address.

## Roadmap (ideas discussed; the built ones say so)

Near:
- **Ninjas.** The night threat, opposite of pirates: silent, empty your crab pots, never seen. Later hireable as palace guards and sushi chefs. Possible late-game faction choice, pirates vs ninjas. Retired (2026-10-23, the owner's call): the ninjas are the Endless Isle's now, a side you fight for, and the faction choice is that island's.
- **The Endless Isle.** The kid's island 13 (2026-10-22, through the owner; he called it Doom Island, and the owner renamed it the Endless Isle on 2026-10-23, after the endless battles between the pirates and the ninjas; Doom Island is to be a separate island, below): past the white buoys, two islands that mirror each other perfectly, pirates on one and ninjas on the other. The ninjas have a snow-peaked mountain with a skiing mini-game; the pirates have crazy treehouses to get round, a puzzle like Donkey Kong. You pick a side to fight for, either or both: battles where you steal a flag or hold several bases, as in old World of Warcraft, with a badge picked on joining (healer, damage or tank, themed for each side) and abilities unlocked the more you fight (the owner's example: a pirate healer's first badge an instant heal, the second a heal over time, the third a group heal). The pirates give you a parrot minion that yells "expletive deleted!" and other censored pirate puns; the ninjas a ninja-bun, a little blue bunny in a ninja outfit with a sword. Her words: a huge mini-game inside the game, to be planned out and made to shine. The plan (bots for the other players, the far far sea's left corner, the mirror built once and reflected, five a side, three roles with three badges each per side at the same numbers, marks toward badges, the minions, both mini-games, and eleven pieces in order) she took as it stood (2026-10-23: "All your picks, start with piece 1"), so her calls are: bots, not real people online; your companions sit the battles out, only your side's minion fighting; in battle your badge sets your hearts and weapon, story armour and the spear staying outside; five a side; the parrot's lines go to her before they ship (the seven the plan listed passed with it); both mini-games open to anyone who has landed; and the ninjas who rob crab pots unseen are retired (above). Why they fight, the kid's answer: "to stimulate the economy". Pieces one to ten, the place, the leaders with the minions, Capture the Flag, the roles with Badge I, the Ninja Slalom's first run, Badges II and III, Hold the Forts, the Treehouse Climb's four stages, and the Ninja Slalom's other two runs with the medals and the yeti, are built (under What exists today); next the side rewards. His answers in his tab (2026-10-23): the parrot is Jacks Parrow, the ninja-bun Bun-San ("something cute"); gorillas roll the barrels down the treehouses, like Donkey Kong; a yeti chases you down the black run; the pirates' flag is the Jolly Roger and the ninjas' a throwing star on a field; and yes, the kraken on foot is on the tar island. The pirate captain is Captain Cappy and the ninja master Master Sensei, his answer to the last question in his tab. The owner (2026-10-23): the wreck on the sandbar in the middle will be a dungeon, in time.
- **The Garden Isle.** The owner's (2026-10-24): "a garden isle. We want to replicate this game", Grow a Garden on Roblox, on an island of its own. Its loop, from the game's wiki: your own plot; seeds from a stall whose stock restocks on a timer, rarer seeds in stock less often; plants that grow in real time, some picked once and some fruiting again and again; fruit sold at a stand for its kind, its weight (value goes with the square of it) and its mutations, Gold (×20, 1 in 100) and Rainbow (×50, 1 in 1,000) as it ripens and weather ones such as Wet in rain and Shocked by lightning, which add; a gear shop of watering cans and sprinklers; pets hatched from eggs. Their trading, stealing, paid restocks and events stay out: there are no other players. Their invented crop names stay theirs; ours are our own. The plan, an HTML page in eleven pieces, and her calls on it (2026-10-24): it grows in real time, closed or open, from saved timestamps, a multi-harvest plant holding 3 or 4 ripe fruit and then waiting, nothing withering and nothing notifying; it lies in the far deep's left corner, on the way to the Endless Isle; it may pay about what the boat does, more than the plan's first guardrail (a full plot of the best crop left overnight about 3 to 5 holds of abyss fish, 30,000 to 50,000, top seeds up to about 50,000, and tended by hand about the boat's rate a minute, never more; a test holds the table to it, and the throttle is the stall, its rarest seeds in stock once in a hundred restocks or so); pets and eggs come last and stay in the garden. And the rest of the plan's picks: the stall and the gear shed restock at every dawn (every 5 minutes of play), the stock saved so a reload cannot reroll it; Shocked is ×10, one bolt a storm, not their ×100; Gold and Rainbow at their numbers; Moonlit (×2) ripening at night and Twilight (×3) at dawn or dusk; weather mutations only while you play; the basket sells only at the isle's stand. Her second round the same day: tie it back to the fishing, both ways, hard enough to be worth it and paying into both. So a lily pond on the isle, cast from its edge on foot: any fruit is bait for its ordinary carp, and its legendary, caught only by hand, rises only for a Moonlit fruit of mythic tier or above cast after dark, once a night at most; hard bait, a fair fight (her call: snook-level, a tell before each run, tested with a person's timing, since a miss costs the bait); it sells for about 5,000, goes on a wall with a guide page, and drops a seed never sold at the stall, the garden's best multi-harvest crop. The other way, fish for the beds: kept from the hold in a compost bin at the isle's dock (10 at most) instead of sold, and buried as a bed is planted, a common fish growing it a quarter faster, a dawn or dusk rare raising its Gold chance, a deep fish making its fruit bigger; and the kid's top crop sprouts only over a legendary-class fish. A fish buried or a fruit used as bait is one not sold, so neither side gets free money. Pets went to a pet system of their own (her call, the same round: one system, not two), with pet boarding, pet battles and pets a kid draws on the phone in-game, saved as small strokes; another session plans and builds it on `claude/pet-system`, and the garden's eggs will hatch into it. The pieces, in order: the place (built, part one above); first harvest (built, part two above); every seed and the dawn restock (built, part three above); size and giants (built, part four above); Gold and Rainbow (built, part five above); weather on the garden (built, part six above); the gear shed; the lily pond; the pond's legendary; fish for the beds; more beds and a bigger basket; the garden journal in the field guide; the kid's crops, then a tuning pass. Planting and picking go on the one action button, as island 10's "Pick an apple" does, with a seed tray above it in the plot, and held down while walking a row to plant or pick each bed crossed. Questions for the kid, through the owner: the island's name; one or two crops of his own for the very top; who keeps the three stalls; up to three more mutations; whether anything should bother the garden, and what scares it off; the pond's legendary fish, its name and look, and what its seed grows into.
- **Doom Island.** A separate island, the kid's, which he will design next (the owner, 2026-10-23); its first name went to what is now the Endless Isle. Nothing of it is decided yet: where it is, what is on it, who lives there. Its save key is not to be `doom`, which old saves used for the Endless Isle.
- **Crab pots.** Drop, leave, return. Passive income and route planning. The thing ninjas rob.
- **Line fishing for legendaries.** Second verb: one giant shadow per zone, timing minigame, trophy mounted on the palace. Nets are volume; lines are single targets. The first legendary is the snook, below.
- **Ambience.** Built 2026-09-17 as designed here; first tuned 2026-09-18, when the owner heard the pad as an engine hum on desktop speakers and no waves at all: a phone speaker cannot play 87 Hz, so the first mix had only ever been heard without its pad, which on full-range speakers masked the swell. The pad went up an octave and down by half, the swell's cutoff up, and the surf came in. Check every sound change on both. Procedural, on its own bus that ducks 40% under every cue for half a second so nothing cute gets stepped on. Waves: brown noise low-passed near 700 Hz on a slow wandering swell, a surf layer (a band-pass sweeping 650 to 1,100 Hz under two low-pass lids at 1,250 Hz, cresting with each swell, always on, which is what reads as water on any speaker; measured offline it leads the mix at a crest with the whistle's band 17 dB beneath it), two LFO phases for stereo, a brighter hiss that rises with boat speed, a quick lap inside the dock ring, lower and quieter at night. Gulls: a triangle sliding 1.1 kHz to 700 Hz with a fast tremolo, one to three syllables, only from birds on screen and awake, one cry every five to twelve seconds, silent for two seconds after an escort starts. The calm layer: three detuned triangles on F3, C4 and F4 under a slow chorus, low-passed at 800 Hz, an A4 at dawn and dusk, two notes at night; rooted on F because the dolphin whistle ends on C7 and D7. Wind chimes near shore (owner, 2026-09-17): sparse F-pentatonic pings with long decays and a couple of bright partials, fading in within about 400 units of the island and the pier, panned toward it, never more than one every few seconds. Dolphin chirps (kid, 2026-09-17): soft single chirps, quieter than the whistle, from a pod within a few hundred units every several seconds with jitter, panned toward it, an occasional double while escorting, and none within two seconds of the escort whistle. Everything stays out of the whistle's 1.4 to 2.4 kHz band.
- **Palace art pass.** Wings, rope bridge to the hut, dock gate, lights. Make stage 5 worth 258 driftwood.
- **Leviathan as a gate.** Started 2026-09-28: it guards the buoys (under Creatures). It should become the thing between you and the next island once there is one past the deep.
- **Stages 4 and 5 should unlock something.** Done 2026-09-18: the watchtower opens the fishmonger and the dome the smokehouse (Vendors, under What exists today). Boat-side rewards for the two stages are still open.
- **Background music.** A layer under the sfx with its own volume, sharing the mute toggle. Tracks load from `assets/music/` and the game stays silent when the folder is empty, so CI and Pages work without it. The folder is gitignored on purpose: the placeholder tracks there are a commercial soundtrack and cannot ship. Needs licensed or original music before it goes live.

Creatures (brainstormed 2026-09-17, in the order they should be built):

- **Jellyfish pools.** Built 2026-09-18 as entities/jellies.ts. One bloom of twenty to forty jellies drifts at 14 a second, its heading wandering, keeping 240 off the shore ring and 300 off the rim; the jellies pulse, the bloom is lit in the night mask and glows through the glow pass. A moving, whole net that passes within half its width plus 6 of a jelly scoops it: the first scoop toasts and squelches, the school sweep and the rare catch stop while any are in the net, the net shows them as pale blobs, and docking shakes them out with a count and three quick shakes. Scooped jellies regrow after a minute. Cosy with teeth. The shipwright sells the fine mesh that lets a net pass clean (2026-09-18).
- **Manta rays, for the owner.** Built 2026-09-18 as entities/mantas.ts. A squadron of three to five glides the middle rings, 1,200 to 1,700 out, at 46 a second, clockwise, the other way round from the whales, in a loose V behind the biggest. Each is a kite with beating wingtips and a whip of a tail, drawn as a shadow under the water. With the boat within 750 of the leader, every 14 to 30 seconds one of them leaps: it rises 78 off its shadow over 1.7 seconds, shows a pale belly as it banks, and comes down in a ring of spray with a low whump (heard within 900, with an octave on top so a phone can play it). The net slides off them. The boat within 420 is a sighting: a toast the first time, a gold chip, a page in the guide, and mantaSeen in the save. Purely spectacle: the kind of thing you point at when someone else is holding the phone.
- **Whales, for the kid.** Built 2026-09-18 as entities/whales.ts and a voice in the ambience. A mother (58 long) and her calf cruise round the island between 1,750 and 2,250 out at 24 a second, the calf on station behind and beside her. They travel as shadows and come up to breathe for four and a half seconds with a spout and a slate back, the mother every 11 to 18 seconds and the calf more often. The net slides over them; nothing catches or harms them. The boat within 450 of the mother is a sighting: a toast the first time, a gold chip in the log and a page in the guide. They sing when in earshot, 1,400 by day and 2,300 at night: the mother a phrase of two to four glides between 110 and 420 Hz every 14 to 26 seconds, the calf answering a second or two later between 260 and 620 Hz, into a shared echo. The roadmap's 80 to 400 Hz would have been silent on a phone, so each voice carries overtones a phone can play, and every partial stays under the dolphin whistle's 1.4 kHz; a test holds them to it.
- **Snook, for the owner's dad.** Built 2026-09-19, his birthday. The first fish taken on a line, in fishing/snook.ts. Silver, a black line down its side, yellow fins. It holds in the shadow of the Ten Cent Bridge's far abutment at dusk, night and dawn; a net towed over it finds nothing but a splash. Tie up within 170 of the spot on the south side, all but stopped, and a Cast button appears: ten coins a cast, which is how the bridge earns its name. The boat holds still and the thumb works the rod: hold to pull when it sulks, let go when it runs. Any press pulls in full, however far it drags, and so does any held key. Pull through a run and the line parts; pull too little and it reaches the piling. Before every run but the first it shakes its head for 0.45 seconds, throwing spray, with three ticks of the reel, and the word over the tension bar turns from Hold to Let go; it still pulls like a sulk while it does. That tell was added 2026-09-24, when the owner reported the snook could not be caught, and she was right: the fight had only been played by a test hand that eased off on the very frame a run began. A thumb a third of a second late, through the game's stick smoothing, pulled through the start of every run, the line ratcheted tighter each cycle, and it parted before the fish tired. It landed one short in two hundred and no giants; in the real game a hand that late lost five landable fish of five to the line. That was not enough. The owner still could not catch it, and two more things were wrong. The pull was how far the stick was dragged, and a thumb that dragged under two thirds of the ring lost every fish to the piling; a natural hold on a phone is a short drag. And a hand that did exactly what the words said, holding on a sulk and letting go for a run, lost most shorts to the piling anyway, because a run took back about as much line as a whole sulk of pulling won (the test hand had only won by keeping a little pressure on through each run, which a thumb on glass cannot do). So any press is a full pull, and the reel pulls at 48 (was 34), half again as fast as a run, so a sulk wins back more than a run takes. In the real game, a thumb dragging 15 px a third of a second late, doing what the words said, lost five landable fish of five to the piling before and landed five of five after. The hooked fish is drawn on the line from the abutment to the boat, as far out as it has been let run, not at a fixed spot south of the abutment, where it sat under the hull for most of the fight. The honest part, and the owner's numbers: every cast decides before the strike what is on the line. One in ten is a snook that can be landed, and most of those are shorts (18 to 27 inches) that go back; one in fifty is inside the slot (28 to 32) and big enough to keep, and goes up on the hut's wall; one keeper in five is the giant, Grampy's Snook (40 to 46), over the slot, which goes back after the photo and hangs as a gilt frame beside the mount, an Easter egg with its own guide page once landed. On every other cast the snook makes a last run no rod could stop. The tests play it with a person who does only what the words say: the thumb is down or up, everything reaches it a third of a second late, give or take a tenth, and the rod follows it as it does in the game, with no partial pressure and no saving a fish from the piling. That hand lands every landable short and keeper and about four giants in five; half a second late it lands the same, since the giant gets away by stamina, not by reflexes; the same hand that waits for the run instead of the tell lands under one short in ten, so reading the tell is the skill. The giant runs at 36 (was 34) so that it stays the one that gets away; this entry used to promise five in six. A heavy hand always parts the line and a timid one always loses it to the piling; a test of twenty thousand casts by that person holds the odds. Every miss ends the same way: "The snook is still under the bridge." The words over the fish say which miss it was: "The line parted", "Into the piling", or, on the last run no rod could stop, "Never coming in". Those last words were added 2026-09-26: the owner kept losing to the piling and could not tell her own misses from the nine casts in ten nobody lands, which all reach the piling on the third run, about six seconds after the strike. The bridge is named for one the owner's family crossed to reach the beach.

Dinner brainstorm (2026-09-17, the husband, the owner and the kid; my notes in the same breath, decisions still the owner's):

- **Seining.** The husband and the owner both want it, and it is the strongest idea here: drop a buoy, drive a loop around a school, come back to the buoy, and everything inside is yours. It is the sweep grown up, which keeps pillar 1 at the centre, and it finally gives the seiner tier its meaning. Skill, not volume: a clean tight loop catches more than a lazy wide one. My pick for island 2's verb, and the owner's (2026-10-10): it comes with island 2's base, its second stage after the hut. Built 2026-10-10 (the Seining entry above).
- **Storms.** Roll in from an edge with a warning (gulls flee, the pad drops out, the swell builds in the ambience layer), last a minute or two, slow the boat, widen its turns and push schools deep; then the reward, schools boiling in the shallows after. Counters to buy: ballast, or shelter in the dock ring. Lightning and rain are island 2's renderer step, with the reduced-motion path from known debt. Already on the Further list; promoted. Built 2026-10-10 past the buoys only, the owner's call (the Storms entry above): ballast is the counter, and the boil is the reward.
- **Sirens and mermaids.** The owner's, and a matched pair. Mermaids are a dusk-and-night sparkle rare on the far rocks whose song takes over the pad for a while and leaves a gift (a pearl in the log, a hull paint). Sirens are the same rocks at night with teeth: the song pulls the wheel toward the rocks and the counter is speed, a dolphin escort, or a bought charm. Island 2's night threat, the sea's answer to the ninjas. Started 2026-10-22 with the mermaids (under What exists today): her calls were their tune sung back for a pearl, five pearls strung into the charm against the sirens, the rocks off island 2's far shore, and the sirens the kid's to design once he has found the mermaids. Done the same day: the charm, and the sirens, the kid's Greek ones (both under What exists today).
- **Coast guard.** The husband's. Take it as the counter to the pirate, not as an enforcer: a cutter on a patrol route that pirates will not approach, and a radio upgrade to call it on a cooldown. No quotas or licences; that is teeth against the player with nothing to buy back. Built 2026-10-12 as the radio alone, after the owner's call: one call a day (her pick over pirates never coming near, which would have made the pirate stop mattering once bought, and over paying per call), pirates only, not the Heron's hired boats, whose fight is the harpoon. The call button shows only while the pirate is about, at sea in home water, with today's call unspent; on the press, like the throw button, since an iPhone fires no click for a second finger while the first steers. `entities/coastguard.ts`; the pirate's `held` flag turns it away.
- **Vendors on the island.** The husband's. Built 2026-09-18: the fishmonger (watchtower) pays double for his pick of the day and the smokehouse (palace dome) half again for tuna, goldfin and shark; both are buildings on the beach south of the pier, and the fish fly to them. The rules live in data/vendors.ts. The cannery stays on the Further list. This is the fix the balance note asked for.
- **Charters.** The husband's: take people out for coins and bring back no fish. A different loop, not a multiplier, and the creatures are the destinations: whales at night, the mantas' leap, the leviathan from a safe distance. Sharks scare the passengers and cost the fare. Island 2. Built 2026-10-10 from island 6's charter office, as the owner mapped it (the Charters entry above).
- **Cast netting.** The owner's. A tap throws a round net a short way for a small instant catch, for shallows and tight spots the tow cannot reach. Keep it a sidearm with a cooldown so the sweep stays the game. Mid island 1 or island 2.
- **Pets.** The kid's, for the base and later islands, and the right way to make the island a place. Started 2026-09-18: entities/pets.ts holds the pets, and the dog is the first, earned by the tree platform, pottering on the pier and barking at the horizon when the pirate sails in. Its shape is a stand-in until the kid's drawing. Still his to design: the cat that keeps gulls off the catch (the gulls have nothing to steal yet), the crab from the illustration, whatever else he draws, earned by palace stages and rare discoveries.
- **Pets, one system.** The owner (2026-10-24): "pet battles and pet boarding and a more structured pet system", and pets the kid draws on the phone. One system for every pet, the Garden Isle's egg pets included (moved here from that plan). The plan (an artifact for the owner, nine pieces in order, each a PR and playable) she took with these calls: boarding is a pet house on the home island, where pets not walking with you live and train in real time while you are away, with no hunger or sadness ever; battles are real time and never turn-based (his rule, the second list), one on one in a ring, you steering your pet with the stick, a bite and a special, the other pet's big moves shown first in red, a ladder of five trainers and a champion; the book indexes the companions rather than rebuilding them (their code and saves stay as they are), and the leggy muffins are in it; one walking pet at a time beside the companions. Taken as proposed: four drawn pets at a time, stored as simplified strokes on a 64 grid with an ink pot as the size cap (about 420 characters a drawing at most), so the copy-my-game link stays small; eggs hatch from saved timestamps in 10 minutes, an hour or four hours (`layEgg`, the Garden Isle's entry point); everything on the Pets shelf can battle, companions cannot; the pad can redraw the dog and the otter, stand-ins until the kid's drawings; the pet house on the home island's beach. The pieces: the pet book (built 2026-10-24), the drawing pad (built 2026-10-24), your walking pet (built 2026-10-24), eggs, the pet house, training while away, pet battles with the first trainer, the ladder, and the kid's own pets. His questions are in his doc's pets tab.
- **Scuba and spearfishing.** The husband's. A whole underwater level with sharks that matter is a second game, and per pillar 4 that is what a later island is for. Island 3's verb, with a counter for the sharks before it ships. Not before. Built 2026-10-10 as THE DEEP (the trench, spearfishing, its treasure, the lurker) and the wreck dive at island 5 with its bone sharks, the spear being the counter.
- **Protected animals.** The husband's: endangered animals to avoid, with a fine if you catch one. Keep the avoiding, reshape the fine. Sea turtles, manatees and sawfish drift near the schools; net one and the catch stops while it is released, a few fish spill, and the coast guard's dock inspection finds bycatch, which costs the clean-catch bonus the fishmonger pays for fish caught without any. A coin fine is loss that is never won back, which is the one thing pillar 5 forbids; a lost bonus stings and leaves nothing permanent. The buyable counter is the real one: a turtle excluder for the net, from the shipwright. Manatees are Florida, which matters here.
- **A field guide, for fish caught by hand.** The owner's. Built 2026-09-18 as far as the water allows: the catch log opens into a book (data/guide.ts for the pages, a full-screen planks panel in the game) with a page per species: a line in the game's voice, where its schools ring the island and which boat reaches them, when it is about, what it is worth, how many you have landed and the day of the first; an uncaught species keeps its name and line to itself but still tells you where to look; the leviathan has the last page. Still to come with the second verb: how each fish was taken, net or line, the biggest yet, and a page per legendary with the bridge or reef it came from, so line-caught fish get their own shelf and the snook's page is worth the wait.
- **A fleet.** Everyone agrees: not until several islands in. Stays on Further.
- **Fishing rivals.** The husband's; the owner is not sold, and neither am I yet. Rivals thin your schools and there is no cosy counter to another boat except conflict. The alive-world feeling they promise comes cheaper from vendors, charters and the coast guard. Parked; revisit once island 2 exists.

The kid's list (2026-09-28), after he sailed the flagship to the buoys and ran out of sea: more ocean, more leviathans, leviathans that attack, enemies, a new island, and getting off the boat. Asked, he wants to dodge the enemies for now rather than fight them, and off the boat he wants to explore the island and to scuba dive. Most of it is one arc, the way out to island 2, which was already the plan ("Leviathan as a gate"). The order proposed, drawn for the kids as a sea chart; the owner started the first and can reorder the rest:

1. **Past the buoys.** The deep opens for the flagship, and the leviathan guards the crossing: it surfaces across the path of a boat going out, spines first, and the counter is to turn away. A hit costs fish, never the boat or coins. Built 2026-09-28: the deep, then the leviathan's guard. It rises when the boat crosses, not when it points at the buoys, because the lanternfish and goldfin school within 300 of them. It lies square across the boat's way rather than along the buoys, so a shallow crossing cannot slip past it. The tests dodge it with a person a third and half a second late, turning home or a quarter turn off their way; in a probe no one was touched at eight tenths of a second late.
2. **More leviathans**, one per stretch of the deep, each attacking a different way that tests the steering: the serpent crosses your path, a kraken grabs the net from below and a zigzag shakes it off, an anglerfish at night whose lure looks like a glowing school. The Cthuluviathan is the one he named (built 2026-10-06), and the anglerfish followed the same day (both under Creatures). One more is still his to draw: the owner wants that slot kept open. The Cthuluviathan's tentacles from below cover most of what the kraken would have been, so the kraken wants rethinking before it is built.
3. **Step off the boat.** Walk the home island from the dock: the pier, the dog, the palace, the bridge, the vendors. Walking started 2026-10-06 (the Ashore entry above): tie up and walk the island, the pier, the bridge and the beach, with nothing to do there yet. Later a message in a bottle and a dig for what its map shows (from the Further list).
4. **Island 2**, past the leviathans: sail there, dock and sell first, then its fish, its storms and seining. Built as a place 2026-10-07 (the World entry above): sail there, sell, walk it, its fish.
5. **Scuba**, island 3's verb as before, with a counter for the sharks before it ships. The diver-down flag (red, white diagonal) marks a dive.

The kid's second list (2026-10-07), after an evening ashore: island 2 now; a leviathan that can eat the boat, with a boat health bar out by the Cthuluviathan; the anglerfish swallowing you whole and you coming back in safe shallows; a camp of enemies in little skull masks (evil monkeys) fought with fishing spears that upgrade, so spearfishing; enough spear to beat a leviathan; a tower on each later island climbed as a mini dungeon, real time and never turn-based, with a boss at the top (on the first, a leviathan sorcerer: a naga-like chimera with a bird's head and wings, fins and a tail), great rewards on every other one, your own flag planted at the top once it is taken and a way straight back to the boat; flags you design for the boat; and more and more leviathans, from other water dimensions too. The proposal back: the dog first, then island 2 as a place, flags, boat health and being swallowed (one system: health exists only past the buoys, the home water stays cosy, and being eaten costs the hold and spits you out in the home shallows, never coins, upgrades or the boat), the spear (a fishing tool first, in island 2's shallows; then thrown at the monkeys on foot, tap to throw and it aims itself; a harpoon from the boat at the top level), the monkey camp (beaten monkeys drop their masks and run), towers climbed up the outside on stairs past landings so the camera we have serves, and the harpoon against leviathans, which drives them off for a trophy rather than killing them. The sorcerer is his to draw, like the monster slot. Two calls are the owner's: whether health and fighting are the way she wants the game to go, and whether a beaten leviathan is driven off or killed. She approved the order the same night and asked for it overnight, so he could play it in the morning; all eight went in as their own PRs (#59 to #66). Driven off is what the harpoon does until she says otherwise; it is one place to change (each leviathan's harpoon method).

Later the same night, three more, noted and not yet built: he hopes the game can look more realistic one day, with beautiful plants; he wants turtles; and, later in the game, being shipwrecked and strapping a few sea turtles together to get back to an island you control. The take on them: realistic should mean richer rather than photographic (light on the water, plants that sway, more on the islands), since the flat cosy look is what the custom renderer does well and a photographic turn would fight it. Turtles fit first as a gentle creature, like the whales and mantas, in island 2's shallows or the home rings. The shipwreck fits late, as another way a trip can end besides being spat out at home: once there are islands you control (a taken tower makes its island yours), you wash up somewhere, gather turtles, and ride the raft back to one of them.

The kid's third list (2026-10-08), after school, and the owner's design round it. His: island 3's boss is human, with a cape and a sword; it slashes, and it can block once. Beaten, it heals you and pulls you into a demon dimension, where it becomes three little demons: a red one with the boss's attacks, a blue one that summons two monkeys, and a green one with both. Beat them and the key it dropped opens a cage, and out comes a companion: a warlock, part human and part bird with a bird's head, three lives like you, who follows you, helps you fish from his own boat and helps you fight. Then a cutscene: an evil monkey summons a tar monster into the sea round another island and turns its water black, a preview of island 4, where you will need a chemistry suit. And island 3 stays the scuba island. The owner's design makes that all one place: island 3 sank, so only a floating shanty town is left up top and the tower stands out of the water, with seaweed gathered at its foot to stand on and get in by. At the top waits what you think is the boss, the same one as the first tower (read as island 2's sorcerer; she wrote "island 1"); beating him gives you scuba gear, and with it you dive down to the tower's base under the water, where the real boss is. The take back, put to the owner the same day and not yet answered: the sorcerer's return is the fake-out and should have one new trick so it is not a straight repeat; every stage stays beaten once it is (die to the demons and the way in is still open next dive), since the chain is long for one go on a phone; scuba comes in two steps, the dive down the tower to the swordsman first with no air to run out of mid-fight, and swimming the drowned town later; the three demons come out together but little, a couple of hits each, each summoner's two monkeys not replaced until they are beaten, tuned with the play simulations; the warlock comes on foot first and his boat after. The order: island 3 as a place, the tower and the sorcerer's return for the scuba gear, the dive with the swordsman, the demon dimension and the cage (as one piece: it is one fight in two phases), the warlock on foot, his boat, then the cutscene and island 4 on the map: all built the same evening, for him to play after his taekwondo graduation. Next on his list: the chemistry suit and island 4 itself, the other-dimension leviathan (the demon dimension is a home waiting for it), and the shipwreck raft of turtles.

The kid's fourth list (2026-10-08), typed up by him, with his answers to the owner's questions the same evening. Part one: a guide to the chemistry suit and islands 5 to 7. The suit lets you walk and swim in the tar; island 4's boss is a tar anchorer, and beating it gives you a tarling companion. Island 5 is like Jaws on the NES, with a giant skeleton shark king with a trident and a dramatic death, in which he claims to know of a portal but dies before he can say. Island 6 is big ("50 boats wide") with a new leviathan: slow, one tentacle that grabs and two that shoot driftwood; beaten, instead of dying it drags you underwater, where it looks like Cthulhu (10 hp) with fast tentacles of 1 hp; it drops a rare fish worth 1,000 coins and two companions with 5 hp each, a healer cat (heals you twice a fight, more if you feed it fish; 1 damage close up; you can pet it) and a Cthulhu warrior (2 damage). Part two: island 7, the same monkeys, leviathan and sorcerer as island 2, but with an alien portal. Part three: the portal takes you to Gigantis, a castle above the water with skeletons, ghosts, zombies and necromancers; in the throne room wait three necromancers and the Forgotten One. Each necromancer summons a skeleton; the Forgotten One shoots skulls and death rays and has a sword (1 damage each); ranged skeletons do 1 and cannot block; melee skeletons do 1; the warlock summons merlocks to help. When the Forgotten One dies he drops a skeleton (2 damage, 4 hp) that summons a ranged ghost and a melee skeleton, and a room behind the throne is left for next time. The take back to the owner: 50 boats wide is about 3,700 units, three quarters of the sea inside the buoys, so 15 to 20 boats (three times island 3) unless he wants that long a walk, which is still hers to call; all four corners of the deep are taken, so islands 5 to 7 need the map to grow, a further ring past a second line of buoys, as its own piece first; a hit of 2 is two of the three hearts; and three helpers make fights easy and busy on a phone, to be tuned with the play simulations. The order: island 4 and the suit, the far deep and island 5, island 6 with the leviathan and the cat and the warrior, island 7 and the portal, then Gigantis. Built so far: the suit and the tar, the Tar Anchorer and the tarling, the far deep, island 5 as a place, the Skeleton Shark King, island 6 as a place, the Deep One and Cthulhu, then the healer cat and the Cthulhu warrior, then island 7 as a place, then its tower, the sorcerer a third time and the portal waking, then Gigantis's courtyard and halls, then its throne room and the Forgotten One. All three parts of the fourth list are built.

The kid's fifth list (2026-10-10), added to the bottom of his document, with the owner's answers the same evening. A bigger ocean, three times the size, past a third line of buoys ("the far far buoys"); a bigger boat you can get out of the driver's seat of and walk round while at sea; ice fishing like the game he plays, the line going down, down, down and hooking things on the way up; maze puzzles; a radar showing fish blue, threats red and the rest grey, in the deep too; islands 10 to 13 with mad gorillas (7 hp, 3 damage) and bosses in old skull masks (19 hp, 3 damage); a base on every island (his "polm", home); a dungeon on every island, towers of five rooms with a swordsman general on the third, homes, and a dive base where scientists do experiments, not hostile. And a new leviathan, the Reaper, with his drawing: semi-aquatic, two of him, one on land and one in the water, roaming the deep and the far deep; beat him for the ice fishing gear and an egg that hatches into a pet. The owner's calls: the ice fishing is on an ice floe in the far deep; the one in the water gives the gear and the one on land, on island 6, the egg; the young one swims beside the boat and walks ashore. The take back: 3 damage is every heart a figure without armour has; a sea three times bigger is empty sailing on a phone unless islands 10 to 13 come with it; walking the deck under way touches the boat and the net, the game's feel, so it goes carefully; and "Reaper Leviathan" is another game's, so he is just the Reaper. The order: the Reaper at sea, the ice floe and ice fishing, the Reaper on land and the egg, the young one; the radar fits anywhere. Then the owner, on the ice fishing (2026-10-11): an igloo to build, a seal pet named Neil, and an all-white scuba suit to keep warm. Her calls: the igloo a base on the floe, built like the huts, a home port, where sleeping skips to the next day so the hole refills; Neil a seal on the floe fed ice fish like the otter, who swims by the boat, flops on deck, comes ice fishing and sits by the hole and sometimes catches one; the white suit for a third dive, under the floe, in water too cold without it, with ice caves, things frozen in the ice and seals swimming. The ice first, then the Reaper on land and the egg. Built so far: the Reaper at sea, the ice floe and ice fishing, the igloo, Neil, then the dive under the ice and the white scuba suit, then the Reaper on land and his egg (2026-10-12), then the baby Reaper out of it the same day. The radar, his, on 2026-10-12: a scope in the corner from the shipwright, the blips put there by each creature for itself (`Entity.radar`) while it is out on the water, so a leviathan still under the sea is not on it and the radar spoils nothing that rises. The Reaper's arc is done.

The kid's island 10 list (2026-10-13), with the owner's answers the same day. Island 10: a forest, a prairie, a swamp and a pond, its animals all back each time you leave and return. The swamp has alligators (hostile, 10 hp) and swamp things: everything but mosquitoes, so frogs, snakes, a swamp monster, swamp panthers, tiny bears and tiny deer, and a spider that can be killed only with bug spray from the prairie's vendor; without it he traps you in his web. The forest has the gorillas, which hit hard (his earlier 7 hp and 3 damage; the owner's fine with 3 if each slam is wound up so it can be dodged). The prairie has cool frolicking animals and a store, kept by a little old man with a beard and a cowboy hat in a rocking chair who tells dad jokes: snacks (an apple, a speed boost, and carrots, an attack boost, each for a while; a banana that heals), a radar upgrade that shows treasure, a treasure submarine (the owner's yes: it dives anywhere in the far deep into the side-on underwater view, with its own fish and treasure), and badges that power companions up with magic, each badge a different power and movable from one companion to another: armour (an energy shield), +3 health, upgradable to +6; damage; healing; and invisibility, for stealth missions to come, never perfect, and only good for sneaking past enemies up to three levels above you. The pond: peaceful fishing with birdsong. The cat heals companions once fed tuna (built). Island 6 gets lots of birds (built). The tower's boss: a giant pelican (15 hp, 3 damage); beaten, it leaves a little bread, which in a birdfeeder brings crows; each asks a science problem, at a third grader's level, and solved, you can pet it, and the crows become pets, needed for island 11. The owner's calls: island 10 goes in the far deep's empty north-east corner now (built), and the crows open the far far sea beyond for island 11 (let one go and it flies toward land, as sailors once let ravens go). The order: the place first, then the prairie's animals and the pond's fishing, the old man and his snacks, the swamp's creatures (built), the spider and the bug spray (built), the gorillas (built), the tower and the pelican (built), the birdfeeder and the crows (built), the radar upgrade (built), the badges (built but invisibility, which waits on what a level is), and the submarine (built).

The kid's sixth list (2026-10-15), Part 6 of his document with four drawings, and his answers to the questions sent back after the island 10 list. His answers: the snacks keep their prices; invisibility comes with stealth missions that a scientist on the bridge hands out (the "levels" in the badge's description did not mean anything to him, so they are dropped); island 11 is Skull Island, whose boss yells at his men and keeps displacer beasts (4 hp, 3 damage). Part 6: (1) the Heron's escape, above, built. His first drawing is Skrill (his answer, below): horned and winged, crowing "The spell worked!", with the same orb as island 9, a tower like island 10's, the monkeys again, and a portal. (2) The farthest far buoys, "10,000 deep, 30 wide", where his fourth drawing has the sea and a tar ocean split by a tall metal bridge with the orb on top, and on the tar side an island with a tower on a hill; tar creatures: the old tar monster, tar humans, tar beasts (3 hp, 2 damage), red tar tentacles, a winged many-legged tar dragon (10 hp, 1 damage) and a tar leader with a sword (25 hp, 2 damage), some marked 20 damage. (3) Skull Island: men in skeleton armour (18 hp, 2 damage), the skeleton guy in his second drawing with a bone weapon; its boss, Skrill, is a picture still to come. (4) Choose your character: a pick screen with three named people and Custom (colours, a cap, a fishing hat, hair, faces). (5) Honor Guard armour, 9 hp, gold with a red plume and a cape, and a survival knife (4 damage). (6) The Battle Barge: free; pick the arena's size and summon up to ten of anything, any enemy or any boss from any island. The take back: 20 damage kills from full even in space armour, so 3 to 5 with a wind-up to dodge, like the gorillas; the Battle Barge starts with the on-foot enemies, ten at once in a small, medium or large arena, and adds bosses a few at a time, since most are built into their own arenas. The escape is built (above). The questions went to the kid the same evening, in a tab of his document, and he typed his answers there. Honor Guard: both, the shipwright's armour (9 hearts) and a look to pick on the character screen, the pick only a look. The survival knife: both, stabbed up close and thrown. Choose your character: name the three presets ourselves (no real names), and they are only looks. The skeleton guy is a bad guy, one of Skull Island's men; their bone weapons are jawblades, and beaten, one goes to "the glass bridge" (asked). The farthest far buoys: a dive that goes 1,000 metres down, small on top ("30 inches wide" in the boat); the farthest far buoys are not tar. The metal bridge is walked across and sailed under; its orb is island 9's, and putting your hand in it shows you knowledge, of the boats and the past and more. The tar island's boss is the tar leader, up on the high ground ("higher area", not "higher sword"); the tar creatures do 3 to 5 damage and have 15 to 20 hits (the 20s were hits, not damage); the tar beasts are wise, and will not fight if they know they will lose, unless cornered. The scientist's stealth missions win knowledge, and enough of them a chemistry suit, a weapon and a boat, all for the tar. Skull Island is island 12; its boss is Skrill (20 hits, 3 damage, "hehe ha ha mwa ha ha"), the horned, winged one in his first drawing. Island 11 is as big as island 6, with a portal to the Old One's place: a key lies in the lagoon, and diving for it you see ten of the lagoon creatures, and your Reapers and anything else that can go under water go with you; the skull key opens the door behind, in the Old One's room, where skeletons (5), zombies (6) and ghosts (4) wait with the Forgotten One again, laughing, who fooled you. The Battle Barge: open anywhere, on the islands and out at sea; it pays 1 coin and is mostly for getting better and for fun; and not island fighters first: you spawn in and pick any enemies and bosses and how many. The swan paddle boat: yes; it (or a catamaran, read either way) unlocks on island 11 by fighting a heron, not the Heron. Asked back the same evening in a second tab, and answered the next day (2026-10-16): the glass bridge is at the farthest far buoys, the new area past all the islands, and it keeps the tar from spreading in (read as the same bridge as the metal one with the orb on top, which his drawing colours blue; asked). The stealth missions sneak past skeleton troops and demons, and they one-shot you. Skrill is the horned, winged one in the first drawing, and the "+5" is five monkeys. "The spell worked!" is both his and the Heron's line, and yes, the Heron comes back. Island 11's heron is the Heron's brother: the same, but with two hands, never beaten, and he says "Have a good day! Oh, by the way, have you seen my brother?". The swan paddle boat is ridden by Caterman ("caterman" was not a catamaran), a technology cat-human on island 11 (3 hits, 2 damage), who comes with you after the fight with the brother. The Forgotten One behind the door: 9 hits, 4 damage, alone; beaten, he lives, shows you the castle, and bursts into bones to save your life, and there is a portable scanning machine ("scares eyes and stuff, more snooks"; asked). And 1,000 metres down at the farthest far buoys: the leviathans, Reapers, the Cthuluviathan, Cthulhu, gulpers and every fish there is, the snook too, and nine new fish worth 91 coins. Asked back in a third tab: what the scanner does, whether the glass bridge and the metal bridge are one, the nine fish (91 each?), and how the fight with the Heron's brother ends. The owner's calls the same night: island 11 goes past island 10, out in the far far sea (built, above), and the Battle Barge comes in stages. His answers the next morning: the scanner, "scanned well things upgrade to scaner and eggs are blue for getting scanned", read as: scanning things makes the scanner better, and eggs glow blue when they can be scanned (asked); the glass bridge is the metal bridge with the orb; the deep fish are glow squid, gloomers, clown fish, nibblers, stoplight loosejaws, viper fish, owl fish, chimaeras, coelacanths and frilled sharks, ten names for his nine (asked, with whether each is worth 91, and what gloomers and nibblers look like); and the fight with the Heron's brother ends with the brother stopping and saying "Bye, it's hard for challengers! Have a good day! Oh, by the way, where is my brother?", and then Caterman comes with you. Asked back in a fourth tab: those three. His answers (2026-10-16): yes to the scanner, and scanning a creature "tells you how" (asked: how to beat it?); all ten deep fish go in at 91 coins each, and "Ramona" too, as big as Star and worth 190 coins (asked what Ramona is); gloomers are flowermouths with naga bodies, and nibblers he will draw; the crypt's 5, 6 and 4 are how many (as built); and the Forgotten One shows you the castle both ways, walking with you and as a cutscene, it becomes your headquarters, and he saves you from the Old One, exploding him into black lagoon water (built, above). Asked back in a fifth tab, still open: what Ramona is, what you can do at your headquarters, and whether the scanner tells you how to beat a creature. The deep fish wait for the farthest far buoys' 1,000-metre dive. Four of the owner's open calls went to him, and the owner passed on his answers the same day (2026-10-16): the sea bosses get a Battle Barge of their own at sea, "you customize your map of islands and stuff" (asked: what goes on the map besides islands, whether the islands do anything in the fight, which bosses, and whether you fight from your own boat); the farthest far buoys "are, that's the white": the white buoys already in the game (read: no fourth line, and the 1,000-metre dive, the glass bridge and the tar ocean go out past the white buoys, in the far far sea with island 11; asked); yes to the weekly reminder to save a backup game link; and the snacks stay bought. Asked in a sixth tab, with the three in the fifth still open. The owner's own call, the same day: back saves up online, under a save code, for anyone who finds the game (Online saves, above), with the weekly reminder telling the code. His answers to the fifth and sixth tabs, the same day: Ramona is the wizard books' Ramora, a silver fish as big as Star and worth 190, renamed, as the repo is public, and named by the kid the Remoro (it waits for the 1,000-metre dive); the headquarters has everything asked about (your flag, your friends living there, sleeping there, keeping things there) with upgrades, and observatories where every leviathan can be spotted; the scanner tells how to beat a creature and its weakness (built, above). The sea battle is the Battle Barge's own, at sea: you make its map, islands, tar and the bridge (a map with tar has a bridge, and tar comes once the bridge is reached), all of it yours to lay out; the islands block the bosses and hide you, and you can go ashore on them, but Stars and Reapers are on them; all the sea bosses; fought from your own boat with the harpoon. And the 1,000-metre dive, the glass bridge and the tar ocean are out past the white buoys, where island 11 is. Asked in a seventh tab: where honey comes from and what the Reaper does about it, what bigger stuff Star fears, what a better scanner does, and whether the observatory is looked through or puts the leviathans on the map. The order put to her: the escape, Honor Guard and the knife, choose your character, the farthest far buoys (the bridge, the tar ocean, the tar island where the Heron returns, the scientist's stealth missions and invisibility), Skull Island, the Battle Barge.

The arc this suggests: island 1 finishes with the creatures, the snook at the bridge, vendors gating the last palace stages, and pets; island 2 brings seining, storms, sirens and mermaids, the coast guard and charters; island 3 goes under the water.

Further:
- **Island 2 and the meta layer.** Reaching a new island unlocks a new mechanic and a renderer upgrade (water shader, shadows, weather, particles). Each island is a chapter in the game growing up.
- Crew and a fleet of auto-fishing boats. Weather and storms (rough water, double prices). Ice and freshness (value decays in the hold). Sonar upgrade. Dock buildings that multiply value: smokehouse, cannery, sushi bar. Pelican thief. Message bottles with map fragments leading to a dig site. More dawn/dusk rares per zone, seasonal rares.

## The kid's lore

The kid's world, as he tells it, collected here as it comes. The game says it where it fits, mostly in the guide.

- The Cthuluviathan is Cthulhu's pet (2026-10-09, the start of the lore). The guide's Cthuluviathan page opens with it, and the Deep One's page, once Cthulhu has been met under island 6, names its pet and where it sleeps.

## Look and voice

- Palette anchors: sea `#2B8A99` / `#1B6676`, foam `#F3FFFB`, sand `#F2D79B`, buoy red `#E4572E`, cream `#FFF6E5`, coin `#FFC53D`, ink `#12303A`. Flat shaded, no outlines, light from the left (+y faces lighter than +x faces).
- Type: Grandstander 500/700/800, with a rounded system fallback. HUD chrome is pills; the shop is a plank sign.
- Copy: sentence case, plain verbs, a button says what it does ("Buy for 20", "Build the treehouse"), the confirmation uses the same words ("Built the treehouse"). Errors say what to do next ("Collect 5 more driftwood out at sea."). No exclamation marks except a rare catch.

## Things learned the hard way

- A canvas with `position:fixed; inset:0` does not stretch. Set `width:100%; height:100%` or the backing store draws at the wrong scale.
- Additive glow over sprites at night washes them out and makes them look darker. Use a darkness mask with holes.
- Panning the camera the moment the boat crosses the dock ring feels like being yanked. Wait until the boat has slowed, zoom out, drift gently.
- Anything that roams needs to know the island exists.
- In night scenes, anything the player must find (driftwood, crates, rares) needs its own small light.
- Test at phone size first. The isometric squash halves vertical distances, so the world feels smaller on screen than the numbers suggest.
- Vite's default ports are shared by every Vite project on the machine, and Playwright's `reuseExistingServer` will happily test whichever app answers first. Pin ports and never reuse.
- Vite blocks any Host header that is not localhost or an IP address. A dev server reached by hostname needs `server.allowedHosts`.
- A network that advertises IPv6 without routing it hangs Node downloads, including Playwright's browser installer, while curl falls back to IPv4 in milliseconds. If curl works and Node does not, suspect IPv6 first.
- Git Bash's tar cannot read zip files. `C:\Windows\System32\tar.exe` can.
- A force-push does not start a GitHub Pages branch build, and with the Pages source set to GitHub Actions nothing deploys until a workflow exists on `main`.
- Phone browsers discard a backgrounded tab. Anything not saved is a lost trip.
- A panel that covers the thumb's home must let steering through. Pointer capture makes it one binding: a press on the panel's background anchors the stick and hands the pointer to the canvas.
- iOS ignores `user-scalable=no`, so a double-tap on any button zooms the page, and once zoomed the canvas's `touch-action: none` swallows the pinch that would zoom back out. `touch-action: manipulation` on the body kills double-tap zoom everywhere and leaves pinch alone. Found by the chief playtester buying an upgrade twice, fast.
- Coins come from the sweep and wood from errands, so a base gated only on wood always trails the boat. Fix supply before price, and give the late stages something to gate.
- Price nothing off a single big docking. Take two screenshots ten minutes apart of the owner actually playing and divide; that number was a tenth of the burst.
- A timing test played by a hand that reacts on the same frame proves nothing about people. Give the test hand a person's reaction, a third of a second, and the game's own input smoothing. The snook's fight passed a perfect hand and parted every line a person played.
- Then give it a person's input too. A thumb on glass is down or up, and a short drag is how people hold. The snook's second fix passed a test hand that dragged three quarters of the ring and eased to a fifth through every run; the owner, holding the way people hold, lost every fish to the piling.
- Trigger a threat on what the player did, not where they are pointing. The leviathan's guard first rose for any flagship heading at the buoys, and the lanternfish school 280 in from them: it would have woken on every sweep of the richest fish in the game.
- Players reach for what the world shows them. The day they could walk past the dog, both players went looking for a way to pet it. Make the obvious touch work, and show how until it is found.
- On the far side of an isometric island a figure stands in front of the water behind it, so one walked to the waterline looks as if it is wading. Keep feet a shadow's width inside the sand.
- Eight-way keys on an isometric map point at a diagonal the boat is rarely facing, so the throttle penalty for turning is on almost all the time and the keyboard feels slower and twitchier than the stick. Put feels side by side behind a switch and let the players pick. Two players picked two different ones, and a setting was cheaper than a winner.
- A grey screen on a phone is the page's process killed, not a script error, which leaves the last frame frozen on the screen. Look for what grows over a long session.
- Safari keeps any Web Audio node that is still connected, and runs it on every block of sound, for good. Every note must disconnect what it made when it ends (`onended`). Before that fix (2026-10-12, #157) ten minutes of gulls, dolphins and whales left 2,727 nodes running, the likely cause of the owner's grey screen during a Star encounter. `tests/unit/audionodes.test.ts` plays the cues and the sea through a fake engine and checks every note unplugs.
- Playwright cannot route a fetch that a service worker handles. A test that serves the page something else runs with `serviceWorkers: 'block'`, and what the worker does is tested on its own (offline, in the installable test).
- A global `canvas{position:fixed;inset:0}` pins every canvas to the top left. A second canvas, as the radar is, needs `top:auto;left:auto`.
- A new game starts just off the dock ring, not in it. A test that means "docked" puts the boat on `DOCK`.
